), and all maps involved in the groupoid structure are polynomial. For a smooth
2
-cocycle
\omega
on a polynomial Lie groupoid the Schwartz space
\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G},\omega):=\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G})
forms a
*
-algebra with the identically defined operations as that of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{G},\omega)
. There is in this case an inclusion
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{G},\omega)\subseteq\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G},\omega)
which defines a
*
-homomorphism with dense range.

Restriction along the unit defines a mapping
E_{M}:C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{G},\omega)\to C^{\infty}_{c}(M)
. For
f_{1},f_{2}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{G},\omega)
we define
\langle f_{1},f_{2}\rangle_{C_{0}(M)}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(M)
by

\langle f_{1},f_{2}\rangle_{C_{0}(M)}:=E_{M}(f_{1}^{*}\ast f_{2}).

Let
\mathpzc{E}_{\mathcal{G},\omega}
denote the completion of
C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{G},\omega)
as a
C_{0}(M)
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-module. Up to natural isomorphism of
C_{0}(M)
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules,
\mathpzc{E}_{\mathcal{G},\omega}
is independent of
\omega
. Left multiplication extends to a faithful
*
-representation

\lambda^{\omega}:C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{G},\omega)\to\mathrm{End}_{C_{0}(M)}^{*}(\mathpzc{E}_{\mathcal{G},\omega}).

We call
\lambda
the left regular representation of
\mathcal{G}
.

Definition 0.10.14
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\rightrightarrows M
be a Lie groupoid and
\omega
a smooth
2
-cocycle on
\mathcal{G}
. The reduced
C^{*}
-algebra
C_{r}^{*}(\mathcal{G},\omega)
is the completion of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{G},\omega)
with respect to the norm

\|f\|_{C_{r}^{*}(\mathcal{G},\omega)}:=\|\lambda^{\omega}(f)\|_{\mathrm{End}_{C_{0}(M)}^{*}(\mathpzc{E}_{\mathcal{G},\omega})}.

The full
C^{*}
-algebra
C^{*}(\mathcal{G},\omega)
is a completion of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{G},\omega)
with respect to all
*
-representations by bounded operators on a Hilbert space (for more details see
[
133
]
).

If
\omega\equiv 1
, we write
C_{r}^{*}(\mathcal{G})
and
C^{*}(\mathcal{G})
instead of
C_{r}^{*}(\mathcal{G},1)
and
C^{*}(\mathcal{G},1)
, respectively.

Remark 0.10.15
.

If
\mathcal{G}\rightrightarrows M
is a polynomial Lie groupoid with a smooth
2
-cocycle
\omega
, the inclusion
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{G},\omega)\subseteq\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G},\omega)
extends to a continuous dense inclusion
\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G},\omega)\subseteq C^{*}_{r}(\mathcal{G},\omega)
. Similarly, there is a continuous dense inclusion
\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G},\omega)\subseteq C^{*}(\mathcal{G},\omega)
.

Example 0.10.16
.

Consider the transformation groupoid
\mathcal{G}=M\rtimes G
associated with a Lie group acting smoothly on a manifold. A choice of Haar system corresponds to a choice of Haar measure on
G
. The convolution product on
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{G})
is given by

f_{1}\ast f_{2}(x,g)=\int_{G}f_{1}(h,x)f_{2}(h^{-1}g,xh)\mathrm{d}h,

where
\mathrm{d}h
is the Haar measure on
G
. The involution is given by

f^{*}(g,x)=\overline{f(g^{-1},xg)}.

Therefore
C_{r}^{*}(\mathcal{G})=C_{0}(M)\rtimes_{r}G
and
C^{*}(\mathcal{G})=C_{0}(M)\rtimes G
.

Example 0.10.17
.

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and consider the pair groupoid
\mathcal{G}=M\times M
. A Haar system on
\mathcal{G}
corresponds to a choice of a positive Radon measure on
M
. We suppress the choice of this measure from the notations. The convolution on
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{G})
is given by

f_{1}\ast f_{2}(x,y)=\int_{M}f_{1}(x,z)f_{2}(z,y)\mathrm{d}z.

The involution is given by

f^{*}(x,y)=\overline{f(y,x)}.

The reduced
C^{*}
-algebra of
\mathcal{G}
is isomorphic to the
C^{*}
-algebra of compact operators
\mathbb{K}(L^{2}(M))
. In fact, this isomorphism is constructed by localizing the left regular representation in a point
x\in M
and the canonical left
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant diffeomorphism
M\cong\mathcal{G}_{x}
.

Theorem 0.10.18
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\rightrightarrows M
be a Lie groupoid with a smooth
2
-cocycle
\omega
. For any subset
X\subseteq M
we define
\mathcal{G}_{X}:=s^{-1}(X)\cap r^{-1}(X)
which is a topological groupoid (but not a Lie groupoid in general). If
X\subseteq M
is open and
\mathcal{G}
-invariant,
\mathcal{G}_{X}
is an open Lie subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
and
C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{X},\omega)
is an ideal in
C^{*}(\mathcal{G},\omega)
. If
X
is a closed
\mathcal{G}
-invariant submanifold,
\mathcal{G}_{X}
is a closed Lie subgroupoid for which restriction defines an epimorphism
C^{*}(\mathcal{G},\omega)\to C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{X},\omega)
that fits into an exact sequence of full groupoid
C^{*}
-algebras:

0\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{M\setminus X},\omega)\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathcal{G},\omega)\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{X},\omega)\rightarrow 0.

We refer the proof of this theorem to
[
133
]
. The reader should beware that in general, the exactness property in Theorem
0.10.18
does not hold for the reduced completion. The following corollary is an important consequence that we will use abundantly.

Corollary 0.10.19
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\rightrightarrows M
be a Lie groupoid. Assume that
\omega
is a smooth
2
-cocycle on the adiabatic groupoid
\mathcal{G}_{ad}
. Let
\mathcal{G}_{\rm ad}^{\circ}
denote the restriction of
\mathcal{G}_{\rm ad}
to
M\times[0,1)
. Then one has the following exact sequence:

0\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathcal{G}\times(0,1),\omega)\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\rm ad}^{\circ},\omega)\xrightarrow{{\rm ev}_{0}}C^{*}(\mathsf{Lie}(\mathcal{G}),\omega)\rightarrow 0.

If
\omega=1
, the short exact sequence reduces to

0\rightarrow C_{0}(0,1)\otimes C^{*}(\mathcal{G})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\rm ad}^{\circ})\xrightarrow{{\rm ev}_{0}}C_{0}(\mathsf{Lie}(\mathcal{G})^{*})\rightarrow 0,

where
\mathsf{Lie}(\mathcal{G})^{*}\to M
denotes the dual vector bundle of the Lie algebroid.

0.11.
A groupoid description of the flat orbits

The ideal of flat orbits of a simply connected nilpotent Lie group can be described as a twisted groupoid
C^{*}
-algebra. We shall see that this relates the flat orbits to a noncommutative Fourier transform of functions on the total space of the vector bundle
\Xi\to\Gamma
.

We start with some preliminary considerations. Consider a Lie group
\mathsf{G}
with a closed central subgroup
\mathsf{Z}\subseteq\mathsf{G}
. For notational convenience, we use multiplicative notation on
\mathsf{G}
and
\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}
but additive notation on
\mathsf{Z}
. Assume that
\tau:\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\to\mathsf{G}
is a smooth splitting of the quotient mapping
\mathsf{G}\to\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}
. We define the smooth
2
-cocycle

\omega:\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\rightarrow\mathsf{Z},\ \omega(g_{1}\mathsf{Z},g_{2}\mathsf{Z}):=\tau(g_{1}\mathsf{Z})\tau(g_{2}\mathsf{Z})\tau(g_{1}g_{2}\mathsf{Z})^{-1}.

Proposition 0.11.1
.

The splitting
\tau
induces an isomorphism
\mathsf{G}\simeq\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\rtimes_{\omega}\mathsf{Z}
, where the cocycle twisted product
\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\rtimes_{\omega}\mathsf{Z}
is the group defined as the set
\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathsf{Z}
equipped with the group operation

(g_{1}\mathsf{Z},z_{1})\cdot_{\omega}(g_{2}\mathsf{Z},z_{2}):=(g_{1}g_{2}\mathsf{Z},z_{1}+z_{2}+\omega(g_{1}\mathsf{Z},g_{2}\mathsf{Z})).

We are interested in the situation that
\mathsf{G}
is a simply connected nilpotent Lie group and
\mathsf{Z}
is its center. We can define the
2
-cocycle

\omega_{Z}:\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\to\mathsf{Z},

(23)

by choosing a Jordan-Hölder basis that splits
\mathsf{G}\to\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}
. The reader should note that this
2
-cocycle pairs with
\mathfrak{z}^{*}
to the Kirillov form on the flat orbits, cf. Remark
0.6.7
. By the discussion above we have that
\mathsf{G}\cong\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times_{\omega_{Z}}\mathsf{Z}
. Let us translate this context into a twisted groupoid. The crucial observation is that
\mathsf{Z}=\mathfrak{z}
whose
C^{*}
-algebra is isomorphic to
C_{0}(\mathfrak{z}^{*})
via the Fourier transform.

Definition 0.11.2
.

Let
\mathsf{G}
be a simply connected nilpotent Lie group with center
\mathsf{Z}=\mathfrak{z}
. Define the groupoid
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}\rightrightarrows\mathfrak{z}^{*}
as the transformation groupoid (see Example
0.10.6
) of the trivial
\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}
-action on
\mathfrak{z}^{*}
.

We write
\mathcal{G}_{\Gamma}
for the restriction of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}
to the open subset of flat orbits
\Gamma\subseteq\mathfrak{z}^{*}
.

We note that
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}^{(2)}=\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*}
and the composition is the product of the two
\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}
-factors.

Definition 0.11.3
.

Define the unitary 2-cocycle
\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}
on
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}
by

\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}:\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}^{(2)}=\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*}\rightarrow U(1),\ \omega_{\mathcal{G}_{Z}}(g\mathsf{Z},h\mathsf{Z},\xi)=e^{-i\xi(\omega_{\mathsf{Z}}(g\mathsf{Z},h\mathsf{Z}))}.

The group
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
acts as smooth groupoid automorphisms on
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}
by

\varphi(g\mathsf{Z},\xi):=(\varphi(g)\mathsf{Z},(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\xi).

We are here implicitly using that a groupoid automorphism preserves the center. Let us describe a lift of this action to an action on
C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
.

Proposition 0.11.4
.

Let
\mathsf{G}
be a simply connected nilpotent Lie group with center
\mathsf{Z}
and fix a smooth splitting
\tau:\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\to\mathsf{G}
. For
\varphi\in\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
, define
\tau_{\varphi}:=\varphi^{-1}\circ\tau\circ\varphi
and

b_{\varphi}:=\tau_{\varphi}\cdot\tau^{-1}:\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\to\mathsf{Z}.

It holds that the group
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
acts strongly continuously on
C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
via the action

\varphi.f(g\mathsf{Z},\xi)=e^{-i\xi b_{\varphi^{-1}}(g\mathsf{Z})}(\varphi^{-1})^{*}(f)(g\mathsf{Z},\xi).

Proof.

Writing
\varphi^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}:=\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}\circ(\varphi\times\varphi)
on
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}^{(2)}
, we compute that

\displaystyle\varphi^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}(g\mathsf{Z},h\mathsf{Z},\xi)=

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}(\varphi(g)\mathsf{Z},\varphi(h)\mathsf{Z},(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\xi)=e^{-i\xi(\varphi^{-1}(\omega_{\mathsf{Z}}(\varphi(g)\mathsf{Z},\varphi(h)\mathsf{Z}))}.

If
\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}(g\mathsf{Z},h\mathsf{Z},\xi)=\tau(g\mathsf{Z})\tau(h\mathsf{Z})\tau(gh\mathsf{Z})^{-1}
for the smooth splitting
\tau
, then

\varphi^{-1}(\omega_{\mathsf{Z}}(\varphi(g)\mathsf{Z},\varphi(h)\mathsf{Z}))=\tau_{\varphi}(g\mathsf{Z})\tau_{\varphi}(h\mathsf{Z})\tau_{\varphi}(gh\mathsf{Z})^{-1},

for the splitting
\tau_{\varphi}:=\varphi^{-1}\circ\tau\circ\varphi
. Since splittings are unique up to
\mathsf{Z}
, we have that

b_{\varphi}:=\tau_{\varphi}\cdot\tau^{-1}:\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\to\mathsf{Z},

is smooth and depends smoothly on
\varphi\in\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\varphi^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}(g\mathsf{Z},h\mathsf{Z},\xi)=

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}(g\mathsf{Z},h\mathsf{Z},\xi)e^{-i\xi b_{\varphi}(g\mathsf{Z})}e^{-i\xi b_{\varphi}(h\mathsf{Z})}e^{i\xi b_{\varphi}(gh\mathsf{Z})}.

The computations above implies that
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
acts
C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
by setting

\varphi.f(g\mathsf{Z},\xi)=e^{-i\xi b_{\varphi^{-1}}(g\mathsf{Z})}(\varphi^{-1})^{*}(f)(g\mathsf{Z},\xi),

for
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
. Indeed, for
\varphi\in\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
and
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
,

\displaystyle(\varphi.f_{1}

\displaystyle\ast_{\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}}\varphi.f_{2})(g,\xi)=

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}}e^{-i\xi b_{\varphi^{-1}}(h)}f_{1}(\varphi^{-1}h,\varphi^{*}\xi)e^{-i\xi b_{\varphi^{-1}}(h^{-1}g)}f_{2}(\varphi^{-1}(h^{-1}g),\varphi^{*}\xi)\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}(h,h^{-1}g,\xi)\mathrm{d}h=

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}}f_{1}(\varphi^{-1}h,\varphi^{*}\xi)f_{2}(\varphi^{-1}(h^{-1}g),\varphi^{*}\xi)(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}(h,h^{-1}g,\xi)\mathrm{e}^{i\xi b_{\varphi^{-1}}(g)}\mathrm{d}h=

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}}f_{1}(h^{\prime},\varphi^{*}\xi)f_{2}(h^{\prime-1}\varphi^{-1}(g),\varphi^{*}\xi)\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}(h^{\prime},h^{\prime-1}\varphi^{-1}(g),\varphi^{*}\xi)\mathrm{e}^{i\xi b_{\varphi^{-1}}(g)}\mathrm{d}h^{\prime}=

\displaystyle=\varphi.(f_{1}\ast_{\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}}f_{2})(g,\xi).

In the second last equality we changed coordinates to
h^{\prime}=\varphi^{-1}h
. We arrive at a strongly continuous action
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})\to\mathrm{Aut}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}))
.
∎

Theorem 0.11.5
.

Let
\mathsf{G}
be a simply connected nilpotent Lie group with center
\mathsf{Z}=\mathfrak{z}
. Fourier transform in the central direction defines an
\mathsf{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
-equivariant
*
-isomorphism

\mathcal{F}_{Z}:C^{*}(\mathsf{G})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}),

where the Fourier transform in the central direction is defined by density on
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathsf{G})=\mathcal{S}(\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathsf{Z})
(cf. Remark
0.10.13
and
0.10.15
) as
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}(f)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{Z}})
where

\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}(f)(g\mathsf{Z},\xi)=\int_{\mathfrak{z}}e^{i\xi(t)}f(g\mathsf{Z},t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

(24)

Moreover, this isomorphism restricts to an isomorphism
I_{\mathsf{G}}\cong C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\Gamma},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
, i.e. we have a commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\mathsf{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\Gamma},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathsf{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}}

The last conclusion of the theorem is interpreted as an empty statement if
\Gamma
is empty. Another way of formulating the last conclusion of the theorem is that
I_{\mathsf{G}}\subseteq C^{*}(\mathsf{G})
consists of those elements whose Fourier transform in the central direction is supported in
\Gamma\subseteq\mathfrak{z}^{*}
. In Equation (
24
) we are implicitly identifying
\mathsf{G})=\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathsf{Z}
via the splitting
\tau
, i.e. we are identifying
f(g\mathsf{Z},t)=f(\tau(g\mathsf{Z})t)
.

Proof.

For
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathsf{G})
it is clear that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}(f)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}})
. We start with verifying the equivariance property. For
\varphi\in\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
and
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathsf{G})
we have that
\varphi.f(g)=f(\varphi^{-1}(g))
which we in Equation (
24
) are implicitly identifying with

\displaystyle\varphi.f(g\mathsf{Z},t)=

\displaystyle f(\varphi^{-1}(\tau(g\mathsf{Z}))\varphi^{-1}(t))=f(\tau_{\varphi}(\varphi^{-1}(g)\mathsf{Z})\varphi^{-1}(t))=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f(\tau(\varphi^{-1}(g)\mathsf{Z})b_{\varphi}(\varphi^{-1}(g)\mathsf{Z})\varphi^{-1}(t)),

where we in the last equality used that
\tau_{\varphi}=\tau\cdot b_{\varphi}
where
b_{\varphi}
takes central values. Therefore

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}(\varphi.f)(g\mathsf{Z},\xi)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{z}}e^{i\xi(t)}f(\tau(\varphi^{-1}(g)\mathsf{Z})b_{\varphi}(g\mathsf{Z})\varphi^{-1}(t))\mathrm{d}t=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{z}}e^{i\xi(\varphi(s)-\varphi(b_{\varphi}(\varphi^{-1}(g)\mathsf{Z})))}f(\tau(\varphi^{-1}(g)\mathsf{Z})s)\mathrm{d}s=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-i\xi b_{\varphi^{-1}}(g\mathsf{Z})}\int_{\mathfrak{z}}e^{i\varphi^{*}(\xi)s}f(\tau(\varphi^{-1}(g)\mathsf{Z})s)\mathrm{d}s=\left(\varphi.\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}(f)\right)(g\mathsf{Z},\xi),

where we in the second identity made the change of variables
s=b_{\varphi}(g\mathsf{Z})\varphi^{-1}(t)
and in the third equality we used that
\varphi\circ b_{\varphi}\circ\varphi^{-1}=b_{\varphi^{-1}}
.

Let us verify that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}:\mathcal{S}(\mathsf{G})\to\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
is a
*
-homomorphism. For
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathsf{G})
, we have that

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}(f_{1})

\displaystyle\ast_{\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}(f_{2}))(g,\xi)=\int_{\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}(f_{1})(h,\xi)\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}(f_{2})(h^{-1}g,\xi)\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{Z}}(h,h^{-1}g,\xi)\mathrm{d}h=

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}}\int_{\mathfrak{z}}\int_{\mathfrak{z}}e^{i\xi(t_{1}+t_{2})}f_{1}(h,t_{1})f_{2}(h^{-1}g,t_{2})\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{Z}}(h,h^{-1}g,\xi)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}h=

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}}\int_{\mathfrak{z}}\int_{\mathfrak{z}}e^{i\xi(t_{1}+\omega_{Z}(h,h^{-1}g))}f_{1}(h,t_{1}-t_{2}-\omega_{Z}(h,h^{-1}g))\cdot

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\cdot f_{2}(h^{-1}g,t_{2})\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{Z}}(h,h^{-1}g,\xi)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}h=

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}}\int_{\mathfrak{z}}\int_{\mathfrak{z}}e^{i\xi(t_{1})}f_{1}(h,t_{1}-t_{2}-\omega_{Z}(h,h^{-1}g))f_{2}(h^{-1}g,t_{2})\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}h=

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathfrak{z}}e^{i\xi(t_{1})}(f_{1}\ast f_{2})(g,t_{1})\mathrm{d}t_{1}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}(f_{1}\ast f_{2})(g,\xi).

By a similar computation, we have for
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathsf{G})
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}(f^{*})

\displaystyle(g,\xi)=\int_{\mathfrak{z}}e^{i\xi(t)}\overline{f(g^{-1}\mathsf{Z},-t+\omega_{\mathsf{Z}}(g,g^{-1}))}\mathrm{d}t=

\displaystyle=e^{i\xi(\omega_{\mathsf{Z}}(g,g^{-1}))}\int_{\mathfrak{z}}e^{-i\xi(t)}\overline{f(g^{-1}\mathsf{Z},t)}\mathrm{d}t=

\displaystyle=e^{i\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}((g,\xi),(g^{-1},\xi)))}\overline{\int_{\mathfrak{z}}e^{i\xi(t)}f(g^{-1}\mathsf{Z},t)\mathrm{d}t}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}(f)^{*}(g,\xi).

This shows that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}:\mathcal{S}(\mathsf{G})\to\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})\subseteq C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
is a
*
-homomorphism. By the universal property, the Fourier transform in the central direction extends to a
*
-homomorphism
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}:C^{*}(\mathsf{G})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
. By Fourier inversion,
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}:\mathcal{S}(\mathsf{G})\to\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
is a
*
-isomorphism, with inverse being the inverse Fourier transform, which by the same argument extends to a
*
-homomorphism
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}^{-1}:C^{*}(\mathsf{G})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
that inverts
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}
. Therefore
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}:C^{*}(\mathsf{G})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
is a
*
-isomorphism.

It remains to prove that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}
restricts to a
*
-isomorphism
I_{\mathsf{G}}\cong C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\Gamma},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
. For
\xi\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}
, we write
\lambda^{\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}}_{\xi}
for the localization of the left regular representation of
C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})
in
\xi
. We let
\pi_{\mathsf{Z},\xi}:=\lambda^{\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}}_{\xi}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}
denote the associated unitary representation of
G
. Restricting
\pi_{\mathsf{Z},\xi}
to the center leads to the dichotomy that
\pi_{\mathsf{Z},\xi}
is either irreducible and corresponds to the flat orbit
\mathcal{O}_{\xi}
(which holds if and only if
\xi\in\Lambda
) or decomposes over representations from
\hat{\mathsf{G}}\setminus\Gamma
(which holds if and only if
\xi\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}\setminus\Lambda
). We conclude that

\displaystyle I_{\mathsf{G}}

\displaystyle=\{a\in C^{*}(\mathsf{G}):\lambda^{\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}}_{\xi}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}a)=0\,\forall\xi\in\mathfrak{z}^{*}\setminus\Lambda\}=

\displaystyle=\{a\in C^{*}(\mathsf{G}):\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z}}a\in C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\Gamma},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}})\},

and the theorem follows.
∎

0.12.
Locally trivial bundles of nilpotent Lie groups

In this subsection, we consider a situation appearing on Carnot manifolds (see Section
0.15
below). The tangent space of a Carnot manifold can be equipped with a certain Lie algebroid structure that integrates to a groupoid with
r=s
and each
r
-fibre is a simply connected nilpotent Lie group.

Proposition 0.12.1
.

Let
(\mathbf{g},\rho,[\cdot,\cdot])
be a Lie algebroid on
M
with anchor map
\rho=0
. Then the Lie bracket on
\mathbf{g}
restricts to a well-defined Lie bracket on each fibre and
\mathbf{g}\to M
is a smooth bundle with a fibrewise defined Lie algebra structure depending smoothly on the base. If there exists an
N\geq 0
such that each fibre of
\mathbf{g}
is at most step
N
nilpotent, the Hausdorff-Baker-Campbell formula integrates to a Lie groupoid structure on
\mathcal{G}:=\mathbf{g}
with
\mathsf{Lie}(\mathcal{G})=\mathbf{g}
.

Proof.

Since
\rho=0
, the bracket
[\cdot,\cdot]
is
C^{\infty}(M)
-linear and the first part of the proposition follows. The Hausdorff-Baker-Campbell formula defines a polynomial map
\mathbf{g}\times\mathbf{g}\to\mathbf{g}
which we can consider a composition on
\mathcal{G}:=\mathbf{g}
. It is readily verified that this is a Lie groupoid for
r=s
being the projection
\mathbf{g}\to M
,
i
defined as the additive inverse on each fiber and
e:M\to\mathcal{G}=\mathbf{g}
being the zero section.
∎

In the case of interest in this work, regular Carnot manifolds (see Section
0.15
below), further structures are imposed.

Definition 0.12.2
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a Lie groupoid with
r=s
, and simply connected
r
-fibers and let
\mathfrak{g}
be a nilpotent Lie algebra.

We say that
\mathcal{G}
is a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups of type
\mathfrak{g}
if the Lie algebroid
\mathbf{g}:=\mathsf{Lie}(\mathcal{G})\to M
is a locally trivial bundle of Lie algebras with typical fibre
\mathfrak{g}
, i.e. every
x\in M
admits a neighbourhood
U
and a Lie algebroid isomorphism
\mathbf{g}_{U}\cong U\times\mathfrak{g}
.

If
\mathfrak{g}
is graded, we say that
\mathcal{G}
is a locally trivial bundle of Carnot-Lie groups of type
\mathfrak{g}
if the Lie algebroid
\mathbf{g}:=\mathsf{Lie}(\mathcal{G})\to M
is graded and the local trivializations are graded.

Proposition 0.12.3
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups of type
\mathfrak{g}
, and set
\mathbf{g}:=\mathsf{Lie}(\mathcal{G})
and
\mathsf{G}:=\mathrm{exp}(\mathfrak{g})
. Then the following holds:

(1)

The bundle
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathbf{g})\to M
of fibrewise Lie algebra automorphisms is a principal
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})
-bundle and there is an isomorphism of Lie algebroids

\mathbf{g}\cong\mathrm{Aut}(\mathbf{g})\times_{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})}\mathfrak{g}.

(2)

If
\mathfrak{g}
is graded and
\mathcal{G}\to M
is a locally trivial bundle of Carnot-Lie groups, the bundle
\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathbf{g})\to M
of fibrewise graded Lie algebra automorphisms is a principal
\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathfrak{g})
-bundle and there is an isomorphism of Lie algebroids

\mathbf{g}\cong\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathbf{g})\times_{\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathfrak{g})}\mathfrak{g}.

(3)

There is a groupoid isomorphism
\mathcal{G}\cong\mathrm{Aut}(\mathbf{g})\times_{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})}\mathsf{G}
, and if
\mathcal{G}
is a locally trivial bundle of Carnot-Lie groups then
\mathcal{G}\cong\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathbf{g})\times_{\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathfrak{g})}\mathsf{G}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathbf{g}
is locally a trivial
\mathfrak{g}
-bundle, it follows that every point
x\in M
admits a neighbourhood
U
such that
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathbf{g})_{U}=\mathrm{Aut}(\mathbf{g}_{U})\cong U\times\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})
so
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathbf{g})\to M
is a principal
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})
-bundle. The remainder of the proof follows from standard verifications in local charts.
∎

For a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups, we shall now define bundles of flat orbits and related ideals. These results are extensions of Theorem
0.11.5
from nilpotent Lie groups to locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups. First, we shall need a multitude of notations.

Definition 0.12.4
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups of type
\mathfrak{g}
coming from a principal
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})
-bundle
P\to M
as in Proposition
0.12.3
.

•

We define the central subgroupoid as

\mathcal{Z}:=P\times_{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})}\mathsf{Z}(\mathsf{G})\to M,

which is a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups of type
\mathfrak{z}
. We identify
\mathcal{Z}
with a subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
.

•

If we pick a complement of
\mathcal{Z}\cong\mathsf{Lie}(\mathcal{Z})
in
\mathbf{g}
, we can similarly as in Equation (
23
) define a smooth
2
-cocycle

\omega_{\mathcal{Z}}:\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Z}.

•

We let
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathcal{Z}^{*}
denote the pull back of
\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{Z}
to
\mathcal{Z}^{*}
. The Lie groupoid
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}
is a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups of type
\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}
and a short computation shows that when viewing
\mathbf{g}^{*}
as a vector bundle on
\mathcal{Z}^{*}
via the restriction map, we can identify

\mathbf{g}^{*}=\mathsf{Lie}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}})^{*}.

•

Define the
2
-cocycle

\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}}:\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}^{(2)}\rightarrow U(1),

as in Definition
0.11.3
.

•

We also set

\Gamma_{P}:=P\times_{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})}\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{Z}^{*},

and the locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups
\mathcal{G}_{\Gamma,P}:=\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}|_{\Gamma_{P}}\to\Gamma_{P}
(it is again of type
\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}
).

•

We define
\Xi_{P}:=P\times_{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})}\Xi
. In light of Proposition
0.6.9
, we can consider
\Xi_{P}\to\Gamma_{P}
as a vector bundle. In fact, a short computation shows that

\Xi_{P}=\mathsf{Lie}(\mathcal{G}_{\Gamma,P})^{*}.

We note that there is an inclusion

\Xi_{P}\subseteq\mathbf{g}^{*}.

(25)

Assume that
G
admits flat orbits. We define
I_{P}
as the
C_{0}
-sections of the bundle of
C^{*}
-algebras

P\times_{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})}I_{\mathsf{G}}\to M,

and
C^{*}(\mathsf{G})_{P}
as the
C_{0}
-sections of the bundle of
C^{*}
-algebras

P\times_{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})}C^{*}(\mathsf{G})\to M.

Note that the frame bundle induces an isomorphism
C^{*}(\mathsf{G})_{P}\cong C^{*}(\mathcal{G})
.

Proposition 0.12.5
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups of type
\mathfrak{g}
defined from a principal
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})
-bundle
P\to M
. Assume that
\mathsf{G}:=\mathrm{exp}(\mathfrak{g})
admits flat orbits. Then the
C^{*}
-algebra
I_{P}
is a continuous trace algebra with spectrum
\Gamma_{P}
and

\delta_{\rm DD}(I_{P})=0\in\check{H}^{3}(\Gamma_{P},\mathbb{Z}).

Proof.

By local triviality of
P
,
p_{\Gamma}:\Gamma_{P}\to M
is locally trivial. We can therefore cover
M
by a open subsets
(U_{j})_{j\in J}
and for local trivializations
\tau_{j}:p_{\Gamma}^{-1}(U_{j})\to U_{j}\times\Gamma
we can cover
\Gamma_{P}
by the open subsets
(\tau_{j}^{-1}(U_{j}\times U_{\boldsymbol{B}}))_{j\in J,\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathfrak{JH}}
. The fact that
I_{\mathsf{G}}
is a continuous trace algebra, we can use the argument in Proposition
0.8.5
to lift the transition functions for
I_{P}
defined on a refinement of the cover
(\tau_{j}^{-1}(U_{j}\times U_{\boldsymbol{B}}))_{j\in J,\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathfrak{JH}}
to a unitary valued Cech cocycle on
\Gamma_{P}
.
∎

The reader can compare Proposition
0.12.5
to Theorem
0.14.6
below. The construction of Proposition
0.12.5
gives a direct method to construct the bundle of flat orbits from the
\eta
-invariants of Definition
0.4.3
and the corrected Lion intertwiner of Proposition
0.8.5
, while Theorem
0.14.6
proves
\delta(I_{P})=0
via a deformation argument.

Let us provide another construction of the bundle of flat orbit representations from an induction procedure. This induction procedure is to our knowledge not always attainable as it relies on finding a suitable subgroup
\text{\mxedr.g}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})
to which we can reduce
P
.

Proposition 0.12.6
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups of type
\mathfrak{g}
as in Proposition
0.12.5
. Assume that there is a subgroup
\text{\mxedr.g}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})
to which
P
can be reduced, and satisfying
[\zeta|_{\text{\mxedr.g}}]=0
and
[c_{\text{\mxedr.g}}]=0
. Then letting
\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma
denote a Hilbert space bundle and
\pi_{\musFlat}
an isomorphism as in Theorem
0.8.11
, item 1), the mapping
\pi_{\musFlat,\text{\mxedr.g}}:=\mathrm{id}\times_{\text{\mxedr.g}}\pi_{\musFlat}
defines a
C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})
-linear isomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras

\pi_{\musFlat,\text{\mxedr.g}}:I_{P}\to C_{0}(\Gamma_{P},\mathbb{K}(\mathcal{H}_{P})),

where
\mathcal{H}_{P}=P\times_{\text{\mxedr.g}}\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{P}
is the induced Hilbert space bundle.

The proof of Proposition
0.12.6
follows from local triviality of the reduction of
P
to
\mxedr
.g
. From the
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
-equivariance of the Fourier transform in the central direction from Theorem
0.11.5
, we can deduce the following description of the
C^{*}
-algebras
C^{*}(\mathsf{G})_{P}
and
I_{P}
as twisted groupoid
C^{*}
-algebras.

Proposition 0.12.7
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups of type
\mathfrak{g}
as in Proposition
0.12.5
. The Fourier transform in the central direction (cf. Theorem
0.11.5
) defines
*
-isomorphisms

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Z}}:C^{*}(\mathsf{G})_{P}\to C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Z}}:I_{P}\to C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\Gamma,P},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}}),

that fits into the commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\Gamma,P},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathsf{G})_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Z}}}

0.13.
Nistor’s Connes-Thom isomorphism and the ideal of flat orbits

In this subsection we will relate the
K
-theory of
I_{G}
to that of the vector bundle
\Xi\to\Gamma
and the
K
-theory of
C^{*}(G)
to that of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. The latter result is known due to work of Nistor
[
119
]
. What will play an important role later on in the monograph is that the results also works for bundles of simply connected nilpotent Lie groups and that they are compatible with the inclusions
I_{G}\hookrightarrow C^{*}(\mathsf{G})
and
C_{0}(\Xi)\hookrightarrow C_{0}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
. The
K
-theory computations are carried out using an adiabatic deformation considered by Nistor, so we therefore formulate the results of this subsection in the language of groupoids.

For a simply connected nilpotent Lie group
\mathsf{G}
we define its adiabatic deformation as

\mathsf{G}_{\rm adb}:=\mathfrak{g}\times\{0\}\dot{\cup}\mathsf{G}\times(0,1].

The fibrewise operations makes
\mathsf{G}_{\rm adb}\rightrightarrows[0,1]
into a groupoid. This is the adiabatic deformation (as in Example (
0.10.8
)) of
\mathsf{G}
viewed as a groupoid over a point. We note that in this case, the smooth structure of
\mathsf{G}_{\rm adb}
is defined by declaring the map
\psi:\mathfrak{g}\times[0,1]\to\mathsf{G}_{\rm adb}
, given by

\psi(X,t):=\begin{cases}(X,0),\;&t=0,\\
(\mathrm{e}^{tX},t),\;&t>0,\end{cases}

to be smooth. We also define a variation on the adiabatic groupoid pertaining the homogeneous structure of the central characters. Since the nilpotent Lie groups arising in the fibre of the osculating groupoid of a Carnot manifold are Carnot-Lie groups (see below in Section
0.15
), we shall adopt our constructions to the dilation action but remark that similar constructions can be carried out for general nilpotent Lie groups.

Definition 0.13.1
.

The groupoid
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}\rightrightarrows\mathfrak{z}^{*}\times[0,1]
, where
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}
is as in Definition
0.11.2
, is defined by

\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}=(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*}\times\{0\})\dot{\cup}(\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*}\times(0,1]),

and whose smooth structure is defined by declaring the map
\psi:\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*}\times[0,1]\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}
, given by

\psi(X,\xi,t):=\begin{cases}(X,\xi,0),\;&t=0,\\
(\mathrm{e}^{\delta_{t}X},\delta_{t^{-1}}^{*}\xi,t),\;&t>0,\end{cases}

to be smooth. The groupoid operations on
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}
are the fibrewise operations.

We note that

\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}^{(2)}=(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}\times\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*}\times\{0\})\dot{\cup}(\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*}\times(0,1]).

The group
\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathsf{G})
acts as smooth groupoid automorphisms of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}
by

\begin{cases}\varphi.(X,\xi,0):=(\varphi(X),(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\xi,0),\;&t=0,\\
\varphi.(g,\xi,t):=(\varphi(g),(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\xi,t),\;&t>0.\end{cases}

Definition 0.13.2
.

Consider the groupoid

\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}:=\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}\times[0,1]\to\rightrightarrows\mathfrak{z}^{*}\times[0,1]^{2}.

Define the mapping

\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}:\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}^{(2)}\rightarrow U(1),

by

\begin{cases}\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}(X,Y,\xi,0,s)=\mathrm{e}^{is\omega_{\xi}(X,Y)}=\mathrm{e}^{is\xi[X,Y]},\\
\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}(g,h,\xi,t,s)=e^{-is\xi.\omega_{\mathsf{Z}}(g,h)},\quad t>0.\end{cases}

We note that the restriction of
\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}
to
s=1
and
t>0
coincides with
\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}}
, the cocycle from Definition
0.11.3
. We also note that
\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}|_{s=0}=1
.

Proposition 0.13.3
.

Let
\mathsf{G}
be a simply connected nilpotent Lie group and
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}
be constructed as above. The mapping
\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}
is a smooth cocycle. Moreover, the
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
-action on
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}
lifts to a
C([0,1]^{2})
-linear strongly continuous
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
-action on
C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}})
following the constructions proceeding Theorem
0.11.5
.

Proof.

To prove that
\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}
is a smooth cocycle, we note that it is by construction a cocycle pointwise so it suffices to prove that

\psi^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}:\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}\times\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*}\times[0,1]\to U(1),

is smooth. We compute that

\psi^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}(X,Y,\xi,t,s)=\begin{cases}\mathrm{e}^{is\xi[X,Y]},\;&t=0,\\
e^{-is(\delta_{t^{-1}}^{*}\xi).(\omega_{\mathsf{Z}}(\mathrm{e}^{\delta_{t}X},\mathrm{e}^{\delta_{t}Y}))},\;&t>0.\end{cases}

We
\rho_{\mathfrak{g}}(X,Y)=\log(\mathrm{e}^{X}\mathrm{e}^{Y})
, which is a polynomial map
\mathfrak{g}\times\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}
since
\mathfrak{g}
is nilpotent. Let
j:\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}\to\mathfrak{g}
denote the inclusion defined from the Jordan-Hölder basis that we use to define
\omega_{\mathsf{Z}}
. A short computation allows us to write

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathsf{Z}}(\mathrm{e}^{\delta_{t}X},\mathrm{e}^{\delta_{t}Y})=

\displaystyle\rho_{\mathfrak{g}}(\delta_{t}j(X),\rho_{\mathfrak{g}}(\delta_{t}Y,-j\rho_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}}(\delta_{t}X,\delta_{t}Y)))=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-[\delta_{t}X,\delta_{t}Y]+P(X,Y,t)=-\delta_{t}[X,Y]+P(X,Y,t),

for a polynomial
P(X,Y,t)
such that
\delta_{t^{-1}}P(X,Y,t)
vanishes to first order at
t=0
. The last equality follows from the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula. We conclude that

\displaystyle\psi^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}(X,Y,\xi,t,s)=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathrm{e}^{is\xi[X,Y]},\;&t=0\\
e^{is(\delta_{t^{-1}}^{*}\xi).(\delta_{t}[X,Y]-P(X,Y,t))},\;&t>0.\end{cases}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathrm{e}^{is\xi[X,Y]},\;&t=0\\
e^{is\xi.([X,Y]-\delta_{t^{-1}}P(X,Y,t))},\;&t>0.\end{cases},

and that
\psi^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}
is smooth.

The proof that the
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
-action lifts goes as in Theorem
0.11.5
.
∎

We introduce the notation
\Delta\subseteq[0,1]\times[0,1]
for the diagonal. When appropriate, we identify
\Delta
with
[0,1]
.

Proposition 0.13.4
.

Let
\mathsf{G}
be a simply connected nilpotent Lie group and
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}
and
\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}}
be constructed as above.

•

The groupoid homomorphism

\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}\times(0,1]\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}\times(0,1],

defined by

\begin{cases}(X,Y,\xi,0,s)\mapsto(X,Y,s^{-1}\xi,0,s),\;&t=0,\\
(g,h,\xi,t,s)\mapsto(g,h,s^{-1}\xi,t,s),\;&t>0.\end{cases}

induces an
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
-equivariant
C([0,1]^{2})
-linear isomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras

C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}\times(0,1],\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}})\to C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}|_{s=1})\otimes C_{0}(0,1].

•

The Fourier transform in the central direction from Theorem
0.11.5
defines an
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
-equivariant
C[0,1]
-linear isomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras

C^{*}(\mathsf{G}_{{\rm adb}})\to C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}|_{\Delta},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}|_{\Delta}).

We omit the proof as it follows from standard computations and the proof of Theorem
0.11.5
. For the next proposition, we recall that if
\Xi\to\Gamma
denotes the total space of flat orbits there is an explicit vector bundle trivialization
\Xi\cong\Gamma\times\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}
by Proposition
0.6.9
that is readily verified to be
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
-equivariant. We conclude that there is an
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
-equivariant groupoid isomorphism

\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}|_{\Gamma\times\{0\}}\cong\Xi^{*},

where the vector bundle
\Xi^{*}\to\Gamma
is considered as a groupoid in the fibrewise addition. We consider
\Xi^{*}
as an open subgroupoid of the Lie groupoid
\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*}\rightrightarrows\mathfrak{z}^{*}
. The Lie groupoid carries a unitary
2
-cocycle

\omega(X,Y,\xi):=\mathrm{e}^{i\xi[X,Y]}.

(26)

Note that
\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*}=\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}|_{s=0}
and
\omega=\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}}|_{t=0,s=1}
.
We write
\Delta_{0}
for the diagonal in
[0,1)^{2}
. The following proposition follows from the notational discussion above, Theorem
0.10.18
and Proposition
0.11.5
.

Proposition 0.13.5
.

Let
\mathsf{G}
be a simply connected nilpotent Lie group admitting flat orbits and
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}
and
\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}}
be constructed as above. Then there are
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
-equivariant commuting diagrams with exact rows:

\begin{CD}0@>{}>{}>\Sigma I_{\mathsf{G}}@>{}>{}>C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}|_{\Gamma\times\Delta_{0}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}})@>{\mathrm{ev}_{0}}>{}>C_{0}(\Xi)@>{}>{}>0\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@ VVV\\
0@>{}>{}>\Sigma C^{*}(\mathsf{G})@>{}>{}>C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}|_{\Delta_{0}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}})@>{\mathrm{ev}_{0}}>{}>C_{0}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})@>{}>{}>0\\
\end{CD},

and

\begin{CD}0@>{}>{}>\Sigma I_{\mathsf{G}}@>{}>{}>C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}|_{\Gamma\times[0,1)\times\{1\}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}})@>{\mathrm{ev}_{0}}>{}>C^{*}(\Xi^{*},\omega)@>{}>{}>0\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@ VVV\\
0@>{}>{}>\Sigma C^{*}(\mathsf{G})@>{}>{}>C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}|_{[0,1)\times\{1\}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}})@>{\mathrm{ev}_{0}}>{}>C^{*}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*},\omega)@>{}>{}>0\\
\end{CD},

and

\begin{CD}0@>{}>{}>\Sigma C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)@>{}>{}>C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}|_{\Gamma\times\{0\}\times[0,1)},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}})@>{\mathrm{ev}_{0}}>{}>C_{0}(\Xi)@>{}>{}>0\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@ VVV\\
0@>{}>{}>\Sigma C^{*}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*},\omega)@>{}>{}>C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}|_{\{0\}\times[0,1)},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}})@>{\mathrm{ev}_{0}}>{}>C_{0}(\mathfrak{g})@>{}>{}>0\\
\end{CD},

The vertical maps in the three diagrams above are all ideal inclusions.

Definition 0.13.6
.

We define the
KK
-morphisms

•

\psi\in KK^{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})}_{0}(C_{0}(\mathfrak{g}^{*}),C^{*}(\mathsf{G}))
;

•

\psi_{I}\in KK^{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})}_{0}(C_{0}(\Xi),I_{\mathsf{G}})
;

•

\psi_{\omega}\in KK^{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})}_{0}(C^{*}(\mathfrak{g},\omega),C^{*}(\mathsf{G}))
;

•

\psi_{I,\omega}\in KK^{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})}_{0}(C^{*}(\Xi^{*},\omega),I_{\mathsf{G}})
;

•

\psi_{\Xi}\in KK^{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})}_{0}(C_{0}(\Xi),C^{*}(\Xi^{*},\omega))
;

as the boundary mappings from the short exact sequences appearing in Proposition
0.13.5
.

Remark 0.13.7
.

We remark that a standard argument shows that

\psi=[\mathrm{ev}_{0}]^{-1}\otimes_{C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}|_{\Delta},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}})}[\mathrm{ev}_{1}],

where the evaluation maps are

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}_{0}

\displaystyle:C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}|_{\Delta},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}})\to C_{0}(\mathfrak{g}^{*}),\quad\mbox{and},

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}_{1}

\displaystyle:C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}|_{\Delta},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}})\to C^{*}(\mathsf{G}).

Indeed,
[\mathrm{ev}_{0}]\in KK(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}|_{\Delta},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm sqadb}}}),C_{0}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
is invertible as it is surjective and its kernel is contractible by Proposition
0.13.4
. Analogous statements hold for
\psi_{I}
,
\psi_{\omega}
,
\psi_{I,\omega}
and
\psi_{I,\Xi}
as well.

Remark 0.13.8
.

Given a group
G
and a principal
G
-bundle
P\to X
one has an induction functor
P:KK^{G}\rightarrow KK^{X}
such that for a
G-C^{*}-
algebra
A

P(A):=\{f:P\rightarrow A:f(p.g)=g^{-1}.f(p),\;(x\mapsto\|f(p)\|_{A})\in C_{0}(X)\}.

For the principal
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})
-bundle
P\to M
associated with a locally trivial bundle
\mathcal{G}
of nilpotent Lie groups on
M
, then

C^{*}(\mathbf{g})=C_{0}(\mathbf{g}^{*})=P(C_{0}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})),\;C^{*}(\mathcal{G})=P(C^{*}(\mathsf{G})),\;\mbox{and}\;C_{0}(\Xi_{P})=P(C_{0}(\Xi)),

and so forth.

Theorem 0.13.9
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of Lie groups of type
\mathsf{G}
with frame bundle
P\to M
. We denote its Lie algebroid by
\mathbf{g}
. Then
\psi\in KK^{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})}_{0}(C_{0}(\mathfrak{g}^{*}),C^{*}(\mathsf{G}))
induces an element
\psi_{P}\in KK(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}),C_{0}(\mathbf{g}^{*}))
such that

•

\psi_{P}
is a
KK
-isomorphism, in particular the Kasparov product with
\psi_{P}
defines isomorphisms

K_{*}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}))\cong K^{*}(\mathbf{g}^{*}).

•

The element
\psi_{P}
is natural with respect to invariant open inclusions and restriction to invariant closed subsets, and in particular if
\mathsf{G}
admits flat orbits then there is a commuting diagram in
KK

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\mathsf{G},P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\Xi_{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\mathbf{g}^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{P,I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{P}}

where the vertical morphisms are isomorphisms. Here, the element
\psi_{P,I}\in KK_{0}(C_{0}(\mathbf{g}^{*}|_{\Gamma_{P}}),I_{\mathsf{G},P})
is defined from
\psi_{I}\in KK^{\mathrm{Aut}(\mathsf{G})}_{0}(C_{0}(\Xi),I_{\mathsf{G}})
.

Proof.

By naturality of boundary mappings the second item of the theorem follows from the first item. To prove the first item, we follow the idea in
[
119
, Lemma 3]
. By a Mayer-Vietoris argument, we can assume that
\mathcal{G}=M\times G\to M
is the trivial bundle. If we pick a character
\chi:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathbb{R}
, the Packer-Raeburn stabilization trick and Connes’ Thom isomorphism allow to reduce the dimension of
G
and item 1 follows from induction on the dimension of
G
.
∎

Theorem 0.13.10
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of Lie groups over a compact manifold of type
\mathsf{G}
with frame bundle
P\to M
. Assume that
\mathsf{G}
admits flat orbits.
Then the map
K_{*}(I_{\mathsf{G},P})\to K_{*}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}))
induced by the inclusion is surjective.

Proof.

We can choose a cover
(U_{j})_{j}
of
M
such that
\mathcal{G}
is trivializable over a neighborhood of each
K_{j}:=\overline{U_{j}}
. Take the cover so that each
K_{j}
is contractible and each
K_{j}\cap K_{k}
contracts to a finite set of points . We can consider the Mayer-Vietoris triangle in
KK
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{j}C^{*}(\mathcal{G}|_{K_{j}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{j,k}C^{*}(\mathcal{G}|_{K_{j}\cap K_{k}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}

(27)

Here
r
is the restriction mapping to each
K_{j}
and
\partial
is of odd degree. Since
K_{j}
is contractible,
K_{*}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}|_{K_{j}}))\cong K_{*}(C^{*}(\mathsf{G}))\cong\mathbb{Z}
via Nistor’s Connes-Thom isomorphism. Therefore, the co-domain of
r
is a free abelian group and so is therefore the range of
r
. Therefore
r
admits a partial splitting
s:\bigoplus_{j}K_{*}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}|_{K_{j}}))\to K_{*}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}))
implementing an equality

K_{*}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}))=\mathrm{i}\mathrm{m}\,(s)+\partial\bigoplus_{j,k}K_{*-1}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}|_{K_{j}\cap K_{j})}).

Since
\Xi\subseteq\mathfrak{g}^{*}
is an inclusion of a Zariski open domain, the induced map
K^{*}(\Xi)\to K^{*}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
is a surjection. Indeed, by Bott periodicity
K^{*}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
is generated by a single element whose support we can place within an arbitrary open subset of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Therefore, Theorem
0.13.9
implies that
K_{*}(I_{\mathsf{G}})\to K_{*}(C^{*}(\mathsf{G}))
is surjective. There is an analogous Mayer-Vietoris sequence as in (
27
) also for
I_{\mathsf{G},P}
, so naturality of the Mayer-Vietoris sequence implies that

\partial\bigoplus_{j,k}K_{*-1}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}|_{K_{j}\cap K_{j})})\subseteq\mathrm{i}\mathrm{m}\,(K_{*}(I_{\mathsf{G},P})\to K_{*}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}))).

Similarly, we can choose the splitting
s
so that it is compatible with a partial splitting
s_{I}:\bigoplus_{j}K_{*}(I_{\mathsf{G},P}|_{K_{j}})\to K_{*}(I_{\mathsf{G},P})
, so it also holds that
\mathrm{i}\mathrm{m}\,(s)\subseteq\mathrm{i}\mathrm{m}\,(K_{*}(I_{\mathsf{G},P})\to K_{*}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G})))
. It follows that
K_{*}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G}))\subseteq\mathrm{i}\mathrm{m}\,(K_{*}(I_{\mathsf{G},P})\to K_{*}(C^{*}(\mathcal{G})))
.
∎

Proposition 0.13.11
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of Lie groups of type
\mathsf{G}
with frame bundle
P\to M
. Then the following diagram in
KK
commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\mathsf{G},P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\Xi_{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega_{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{I,P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\Xi,P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\omega,I,P}}

Proof.

The proof follows from naturality of boundary mappings and the fact that all involved maps comes from a deformation parametrized by
[0,1]^{2}
as in Theorem
0.13.5
.
∎

0.14.
The Thom-Connes isomorphism and the bundle of flat representations

The constructions with adiabatic deformations of Section
0.13
have favorable functorial properties and produce
K
-theoretical information, but for later purposes of computing with Heisenberg symbols we shall need to relate the Connes-Thom isomorphism to the bundle of flat representations from Section
0.8
. Along the way, we will give a new proof of the fact that the ideal of flat orbits, also in a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups, has vanishing Dixmier-Duoady class.

For these constructions, we shall need some further notation. Let
\mathsf{G}
denote a nilpotent Lie group admitting flat orbits and
\mathcal{G}\to M
a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups with fibre
\mathsf{G}
. Recall the groupoid

\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z}}=\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}\times\mathfrak{z}^{*}\rightrightarrows\mathfrak{z},

constructed in Section
0.11
. We can analogously define
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}\rightrightarrows\mathcal{Z}^{*}
as the pull back of
\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{Z}
up to the dual central subbundle
\mathcal{Z}^{*}
. Analogously to Definition
0.11.3
, after choosing a Riemannian metric for the Lie algebroid of
\mathcal{G}
, we have a unitary 2-cocycle
\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}}
on
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}
and a (relative to the choice of metric) canonical isomorphism

C^{*}(\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{\sim}C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}}),

implemented via the Fourier transform in the central direction as in Theorem
0.11.5
. This isomorphism implements an isomorphism
I_{\mathsf{G},P}\cong C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}|_{\Gamma_{P}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}}})
. Similarly to the constructions in Section
0.13
, we can also define the adiabatic deformation
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z},{\rm adb}}\rightrightarrows\mathcal{Z}^{*}
and the smooth adiabatic cocycle
\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z},{\rm adb}}}:(\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z},{\rm adb}}\times[0,1])^{(2)}\rightarrow U(1)
. We shall now use the machinery of Section
0.8
to describe the representation theory of
C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z},{\rm adb}}|_{\Gamma_{P}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}},{\rm adb}}^{s=1})
.

First, we consider the case of
M
being a point and later we shall glue these construction together. For a Jordan-Hölder basis
\boldsymbol{B}
of
\mathfrak{g}
, we introduce the notation

\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb},\boldsymbol{B}}:=\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}|_{U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\times[0,1]}.

Consider the subgroupoid
\mathcal{F}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb},\boldsymbol{B}}
defined as follows. We let
\mathcal{F}_{\boldsymbol{B}}\rightrightarrows U_{\boldsymbol{B}}
be the Lie groupoid that integrates the quotient bundle
F_{V,\boldsymbol{B}}/\mathfrak{z}\to U_{\boldsymbol{B}}
of the bundle Vergne polarizations from Proposition
0.5.2
. The groupoid
\mathcal{F}_{\boldsymbol{B}}
is well defined by Proposition
0.12.1
. We let
\mathcal{F}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}\rightrightarrows U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\times[0,1]
be the adiabatic deformation of
\mathcal{B}
constructed as in Definition
0.13.1
.

We define the space
\mathpzc{H}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb},c}
to consist of all
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb},\boldsymbol{B}})
such that

f(gh)=f(g)\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}}^{s=1}(g,h)^{-1},\quad\mbox{for}\quad(g,h)\in(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}\times\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}})\cap\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}^{(2)},

and that
f
is compactly supported in the
U_{\boldsymbol{B}}
-direction and of Schwarz class modulo
\mathcal{F}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}
in the
\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{Z}
-direction. On
\mathpzc{H}^{\infty}_{V,\varepsilon,c}
, there is a
C_{0}(U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\times[0,1])
-valued inner product (as on page
0.5
) and we let
\mathpzc{H}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}
denote its completion as a
C_{0}(U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\times[0,1])
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-module.

Following the constructions of Section
0.4
, we make the following observation. We implicitly use the isomorphisms of Proposition
0.13.4
.

Proposition 0.14.1
.

The
C_{0}(U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\times[0,1])
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-module
\mathpzc{H}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}

carries a natural
C[0,1]
-linear bijective representation

C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb},\boldsymbol{B}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}}^{s=1})\to\mathbb{K}_{C_{0}(U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\times[0,1]))}(\mathpzc{H}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}),

such that

•

There exists a bundle of Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{H}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}\to U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\times[0,1]
such that

\mathpzc{H}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}=C_{0}(U_{\boldsymbol{B}},\mathcal{H}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}).

•

\mathpzc{E}_{\boldsymbol{B}}|_{(0,1]}=C_{0}(U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\times(0,1],\mathcal{H}_{\boldsymbol{B},V})
as
(C_{0}((0,1],I_{\mathsf{G},\boldsymbol{B}}),C_{0}(U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\times(0,1]))
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules.

•

\mathpzc{E}_{\boldsymbol{B}}|_{t=0}=C_{0}(U_{\boldsymbol{B}},L^{2}(\mathfrak{g}/F_{V,\boldsymbol{B}}))
as
(C^{*}((\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z})\times U_{\boldsymbol{B}},\omega),C_{0}(U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\times(0,1]))
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules (using the notation
\omega
as in Equation (
26
)), where the action of
C^{*}((\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z})\times U_{\boldsymbol{B}},\omega)
on the bundle of Hilbert spaces
L^{2}(\mathfrak{g}/F_{V,\boldsymbol{B}})
is given by twisted convolution.

For two Jordan-Hölder basis
\boldsymbol{B}
and
\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime}
, we can (following Lemma
0.4.2
and Section
0.8
) define the adiabatic Lion intertwiner

\mathfrak{L}_{\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime},{\rm adb}}^{(0)}:\mathpzc{H}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}|_{U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\cap U_{\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime}}}\to\mathpzc{H}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}|_{U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\cap U_{\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime}}},

(28)

by extending the operation
\mathfrak{L}_{\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime},{\rm adb}}^{(0)}:\mathpzc{H}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime},{\rm adb},c}\to\mathpzc{H}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}
, defined by

\mathfrak{L}_{\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime},{\rm adb}}^{(0)}f(gh):=\int_{(d^{-1}(g)\cap\mathcal{F}_{V,\boldsymbol{B}})/(d^{-1}(g)\cap\mathcal{F}_{V,\boldsymbol{B}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{V,\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime}})}f(gh)\mathrm{d}h,

by unitarity. We remark that at
t=0
, we have deformed away all group structure while retaining the cocycle in the central direction so we can consider the adiabatic Lion intertwiner at fixed
\xi
and
t=0
as the Lion intertwiner for the polarizations that
F_{V,\boldsymbol{B}}(\xi)
and
F_{V,\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime}}(\xi)
define for the real Heisenberg algebra structure
(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z})_{\mathbb{R}\omega_{\xi}}
(for notation and further context, see Example
0.9.11
). In particular, a computation as in Proposition
0.8.5
and using Lemma
0.4.2
allow us to deduce the following:

Proposition 0.14.2
.

The adiabatic Lion intertwiner from Equation (
28
) intertwines the action of
C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}}^{s=1})
on
\mathpzc{H}_{\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime},{\rm adb}}
with that on
\mathpzc{H}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}
. Moreover, on triple intersections
U_{\boldsymbol{B}}\cap U_{\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime}}\cap U_{\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime\prime}}
the Lion intertwiners satisfy the following property:

\mathfrak{L}_{\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime},{\rm adb}}^{(0)}\mathfrak{L}_{\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime\prime},{\rm adb}}^{(0)}\mathfrak{L}_{\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime\prime},\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}^{(0)}\equiv\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\pi i}{4}\mathrm{Mas}(F_{V,\boldsymbol{B}},F_{V,\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime}},F_{V,\boldsymbol{B}^{\prime\prime}})}.

This will allow to make several conclusions for the structure of
C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z},{\rm adb}}|_{\Gamma_{P}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z}},{\rm adb}}^{s=1})
. We start by describing what happens at
t=0
. For this, and also later purposes, we need a lemma.

Lemma 0.14.3
.

Suppose that
A
is a continuous trace algebra with spectrum
X
and trivial Dixmier-Duoady invariant. Assume for
j=1,2
that
\mathcal{H}_{j}\to X
is a bundle of Hilbert spaces and
\pi_{j}:A\to C_{0}(X,\mathbb{K}(\mathcal{H}_{j}))
are
C_{0}(X)
-linear
*
-isomorphisms. Then there is a line bundle
L_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{2}}\to X
, uniquely determined up to isomorphism, such that

\mathcal{H}_{1}\cong L_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{2}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{2},

via a unitary intertwining
\pi_{1}
with
\pi_{2}
.

Proof.

By Kuiper’s theorem we can without restriction assume that
\mathcal{H}_{1}=X\times\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})
is trivial, that
A=C_{0}(X,\mathbb{K}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})))
and that
\pi_{1}
is the identity map. Pick a cover
\mathfrak{U}=(U_{j})_{j}
of
X
over which
\mathcal{H}_{2}
and
\pi_{2}
trivialises. In other words, there are unitary isomorphisms
L_{j}:\mathcal{H}_{2}|_{U_{j}}\cong U_{j}\times\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})
and
L_{j}aL_{j}^{*}=\pi_{2}(a)
for
a\in C_{0}(U_{j},\mathbb{K}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})))
. We note that for
a\in C_{0}(U_{j}\cap U_{k},\mathbb{K}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})))
,
L_{j}^{*}L_{k}a(L_{j}^{*}L_{k})^{*}=a
so
u_{jk}=L_{j}^{*}L_{k}
defines a
U(1)
-valued Cech
1
-cochain. It is direct from the construction that
(u_{jk})_{j,k}
is a
U(1)
-valued Cech
1
-cocycle, and it defines a line bundle
L_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{2}}\to X
. The collection
(L_{j})_{j}
glues together to the required isomorphism
\mathcal{H}_{1}\cong L_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{2}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{2}
.
∎

For locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups with fibre
\mathsf{G}
and frame bundle
P
, we define
\Xi_{P}
as above, and consider the vector bundle
\Xi_{P}^{*}\to\Gamma_{P}
as a groupoid. We consider the fibrewise the Kirillov form
\omega
as a unitary
2
-cocycle
\omega:(\Xi_{P}^{*})^{(2)}\to U(1)
. Choose a Riemannian metric
g_{\Xi}
on
\Xi_{P}
. The Riemannian metric and the Kirillov form induces a complex structure on
\Xi_{P}
, in particular
\Xi_{P}\to\Gamma_{P}
inherits a spin
c
-structure. Following the construction of the Fock space bundle in Example
0.9.20
, we arrive at a bundle of Fock spaces
\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}\to\Gamma_{P}
carrying an
\omega
-twisted representation of
\Xi_{P}^{*}
. As a bundle,
\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}\to\Gamma_{P}
is defined as

\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}:=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\infty}\Xi_{P}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}^{s}k}.

The twisted action of
\Xi_{P}^{*}
on
\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}
is defined as in Equation (
21
) by identifying
\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}
with the subbundle of fiberwise holomorphic sections of a weighted
L^{2}
-bundle defined from
\Xi_{P}^{*}\to\Gamma_{P}
and the Riemannian metric. The representation theory of the twisted groupoid
(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)
is computed from that of the locally trivial bundle of Heisenberg groups
\Xi_{P}^{*}\rtimes_{\omega}\mathbb{R}\to\Gamma_{P}
. Standard results on the Bargman-Fock representations, see for instance
[
71
]
, implies the following.

Proposition 0.14.4
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups and
g_{\Xi}
a choice of metric on its Lie algebroid. The bundle of Fock spaces

\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}:=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\infty}\Xi_{P}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}^{s}k}\to\Gamma_{P},

and the
\omega
-twisted action of
\Xi_{P}^{*}
on
\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}
defined as in Equation (
21
) defines a
*
-isomorphism

C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)\cong C_{0}(\Gamma_{P};\mathbb{K}(\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi})).

In particular, the
C^{*}
-algebra
C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)
is a continuous trace algebra with spectrum
\Gamma_{P}
and vanishing Dixmier-Duoady invariant and if
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\to\Gamma_{P}
is a bundle of Hilbert spaces such that there is an isomorphism

C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)\cong C_{0}(\Gamma_{P};\mathbb{K}(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})),

then there exists a line bundle
L_{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}\to\Gamma_{P}
and an isomorphism of bundles of Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\otimes L\cong\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}
.

The uniqueness of the Fock bundle up to line bundles follows from Lemma
0.14.3
. Next we consider a result that will play an important role in comparing the action of the Fock bundle on
K
–theory to Nistor’s Connes-Thom isomorphism.

Theorem 0.14.5
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups and
g_{\Xi}
a choice of metric on its Lie algebroid. The class
[\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}]\in KK_{0}(C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega),C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}))
defined using the isomorphism of Proposition
0.14.4
, fits into a commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\Xi_{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega_{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\not{D}_{\Xi}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\Xi,P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}]}

where
\psi_{\Xi,P}\in KK_{0}(C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega),C_{0}(\Xi_{P}))
is the deformation morphism induced from
\psi_{\Xi}
defined in Definition
0.13.6
and
[\not{D}_{\Xi}]\in KK_{0}(C_{0}(\Xi_{P}),C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}))
is the dual Thom class, i.e. the fibre-wise spin
c
-Dirac operator on
\Xi_{P}\to\Gamma_{P}
defined from the complex structure induced from
\omega
and
g_{\Xi}
.

Proof.

Let
\tau_{\Xi_{P}^{*}}\in KK_{0}(C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}),C_{0}(\Xi))
denote the Thom class, it has an unbounded
KK
-representative defined from
(C_{0}(\Xi_{P};S),T)
where
S\to\Xi_{P}
is the complex spinor bundle and
T
is given by Clifford multiplication by the fibrewise complex coordinate. A computation with the unbounded Kasparov product shows that the
KK
-element

\tau_{\Xi_{P}^{*}}\otimes_{C_{0}(\Xi_{P})}\psi_{\Xi,P}\in KK_{0}(C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}),C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)),

is represented by the unbounded
(C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}),C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega))
-Kasparov cycle
(C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega;S),\mathcal{T})
where
C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega;S)
is the completion of
\mathcal{S}(\Xi_{P}^{*};S)
in the
C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)
-valued inner product

\langle f,g\rangle(v)=\int_{(\Xi_{P}^{*})_{p(v)}}\omega(v+w,w)\langle f(w),g(v+w)\rangle_{S}\mathrm{d}w,\quad f,g\in\mathcal{S}(\Xi_{P}^{*};S),

and the operator
\mathcal{T}
is the regular self-adjoint operator on
C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega;S)
defined from the closure of
T
acting on the dense subspace
\mathcal{S}(\Xi_{P};S)\subseteq C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega;S)
.

The
KK
-element

\tau_{\Xi_{P}^{*}}\otimes_{C_{0}(\Xi_{P})}\psi_{\Xi,P}\otimes_{C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)}[\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}]\in KK_{0}(C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}),C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})),

is therefore represented by the unbounded
KK
-cycle

(\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi,S},\mathfrak{T})=(C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega;S)\otimes\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi},\mathcal{T}\otimes 1_{\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}}).

The
C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})
-module
\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi,S}
identifies with the Fock space of spinor valued sections and
\mathfrak{T}
with the fibrewise Toeplitz operator defined from Clifford multiplication by the fibrewise complex coordinate.

Let us prove that

[(\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi,S},\mathfrak{T})]=[(C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}),0)]=1_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})}.

(29)

One can prove Equation (
29
) by considering the corresponding unbounded cycle
(\mathpzc{F}_{0},\mathfrak{T}_{0})
localized to a point. That is,
\mathpzc{F}_{0}=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\infty}V^{\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}^{\rm sym}k}\otimes S_{0}
, for a complex spinor space
S_{0}
and
V=\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{z}
, and
\mathfrak{T}_{0}
is Clifford multiplication by the complex coordinate. The unbounded cycle
(\mathpzc{F}_{0},\mathfrak{T}_{0})
is
U(d)
-equivariant. The operator
\mathfrak{T}_{0}
is by standard considerations for Toeplitz operators surjective and has a one-dimensional kernel, on which
U(d)
acts trivially. Therefore, as classes in
KK_{0}^{U(d)}(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{C})
we have that

[(\mathpzc{F}_{0},\mathfrak{T}_{0})]=\varepsilon_{U(d)},

(30)

where
\varepsilon_{U(d)}
is the trivial
U(d)
-representation. Since
\Xi
has a complex structure, the lift of
P
to
\Gamma
has a reduction to a principal
U(d)
-bundle
P_{U}\to\Gamma_{P}
. By going to local coordinates we see that
[(\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi,S},\mathfrak{T})]
is the image of
[(\mathpzc{F}_{0},\mathfrak{T}_{0})]
under the induction functor

KK_{0}^{U(d)}(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{C})\to KK_{0}^{U(d)}(C_{0}(P_{U}),C_{0}(P_{U}))\to KK_{0}(C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}),C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})).

Therefore, Equation (
29
) follows from Equation (
30
).

We conclude that
\tau_{\Xi_{P}^{*}}\otimes_{C_{0}(\Xi_{P})}\psi_{\Xi,P}\otimes_{C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)}[\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}]=1_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})}
, so
\psi_{\Xi,P}\otimes_{C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)}[\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}]
is a right inverse to the Thom isomorphism and since the Thom isomorphism is an isomorphism, uniqueness of inverses gives that
\psi_{\Xi,P}\otimes_{C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)}[\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}]
is the inverse and therefore
\psi_{\Xi,P}\otimes_{C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)}[\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi}]=[\not{D}_{\Xi}]
.
∎

Theorem 0.14.6
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups with fibre
\mathsf{G}
and frame bundle
P
. Assume that
\mathsf{G}
admits flat orbits. Then
I_{\mathsf{G},P}
is a continuous trace algebra with spectrum
\Gamma_{P}
and vanishing Dixmier-Duoady invariant.

In particular, there exists a bundle of Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{P}
and a
C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})
-linear
*
-isomorphism
\pi_{\musFlat}:I_{\mathsf{G},P}\to C_{0}(\Gamma_{P},\mathbb{K}(\mathcal{H}))
. This choice is unique up to isomorphism of the Hilbert space bundle and tensoring by a line bundle.

Proof.

We construct a cover
\mathfrak{U}
of
\Gamma_{P}
by covering
M
by open subsets
(U_{j})_{j}
over which
\mathcal{G}
trivializes and setting
\mathfrak{U}
to be the image of
(U_{j}\times U_{\boldsymbol{B}})_{j,\boldsymbol{B}}
in said trivializations. Over each
U_{j}\times U_{\boldsymbol{B}}
we can write

I_{\mathsf{G},P}|_{U_{j}\times U_{\boldsymbol{B}}}\cong C_{0}(U_{j}\times U_{\boldsymbol{B}},\mathbb{K}(\mathcal{H}_{\boldsymbol{B},{\rm adb}}|_{t=1})),

and the transitition cocycle lifts to a cochain of adiabatic Lion intertwiners as in Proposition
0.14.2
restricted to
t=1
. The same construction applies ad verbatim to
C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega)
with adiabatic Lion intertwiners restricted to
t=0
. By Proposition
0.14.2
,
\delta_{\rm DD}(I_{\mathsf{G},P})
and
\delta_{\rm DD}(C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega))
are represented by the same
U(1)
-cocycle. But
\delta_{\rm DD}(C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega))=0
by Proposition
0.14.4
.
∎

Proposition 0.14.7
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups
\mathsf{G}
that admits flat orbits. Let
g_{\Xi}
be a fixed metric on the Lie algebroid of
\mathcal{G}
. Assume that
\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{P}
is a bundle of Hilbert spaces and
\pi_{\musFlat}:I_{\mathsf{G},P}\to C_{0}(\Gamma_{P},\mathbb{K}(\mathcal{H}))
a
C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})
-linear
*
-isomorphism. Then there exists a uniquely determined line bundle

\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})\to\Gamma_{P},

that we call the metaplectic correction bundle, satisfying that
\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})
extends to a Hilbert space bundle
\mathcal{H}_{\rm mod}\to\Gamma_{P}\times[0,1]
and
\pi_{\musFlat}
extends to a
C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}\times[0,1])
-linear
*
-isomorphism

\pi_{\rm mod}:C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z},{\rm adb}}|_{\Gamma_{P}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}}|_{s=1})\to C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}\times[0,1],\mathbb{K}(\mathcal{H}_{\rm mod})),

and satisfying

\mathcal{H}_{\rm mod}|_{t=0}\cong\mathpzc{F}_{\Xi},

as
(C^{*}(\Xi_{P}^{*},\omega),C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}))
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules.

Proof.

By the argument in Theorem
0.14.6
,
C^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Z},{\rm adb}}|_{\Gamma_{P}},\omega_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{Z},{\rm adb}}}|_{s=1})
is a continuous trace algebra with spectrum
\Gamma_{P}\times[0,1]
and trivial Dixmier-Duoady class. The proposition now follows from Lemma
0.14.3
.
∎

Remark 0.14.8
.

In light of Lemma
0.14.3
, Proposition
0.14.7
implies that for any two bundles of Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\Gamma_{P}
with equipped with
C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})
-linear
*
-isomorphisms
\pi_{j}:I_{\mathsf{G},P}\to C_{0}(\Gamma_{P},\mathbb{K}(\mathcal{H}))
,
j=1,2
, there is an isomorphism of Hilbert space bundles

\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H}_{1})\cong\mathcal{H}_{2}\otimes\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H}_{2}),

compatible with the action of
I_{\mathsf{G},P}
.

The reader should also note that
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})\to\Gamma_{P}
depends on
\pi_{\musFlat}
. This follows from Kuiper’s theorem allowing a reduction to the case that
\mathcal{H}
is trivial.

Let us describe the metaplectic correction line bundle for a particular choice of bundle of flat orbit representations.

Lemma 0.14.9
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups with fibre
\mathsf{G}
and frame bundle
P
. Assume that
\mathsf{G}
admits flat orbits. Consider the bundle of flat orbit representations

\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{P},

defined from gluing together along the corrected Lion intertwiners defined in small enough local trivializations as in Proposition
0.8.5
and
0.12.5
. Then it holds that

c_{1}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H}))-c_{1}(\det(\Xi^{*}_{P}))\in\mathrm{im}(H^{1}(\Gamma_{P},\mathbb{Z}/8)\to H^{2}(\Gamma_{P},\mathbb{Z})),

is in the image of the Bockstein map associated with multiplication by
8
. In particular, the image of
c_{1}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H}))
in
H^{2}(\Gamma_{P},\mathbb{R})
coincides with that of
c_{1}(\det(\Xi^{*}_{P}))
.

Proof.

The reader should note that
\Xi_{P}
carries an essentially unique complex structure adapted to the Kirillov form, and
\det(\Xi^{*}_{P})\equiv\wedge_{\mathbb{C}}^{d}\Xi^{*}_{P}\to\Gamma_{P}
is a well defined line bundle (for
d=\mathrm{rk}(\Xi_{P})=\mathrm{codim}(\mathfrak{z})/2
). To prove the lemma we must show that there are transition functions for
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})
and
\det(\Xi^{*}_{P})
that coincides up to powers of
\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\pi i}{4}}
. We can construct
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})
as the line bundle
L_{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}
from Proposition
0.14.4
where
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\to\Gamma_{P}
is the
\omega
-twisted representation of
\Xi_{P}^{*}
constructed from gluing together along the corrected Lion intertwiners defined in small enough local trivializations as in Proposition
0.8.5
. We can therefore construct
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})
explicitly from the
U(d)
-frame bundle on
\Xi_{P}
and the associated corrected Lion transforms (cf. Proposition
0.4.5
).

Fix a transition
U(d)
-valued Cech cocycle
(g_{ij})_{i,j}
for
\Xi_{P}
. By the structure of the metaplectic representation of
U(d)
on the Fock bundle (see
[
71
, Chapter 4]
), the Fock bundle is constructed from the Lion intertwiner corrected by transition functions of the form
(\pm\sqrt{\det(g_{ij}^{*})})_{i,j}
. By Remark
0.4.4
, the bundle
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
is constructed from the Lion intertwiner corrected by transition functions of the form
(\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\pi i}{4}k_{0,i,j}}\sqrt{\det(g_{ij}^{*})})_{i,j}
for some
k_{0,i,j}\in\mathbb{Z}
. The transition functions of
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})
therefore take the form
(\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\pi i}{4}k_{i,j}}\det(g_{ij}^{*}))_{i,j}
for some
k_{i,j}\in\mathbb{Z}
. The cocycle
(\det(g_{ij}^{*}))_{i,j}
is a transition cocycle for
\det(\Xi^{*}_{P})
so the lemma follows.
∎

Proposition 0.14.10
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups
\mathsf{G}
that admits flat orbits. Let
g_{\Xi}
be a fixed metric on the Lie algebroid of
\mathcal{G}
. Assume that
\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{P}
is a bundle of Hilbert spaces and
\pi_{\musFlat}:I_{\mathsf{G},P}\to C_{0}(\Gamma_{P},\mathbb{K}(\mathcal{H}))
a
C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})
-linear
*
-isomorphism.
The class
[\mathcal{H}]\in KK_{0}(I_{\mathsf{G},P},C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}))
fits into a commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\Xi_{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\mathsf{G},P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\not{D}_{\Xi}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{I,P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathcal{H}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})^{*}]}

where
\psi_{I,P}\in KK_{0}(I_{\mathsf{G},P},C_{0}(\Xi_{P}))
is the deformation morphism induced from
\psi_{I}
defined in Definition
0.13.6
and
[\not{D}_{\Xi}]\in KK_{0}(C_{0}(\Xi_{P}),C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}))
is the dual Thom class.

Proof.

The proposition follows from Theorem
0.14.5
and Proposition
0.14.7
.
∎

We summarize our constructions in the following theorem:

Theorem 0.14.11
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\to M
be a locally trivial bundle of nilpotent Lie groups
\mathsf{G}
that admits flat orbits. Let
g_{\Xi}
be a fixed metric on the Lie algebroid of
\mathcal{G}
. Assume that
\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{P}
is a bundle of Hilbert spaces and
\pi_{\musFlat}:I_{\mathsf{G},P}\to C_{0}(\Gamma_{P},\mathbb{K}(\mathcal{H}))
a
C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})
-linear
*
-isomorphism. The element
[\mathcal{H}]\in KK(I_{G,P},C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}))
, the Thom isomorphism
\tau_{\Xi_{P}}\in KK(C_{0}(\Gamma_{P}),C_{0}(\Xi_{P}))
(defined from the complex structure induced from the metric), and the metaplectic correction bundle
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})
(see Proposition
0.14.7
) fits into a commuting diagram in
KK
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{G,P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\Xi_{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\mathbf{g}^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathcal{H}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\Xi_{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{I,P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{P}}

Proof.

The right square commutes by Theorem
0.13.9
. The left triangle commutes by Proposition
0.14.10
using the fact that the inverse of the Thom class is the fibreswise Dirac operator.
∎

Combining Theorem
0.13.10
with Theorem
0.14.11
we arrive at the following.

Corollary 0.14.12
.

Under the same assumptions and notations as in Theorem
0.14.11
, and
j:\Gamma_{P}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{g}^{*}
denoting the inclusion, the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\mathsf{G},P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\Gamma_{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}(\mathbf{g}^{*}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{P}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{!}}

and the vertical maps are
KK
-isomorphisms and the horizontal maps are surjective on
K
-theory.

Part III
Carnot manifolds and associated groupoids

Introduction to part 3

At last, we have reached the point in the work where we see some geometry. The underlying geometric object of study in this monograph is Carnot manifolds. We shall at first explore their general properties and then explore some prototypical examples. Until that point, the reader is encouraged to keep contact manifolds and filtrations in mind. This section contains no novel results, but is included for context. The contents of this part is organized into the three sections:

•

Section
0.15
where we recall the geometry of Carnot manifolds. The main object we make use of is the osculating Lie groupoid
T_{H}X\to X
. An important notion we introduce here is that of
\boldsymbol{F}
-regularity, when the osculating Lie groupoid has (up to isomorphism) constant fibre with flat coadjoint orbits.

•

Section
0.16
where the reader can find several examples of Carnot manifolds. For instance, contact manifolds, polycontact manifolds, pluricontact manifolds, equiregular differential systems, and parabolic geometries.

•

Section
0.17
where we recall the construction of the parabolic tangent groupoid from
[
149
]
. Here we also recall the construction of the adiabatic parabolic tangent groupoid and equipp
\Xi_{X}
and
\Gamma_{X}
with spin
c
-structures.

0.15.
Carnot manifolds

Definition 0.15.1
.

A Carnot manifold
(X,\mathcal{F})
is a smooth manifold
X
equipped with a
filtration
\mathcal{F}
of the tangent bundle
TX
by smooth subbundles

\mathcal{F}:0=T^{0}X\subset T^{-1}X\subset T^{-2}X\ldots\subset T^{-r+1}X\subset T^{-r}X=TX,

(31)

such that all inclusions are strict and for any vector fields
Y\in C^{\infty}(X,T^{i}X)
,
Y^{\prime}\in C^{\infty}(X,T^{j}X)
we have that
[Y,Y^{\prime}]\in C^{\infty}(X,T^{i+j}X)
. When the filtration is understood from context, we simply write
X
for the Carnot manifold. We call
r
the depth of the filtration.

A morphism
\phi:(X_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1})\to(X_{2},\mathcal{F}_{2})
of two Carnot manifolds is a smooth mapping
\phi:X_{1}\to X_{2}
such that
D\phi
preserves the filtration. An isomorphism of Carnot manifolds is a morphism
\phi:(X_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1})\to(X_{2},\mathcal{F}_{2})
such that
\phi:X_{1}\to X_{2}
is a diffeomorphism.

Remark 0.15.2
.

In
[
115
,
136
,
149
,
150
]
, a Carnot manifold is called a filtered manifold. In
[
108
]
, a filtration as in (
31
) appears with a slightly different grading and is there called a Lie filtration. We have chosen the term Carnot manifold in accordance with
[
35
,
46
,
47
,
112
]
.

Given a Carnot manifold
(X,\mathcal{F})
, define
\mathsf{gr}_{i}(TX):=T^{i}X/T^{i+1}X
and let
q_{i}:T^{i}X\rightarrow\mathsf{gr}_{i}(TX)
denote the natural quotient map. We consider the graded vector bundle:

\mathsf{gr}(TX):=\bigoplus_{i=-r}^{-1}\mathsf{gr}_{i}(TX)=\bigoplus_{i=-r}^{-1}T^{i}X/T^{i+1}X.

For every
i\in 1,\ldots,r
one has an exact sequence

0\rightarrow T^{i+1}X\rightarrow T^{i}X\rightarrow\mathsf{gr}_{i}(TX)\rightarrow 0,

and a choice of splitting of this sequence gives rise to a vector bundle isomorphism
T^{i}X\simeq T^{i+1}X\oplus\mathsf{gr}_{i}X
. Thus there exists a non-canonical vector bundle isomorphism
TX\simeq\mathsf{gr}(TX)
.

Using the property that the Lie bracket on vector fields respect the filtration, one equips
\mathsf{gr}(TX)
with a non-trivial graded Lie algebroid structure, which differs from the standard Lie algebroid structure of
TX
. By definition, a Carnot manifold
(X,\mathcal{F})
induces a Lie algebra filtation of
C^{\infty}(X,TX)
given by

\displaystyle 0=C^{\infty}(X,T^{0}X)

\displaystyle\subset C^{\infty}(X,T^{-1}X)\subset\ldots

(32)

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\subset C^{\infty}(X,T^{-r}X)=C^{\infty}(X,TX).

We note that for
Y\in C^{\infty}(X,T^{i}X)
,
Y^{\prime}\in C^{\infty}(X,T^{j}X)
and
f,g\in C^{\infty}(X)
one has

[fY,gY^{\prime}]=fg[Y,Y^{\prime}]+f(Yg)Y^{\prime}-g(Y^{\prime}f)Y=fg[Y,Y^{\prime}]+\Gamma^{\infty}(T^{i+j+1}X).

In particular, the Lie bracket on vector fields induces a
C^{\infty}(X)
-linear map

\mathcal{L}:C^{\infty}(X,\mathsf{gr}(TX))\times C^{\infty}(X,\mathsf{gr}(TX))\to C^{\infty}(X,\mathsf{gr}(TX)),\\

(33)

defined on
Y\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathsf{gr}_{i}(TX))=C^{\infty}(X,T^{i}X)/C^{\infty}(X,T^{i+1}X)
and
