\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}:=\{k\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(\mathsf{G})\cap C^{\infty}(\mathsf{G}\setminus\{0\},|\Lambda|):\lambda_{*}k-\lambda^{m}k\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathsf{G})\}.

The spaces
\Sigma^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}
and
\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}
are equipped with associative products defined from convolution

\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}\times\tilde{\Sigma}^{m^{\prime}}_{\mathsf{G}}\to\tilde{\Sigma}^{m+m^{\prime}}_{\mathsf{G}},

\Sigma^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}\times\Sigma^{m^{\prime}}_{\mathsf{G}}\to\Sigma^{m+m^{\prime}}_{\mathsf{G}}.

Using the ideas underlying Proposition
0.18.7
, we arrive at a short exact sequence of Frechet
*
-spaces

0\to C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathsf{G},|\Lambda|)\to\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}\xrightarrow{\rho_{m}}\Sigma^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}\to 0.

(47)

The projection
\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}\to\Sigma^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}
admits a linear splitting by choosing a cut off function around
0\in\mathsf{G}
. Moreover,

\rho_{m+m^{\prime}}(aa^{\prime})=\rho_{m}(a)\rho_{m^{\prime}}(a^{\prime})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\rho_{m}(a)^{*}=\rho_{m}(a^{*}),

for
a\in\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}
,
a^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Sigma}^{m^{\prime}}_{\mathsf{G}}
. Pushing forward along graded automorphisms defines continuous actions

\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathsf{G})\times\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}\to\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{\mathsf{G}},

\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathsf{G})\times\Sigma^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}\to\Sigma^{m}_{\mathsf{G}},

compatible with products, adjoints and the quotient map
\rho_{m}
. The following observation is a direct consequence of local triviality of a regular Carnot manifold. Recall the definition of
\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{H}
from Equation (
37
).

Proposition 0.20.10
.

Let
X
be a regular Carnot manifold of type
\mathfrak{g}
with graded frame bundle
P_{X}\to X
. Define the locally trivial bundles of Frechet
*
-spaces

\Sigma_{X}^{m}:=P_{X}\times_{\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathsf{G})}\Sigma^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}\to X,

\tilde{\Sigma}_{X}^{m}:=P_{X}\times_{\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathsf{G})}\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}\to X.

Then there are natural isomorphisms

\Sigma^{m}_{H}(X;\mathbb{C})\cong C^{\infty}(X;\Sigma^{m}_{X}),

\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{H}(X;\mathbb{C})\cong C^{\infty}(X;\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{X}),

compatible with products, adjoints and the quotient map
\rho_{m}
.

Remark 0.20.11
.

The situation for symbols of operators acting between vector bundles is quite similar. Let
E_{1},E_{2}\to X
be complex vector bundles. Let
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
denote the fibres of
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
, respectively. The frame bundle
P_{j}\to X
of
E_{j}
is a principal
GL(V_{j})
-bundle (that can be reduced to
U(V_{j})
given a hermitean structure). Writing
P_{X,E_{1},E_{2}}:=P_{X}\times_{X}P_{1}\times_{X}P_{2}
, we have that
\Sigma^{m}_{H}(X;E_{1},E_{2})
coincides with the sections of the bundle

\Sigma_{X}^{m}\otimes\mathrm{Hom}(E_{1},E_{2})=P_{X,E_{1},E_{2}}\times_{\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathsf{G})\times\mathrm{GL}(V)\times\mathrm{GL}(W)}(\Sigma^{m}_{\mathsf{G}}\otimes\mathrm{Hom}(V,W)).

Remark 0.20.12
.

In light of the global picture from Proposition
0.20.10
, we note that if
\mathbb{D}\in\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{m}_{H}(X;E_{1},E_{2})
then
\rho_{m}:\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{H}(X;E_{1},E_{2})\to\Sigma^{m}_{H}(X;E_{1},E_{2})
fits together with the symbol mapping via the identity

\rho_{m}\circ\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}(\mathbb{D})=\sigma_{H}\circ\mathrm{ev}_{t=1}(\mathbb{D}).

Let us now consider the situation when there are flat orbits. For the remainder of this section we consider an
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold of type
\mathfrak{g}
. Recall the notation
\Gamma
for the set of flat orbits of
\mathsf{G}
and
\Gamma_{X}:=P_{X}\times_{\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathsf{G})}\Gamma
for the bundle of flat orbits of
T_{H}X
. We fix a bundle of flat representations
\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{X}
as in Theorem
0.14.6
. Recall that there is a
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-linear
*
-isomorphism
\pi_{\musFlat}:I_{X}\to C_{0}(\Gamma_{X},\mathbb{K}(\mathcal{H}))
and the choice of Hilbert space bundle
\mathcal{H}
is unique up to isomorphism and tensoring by a line bundle. We introduce the notation

\mathpzc{H}:=C_{0}(\Gamma_{X},\mathcal{H}),

for the associated
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-module and remark that
I_{X}\cong\mathbb{K}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}(\mathpzc{H})
. We write
\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathcal{H})\to\Gamma_{P}
for the locally trivial bundle of Frechet spaces (with fibre
\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for
d=\mathrm{codim}(\mathfrak{z})/2
) defined from
\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathcal{H})_{\xi}=\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathcal{H}_{\xi})
. Indeed, by the considerations of Section
0.5
,
\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathcal{H})
is well defined. Since the trivial representation is not flat, the inclusion
\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathcal{H})\subseteq\mathcal{H}
is fibrewise norm dense. We similarly define the dense
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-submodule

\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathpzc{H}):=C_{0}(\Gamma_{X},\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathcal{H}))\subseteq\mathpzc{H}.

An operator
T
on
\mathpzc{H}
is said to be locally
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-compact if
\varphi T\in\mathbb{K}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}(\mathpzc{H})
for any
\varphi\in C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
.

Proposition 0.20.13
.

Let
X
be a compact
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold. Any
a\in\Sigma_{H}^{m}(X;\mathbb{C})
defines a densely defined
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-linear semiregular operator

\pi_{\musFlat}(a):\mathpzc{H}\dashrightarrow\mathpzc{H},

defined on
\mathrm{dom}(\pi(a)):=\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathpzc{H})
by

(\pi_{\musFlat}(a)v)_{\xi}:=\pi_{\musFlat,\xi}(a)v_{\xi}.

If
m\leq 0
, then
\pi_{\musFlat}(a)
is bounded, adjointable and
\pi_{\musFlat}(a)^{*}=\overline{\pi_{\musFlat}(a^{*})}
. In particular,
\pi_{\musFlat}
defines a faithful
*
-homomorphism

\pi_{\musFlat}:\Sigma^{m}_{H}(X,\mathbb{C})\to\mathrm{End}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\mathpzc{H}),

for any
m\leq 0
. If
m<0
, then
\pi_{\musFlat}(a)
is locally
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-compact.

Proof.

The operator
\pi_{\musFlat}(a)
preserves
\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathpzc{H})
and on there, the formal adjoint of
\pi_{\musFlat}(a)
is
\pi_{\musFlat}(a^{*})
. Therefore
\pi_{\musFlat}(a)
is semi-regular. It is clear that
\pi_{\musFlat}(ab)=\pi_{\musFlat}(a)\pi_{\musFlat}(b)
on
\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathpzc{H})
.

Assume now that
m\leq 0
. By order reduction it suffices to consider
m=0
. If suffices to prove that
\pi_{\musFlat}(a)
is bounded; it then follows from the properties in the preceding parapraph that
\pi_{\musFlat}(a)
is adjointable and
\pi_{\musFlat}(a)^{*}=\overline{\pi_{\musFlat}(a^{*})}
. As in the proof of Proposition
0.19.3
, we use
[
66
, Theorem 8.18]
to conclude that there is a constant
C>0
such that
a
is norm-bounded
L^{2}(T_{H}X_{x})\to L^{2}(T_{H}X_{x})
by
C
for any
x
. Therefore,
a
defines an element of
\mathcal{M}(C^{*}(T_{H}X_{x}))
and will therefore be bounded on
\mathpzc{H}_{x}
by
C
for any
x
.

Finally, for
m<0
, we note that
a
induces a
C^{*}(T_{H}X)
-compact multiplier on
\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}(X;\mathbb{C})\otimes_{\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}}C^{*}(T_{H}X)
by Proposition
0.20.2
. In particular,
a
acts as an element of
C^{*}(T_{H}X)
and the proposition follows from the fact that the
C^{*}(T_{H}X)
-action on
\mathpzc{H}
is
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-locally compact.
∎

Proposition 0.20.14
.

Let
X
be an
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold. Any
a\in\tilde{\Sigma}_{H}^{m}(X;\mathbb{C})
defines a densely defined
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-linear semiregular operator

\tilde{\pi}_{\musFlat}(a):\mathpzc{H}\dashrightarrow\mathpzc{H},

defined on
\mathrm{dom}(\tilde{\pi}_{\musFlat}(a)):=\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathpzc{H})
by

(\tilde{\pi}_{\musFlat}(a)v)_{\xi}:=\pi_{\musFlat,\xi}(a)v_{\xi}.

If
m\leq 0
, then
\tilde{\pi}_{\musFlat}(a)
is bounded, adjointable and
\tilde{\pi}_{\musFlat}(a)^{*}=\overline{\tilde{\pi}_{\musFlat}(a)}
. In particular,
\tilde{\pi}_{\musFlat}
defines a faithful
*
-homomorphism

\tilde{\pi}_{\musFlat}:\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{H}(X,\mathbb{C})\to\mathrm{End}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\mathpzc{H}),

for any
m\leq 0
. If
m<0
, then
\tilde{\pi}_{\musFlat}(a)
is locally
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-compact.

The proof of Proposition
0.20.14
goes along the same lines as the proof of Proposition
0.20.13
and is omitted.

Remark 0.20.15
.

We remark that the representations of Proposition
0.20.13
and
0.20.14
are not compatible with the quotient mapping
\rho_{m}
, i.e
\tilde{\pi}_{\musFlat}(a)\neq\pi_{\musFlat}(\rho_{m}(a))
in general. Indeed, the situation should be understood in analogy with the short exact sequence

0\to C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\to C(\overline{B}_{n})\to C(S^{n-1})\to 0,

defining the radial compactification of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
(by identifying
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with the open unit ball
B_{n}
). In this case,
C(\overline{B}_{n})
and
C(S^{n-1})
both acts on
C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{0\})
, the latter by extending homogeneously.

Definition 0.20.16
.

Let
X
be an
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold. We fix a
\mathfrak{D}\in\mathcal{DO}_{H}^{2m}(X;\mathbb{C})
as in Proposition
0.19.15
. For
s\geq 0
we define

\mathcal{H}^{s}:=\sigma_{H}(\mathfrak{D}^{-s/2m})\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{X},

viewed as a the Hilbert space bundle by declaring
\sigma_{H}(\mathfrak{D}^{-s/2m}):\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}^{s}
to be a unitary isomorphism. We let
\mathpzc{H}^{s}
denote the associated
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-module. For
s<0
we define

\mathcal{H}^{s}:=\sigma_{H}(\mathfrak{D}^{-s/2m})\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{X},

and the associated
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-module
\mathpzc{H}^{s}
, by duality. We also introduce the notation

\mathpzc{H}^{s}(E):=\mathpzc{H}^{s}\otimes_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}C_{0}(\Gamma_{X};E),

for a vector bundle
E\to X
. If
\boldsymbol{E}\to X
is a hermitean graded vector bundle, we set

\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}(\boldsymbol{E}):=\bigoplus_{j}\mathpzc{H}^{s-j}(\boldsymbol{E}[j]).

Proposition 0.20.17
.

Let
X
be an
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold,
\boldsymbol{E}\to X
a graded hermitean vector bundle and
\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E})
the
C^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{H}X)
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-module from Section
0.20
. Consider
\mathpzc{H}
as a
(C^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{H}X),C_{0}(\Gamma_{X}))
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-module via restriction to
t=0
. Then there is a canonical isomorphism of
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules

\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}(\boldsymbol{E})\cong\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E})\otimes_{C^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{H}X)}\mathpzc{H}.

Conversely, the localization of
\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E})|_{t=0}
to
\Gamma_{X}
along the natural map
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})\to\mathcal{ZM}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
, is canonically isomorphic to
\mathbb{K}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}(\mathpzc{H},\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}(\boldsymbol{E}))
as right
I_{X}
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules.

Proof.

The statement is readily verified in local coordinates and globalizes due to the regularity.
∎

Proposition 0.20.18
.

Let
X
be a regular Carnot manifold admitting flat orbits and
\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\to X
two graded, hermitean vector bundles. Any
a\in\Sigma^{m}_{H,{\rm gr}}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\boldsymbol{E}_{2})
defines a bounded densely defined
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-linear operator
\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1})\dashrightarrow\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2})
defined on
\mathrm{dom}(\pi_{\musFlat,s}(a)):=\mathcal{S}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}))
by

(\pi(a)_{\musFlat,s}v)_{\xi}:=\pi_{\musFlat,s,\xi}(a)v_{\xi}.

The closure of
\pi_{\musFlat,s}(a)
is an adjointable operator

\pi_{\musFlat,s}(a):\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1})\to\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2}).

Moreover, we obtain a mapping

\pi_{\musFlat,s}:\Sigma_{H,{\rm gr}}^{m}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\boldsymbol{E}_{2})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}),\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2})),

which respects products in the sense that for
a\in\Sigma_{H,{\rm gr}}^{m}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{2},\boldsymbol{E}_{3})
and
a^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{H,{\rm gr}}^{m^{\prime}}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\boldsymbol{E}_{2})
we have that

\pi_{\musFlat,s-m}(a^{\prime})\pi_{\musFlat,s}(a)=\pi_{\musFlat,s}(a^{\prime}a)\in\mathrm{Hom}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}),\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m-m^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{E}_{3}).

Proof.

Using order reductions with
\Lambda_{\boldsymbol{E},s}
as in the proof of Proposition
0.20.3
, we can reduce to the case that
s=0
and
\boldsymbol{E}_{1}
and
\boldsymbol{E}_{2}
are trivially graded. Further order reductions reduce to
m=0
. Now the Proposition follows from Proposition
0.20.14
.
∎

0.20.2.
Characterizing
H
-ellipticity in the flat orbits

We now restrict to the transveral
\Gamma^{\partial}_{X}:=P_{X}\times_{\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathsf{G})}\Gamma_{\partial}
for the dilation action (see more in Proposition
0.7.6
). By Proposition
0.7.6
,
\Gamma^{\partial}_{X}
is a smooth hypersurface in
\Gamma_{X}
, in general
\Gamma^{\partial}_{X}
is not compact. We introduce the subscript
\partial
on modules and bundles to indicate restriction to
\Gamma^{\partial}_{X}
. For instance:

\mathpzc{H}^{s}_{\partial}(E):=\mathpzc{H}^{s}(E)\otimes_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}C_{0}(\Gamma^{\partial}_{X}),

where the tensor product is over the
*
-homomorphism
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})\to C_{0}(\Gamma^{\partial}_{X})
is defined from restriction along the inclusion
\Gamma^{\partial}_{X}\subseteq\Gamma_{X}
.

Definition 0.20.19
.

Let
X
be an
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold and
E_{1},E_{2}\to X
two vector bundles. We say that
a\in\Sigma_{H}^{m}(X;E_{1},E_{2})
is
a uniformly
H
-elliptic symbol on flat orbits
if for some
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
\pi_{\musFlat,s}(a):\mathpzc{H}^{s}_{\partial}(E_{1})\to\mathpzc{H}^{s-m}_{\partial}(E_{2})
is invertible as a bounded adjointable
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-linear operator.

Theorem 0.20.20
.

Let
X
be an
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold,
E_{1},E_{2}\to X
two vector bundles and
D\in\Psi_{H}^{m}(X;E_{1},E_{2})
. Then the following are equivalent:

i)

D
is
H
-elliptic.

ii)

\sigma_{H}(D)
is uniformly
H
-elliptic symbol on flat orbits.

iii)

\pi_{\musFlat,s}(\sigma_{H}(D)):\mathpzc{H}^{s}_{\partial}(E_{1})\to\mathpzc{H}^{s-m}_{\partial}(E_{2})
is invertible as a bounded adjointable
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X}^{\partial})
-linear operator for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

It is clear that iii)
\Rightarrow
ii). It follows from Lemma
0.19.6
that i)
\Rightarrow
iii). It remains to prove that ii)
\Rightarrow
i). After replacing
D
with
\mathfrak{D}_{E_{2}}^{(s-m)/2m_{2}}D\mathfrak{D}_{E_{1}}^{s/2m_{1}}
and doubling up the vector bundle, we can assume that
D\in\Psi_{H}^{0}(X;E)
and that
\sigma_{H}(D):\mathpzc{H}_{\partial}(E)\to\mathpzc{H}_{\partial}(E)
is invertible as a bounded adjointable
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-linear operator. It follows from Proposition
0.20.13
that
\sigma_{H}(D)
admits an inverse in the
C^{*}
-algebra closure of
\Sigma^{0}_{H}(X;E)
inside
\mathrm{End}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\mathpzc{H}(E))
. Since the flat orbits are dense in the representation space, i.e.
C^{*}(T_{H}X)\to\mathcal{M}(I_{X})
is faithful, the isomorphism
\mathrm{End}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\mathpzc{H}(E))\cong\mathcal{M}(I_{X})
ensures that
\sigma_{(x,\pi)}^{0}(D)
is invertible for all
x\in X
and all non-trivial irreducible representations. As such,
D
is
H
-elliptic by Lemma
0.19.6
.
∎

Let us give an example of how to apply Theorem
0.20.20
. Recall the setting and notation of Theorem
0.19.11
.

Proposition 0.20.21
.

Let
X
be a compact regular polycontact manifold. Let
H=T^{-1}X
denote the polycontact structure and assume it is equipped with a Riemannian metric
g
. Take
D_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{DO}_{H}^{2}(X;E)
as in Equation (
43
). Let
\omega
denote the Kirillov form and take
s_{k}(g_{\omega})
as in (
44
). Consider the family of endomorphisms

\gamma_{k}:=s_{k}(g_{\omega})+\frac{\mathrm{Tr}(|\omega|)}{2}+\gamma:p_{\Gamma}^{*}H^{\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}^{\rm sym}k}\otimes E\to p_{\Gamma}^{*}H_{X}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}^{\rm sym}k}\otimes E.

Then
D_{\gamma}
is
H
-elliptic if and only if each
\gamma_{k}
is an automorphism.

Proof.

By Theorem
0.20.20
, it suffices to prove uniform invertibility of
\sigma_{H}^{2}(D_{\gamma})\sigma^{2}_{H}(\Delta_{H})^{-1}
. Using the computations in the proof of Theorem
0.19.11
, we see that
\sigma^{2}_{H}(\Delta_{H})
acts on
\mathpzc{F}_{X}
as
s_{k}(g_{\omega})+\frac{\mathrm{Tr}(|\omega|)}{2}
. A rather naive estimate shows that

C_{1}(1+k)|\xi|\leq\left\|s_{k}(g_{\omega})+\frac{\mathrm{Tr}(|\omega|)}{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{End}(p_{\Gamma}^{*}H_{\xi}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}^{\rm sym}k})}\leq C_{2}(1+k)|\xi|.

We conclude that uniform invertibility of
\sigma_{H}^{2}(D_{\gamma})\sigma^{2}_{H}(\Delta_{H})^{-1}
is equivalent to each
\gamma_{k}
being an automorphism and there being a norm estimate
\|\gamma_{k}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim(1+k)^{-1}
. However, by compactness of
S(H^{\perp})
the norm estimate is immediate.
∎

0.21.
K
-theoretical invariants of
H
-elliptic operators

In this section we shall define a number of different
K
-theoretical invariants associated with an
H
-elliptic operator. These invariants, and their internal relations, will be put to use below in Part
V
. For a hermitean vector bundle
E\to X
, we introduce the notation
C^{*}(T_{H}X;E)
for the closure of
C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X;r^{*}E)
as a
C^{*}(T_{H}X)
-module, note that
C^{*}(T_{H}X;E)=\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}(X;E)|_{t=0}
.

Definition 0.21.1
.

Let
X
be a compact Carnot manifold with a fixed volume density,
E_{1},E_{2}\to X
hermitean vector bundles and
D\in\Psi^{m}_{H}(X;E_{1},E_{2})
an
H
-elliptic operator with
m>0
.

•

The even
K
-homology class
[D]\in KK_{0}(C(X),\mathbb{C})
of
D
is defined as the class of the even higher order spectral triple
(L^{2}(X;E_{1}\oplus E_{2}),\tilde{D})
,
where

\tilde{D}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&D\\
D^{*}&0\end{pmatrix}.

•

The even
K
-theory class
[\sigma_{H}(D)]\in KK_{0}(\mathbb{C},C^{*}(T_{H}X))
is defined as the class of the even higher order unbounded Kasparov module
(C^{*}(T_{H}X;E_{1}\oplus E_{2}),a)
where
a\in\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{H}(X;E_{1}\oplus E_{2})
is any lift of
\sigma_{H}(\tilde{D})
.

•

The even
KK
-class
[\mathbb{D}]\in KK_{0}^{[0,\infty)}(C_{0}([0,\infty)\times X),C^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{H}X))
is defined as the class of the even higher order unbounded Kasparov module
(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}(X;E_{1}\oplus E_{2}),\tilde{\mathbb{D}})
for some
\mathbb{D}\in\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{m}_{H}(X;E_{1},E_{2})
lifting
D
.

If additionally
E:=E_{1}=E_{2}
and
D=D^{*}
,

•

The odd
K
-homology class
[D]\in KK_{1}(C(X),\mathbb{C})
of
D
is defined as the class of the odd higher order spectral triple
(L^{2}(X),D)
.

•

The odd
K
-theory class
[\sigma_{H}(D)]\in KK_{1}(\mathbb{C},C^{*}(T_{H}X))
is defined as the class of the odd higher order unbounded Kasparov module
(C^{*}(T_{H}X;E),a)
where
a\in\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{H}(X;E)
is any lift of
\sigma_{H}(D)
.

•

The odd
KK
-class
[\mathbb{D}]\in KK_{1}^{[0,\infty)}(C_{0}([0,\infty)\times X),C^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{H}X))
is defined as the class of the odd higher order unbounded Kasparov module
(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}(X;E),\mathbb{D})
for some self-adjoint
\mathbb{D}\in\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{m}_{H}(X;E)
lifting
D
.

Remark 0.21.2
.

The classes in Definition
0.21.1
are well defined due to Corollary
0.20.8
. Using Proposition
0.18.3
and Lemma
0.20.2
, it is readily verified that the classes
[D]\in KK_{*}(C(X),\mathbb{C})
and
[\mathbb{D}]\in KK_{*}^{[0,\infty)}(C_{0}([0,\infty)\times X),C^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{H}X))
are determined by the principal symbol
\sigma_{H}(D)
, i.e.
[D]=[D^{\prime}]
and
[\mathbb{D}]=[\mathbb{D}^{\prime}]
as soon as
\sigma_{H}(D)=\sigma_{H}(D^{\prime})
.

Remark 0.21.3
.

For an
H
-elliptic operator
D\in\Psi^{m}_{H}(X;E_{1},E_{2})
for
m\leq 0
, the classes
[D]
,
[\sigma_{H}(D)]
and
[\mathbb{D}]
are defined similarly after increasing the order to being positive by conjugating by suitable powers of an invertible positive Heisenberg operator. It is readily verified that the choice of invertible positive Heisenberg operator does not affect the class.

Proposition 0.21.4
.

Let
X
be a compact Carnot manifold with a fixed volume density, and
D
an
H
-elliptic operator in the Heisenberg calculus. The classes
[D]
,
[\sigma_{H}(D)]
and
[\mathbb{D}]
are related by

[D]=[\mathbb{D}]\otimes[\mathrm{ev}_{t=1}],\quad\mbox{and}\quad[\sigma_{H}(D)]=[i^{*}]\otimes[\mathbb{D}]\otimes[\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}],

where
i:\mathbb{C}\to C(X)
is the inclusion of the unit.

Proof.

The identity
[D]=[\mathbb{D}]\otimes[\mathrm{ev}_{t=1}]
follows from the construction, cf. Corollary
0.20.8
. The identity
[\sigma_{H}(D)]=[i^{*}]\otimes[\mathbb{D}]\otimes[\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}]
follows from that
\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}(\mathbb{D})\in\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{H}
is a lift of
\sigma_{H}(D)
.
∎

Remark 0.21.5
.

Let
X
be a compact Carnot manifold with a fixed volume density, and
F\in\Psi^{0}_{H}(X;E)
a self-adjoint
H
-elliptic operator in the Heisenberg calculus which is unitary up to
\Psi^{-1}_{H}(X;E)
. Following Proposition
0.18.7
, we have a short exact sequence of Frechet
*
-algebras

0\to C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X,\mathrm{End}(E)\otimes|\Lambda|)\to\tilde{\Sigma}^{0}_{H}(X;E)\xrightarrow{\rho_{m}}\Sigma^{0}_{H}(X;E)\to 0.

Compare to Equation (
47
). Since
\sigma_{H}(F)
is a self-adjoint unitary, the element

p_{F}:=\frac{1+\sigma_{H}(F)}{2}\in\Sigma^{0}_{H}(X;E),

is a projection and defines an element
[p_{F}]\in K_{0}(\Sigma^{0}_{H}(X;E))
. Under the isomorphism
K_{1}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))\cong KK_{1}(\mathbb{C},C^{*}(T_{H}X))
we have the identity

[\sigma_{H}(F)]=j_{*}\partial[p_{F}]\in K_{1}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)),

where
\partial:K_{0}(\Sigma^{0}_{H}(X;E))\to K_{1}(C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X,\mathrm{End}(E)\otimes|\Lambda|)))
is the boundary mapping and
j:C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X,\mathrm{End}(E)\otimes|\Lambda|)\to C^{*}(T_{H}X;\mathrm{End}(E))
is the inclusion.

Similar statements can be made for the even
K
-theory class of an
H
-elliptic operator
[\sigma_{H}(F)]\in K_{0}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
if
F\in\Psi^{0}_{H}(X;E_{1},E_{2})
is unitary up to
\Psi^{-1}_{H}
. More directly (and generally), following
[
10
]
, if
F\in\Psi^{m}_{H}(X;E_{1},E_{2})
is an
H
-elliptic operator and
a\in\tilde{\Sigma}_{H}^{m}(X;E_{1},E_{2})
lifts
\sigma_{H}(D)
then
(C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X;E_{1}),C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X,E_{2}),a)
is a cycle for algebraic relative
K
-theory class in
K_{0}^{\rm alg}(\tilde{\Sigma}_{H}^{m}(X),C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X)
. Using excision in algebraic
K
-theory, we can identify

K_{0}^{\rm alg}(\tilde{\Sigma}_{H}^{m}(X),C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X)\cong K_{0}(C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X)),

and under this identification

[\sigma_{H}(F)]=j_{*}[(C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X;E_{1}),C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X,E_{2}),a)]\in K_{0}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)).

0.21.1.
Localization and approximation of the symbol class

Let us specialize to the case that
X
is a regular Carnot manifold admitting flat orbits. We follow the notation of the Subsection
0.20.1
. We are interested in better describing the class
[\sigma_{H}(D)]
and in particular in finding pre-images under the surjection
K_{*}(I_{X})\to K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
. We write

\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E})_{t=0}:=\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E})\otimes_{\mathrm{ev}_{t=0}}C^{*}(T_{H}X),

where
\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E})
is as in Section
0.20
. For
B
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules
\mathpzc{E}_{1}
and
\mathpzc{E}_{2}
, write

\mathcal{Q}_{B}(\mathpzc{E}_{1},\mathpzc{E}_{2}):=\mathrm{Hom}_{B}^{*}(\mathpzc{E}_{1},\mathpzc{E}_{2})/\mathbb{K}_{B}(\mathpzc{E}_{1},\mathpzc{E}_{2}),

for the corona algebra space. We also write

\Gamma_{\geq\lambda_{0},X}:=\cup_{\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}}\delta_{\lambda}(\Gamma^{\partial}_{X}),

(48)

for
\lambda_{0}>0
. The set
\Gamma_{\geq\lambda_{0},X}
is closed both in
\Gamma_{X}
and in
\widehat{T_{H}X}
.

Lemma 0.21.6
.

Let
X
be a compact
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold. Let
\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\to X
be graded hermitean vector bundles. Assume that
\tilde{a}\in\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{H,{\rm gr}}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\boldsymbol{E}_{2})
is invertible modulo
C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X;\mathrm{Hom}(E)\otimes|\Lambda|)
. Let

\displaystyle q:\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{*}(T_{H}X)}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s}_{\rm gr}

\displaystyle(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1})_{t=0},\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{2})_{t=0})\to

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{C^{*}(T_{H}X)}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1})_{t=0},\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{2})_{t=0}),

denote the quotient map.

Then, up to stabilizing by a trivial, graded vector bundle, there exists a self-adjoint element

\hat{\sigma}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}),\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2}))

such that

(1)

\hat{\sigma}
has an inverse
\hat{\sigma}^{-1}\in\mathrm{End}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2}),\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}))
modulo
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-compact operators.

(2)

\hat{\sigma}
(and similarly for
\hat{\sigma}^{-1}
) is in the image of the injection

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{*}(T_{H}X)}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1})_{t=0}

\displaystyle,\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{2})_{t=0})\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}),\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2}))

(cf. Proposition
0.20.17
) and there is a norm-continuous homotopy

q(\tilde{a})\sim_{h}q(\hat{\sigma}),

in the set of invertibles in

\mathcal{Q}_{C^{*}(T_{H}X)}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1})_{t=0},\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{2})_{t=0})

(3)

For
\lambda_{0}>0
, then as operators on
\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}_{\geq\lambda_{0},\partial}^{s}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}):=\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1})\otimes_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}C_{0}(\Gamma_{\geq\lambda_{0},X})
,

\hat{\sigma}=\rho_{m}(a),

and in particular
\hat{\sigma}
is as an operator on
\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1})
homogeneous of degree
m
up to locally
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-compact operators.

In particular, the index class
[\tilde{a}]\in K_{0}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
, defined from the Kasparov cycle
(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1})_{t=0},\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{2})_{t=0},\tilde{a})
, is the image of the index class
[\hat{\sigma}]\in K_{0}(I_{X})
, defined from the Kasparov cycle
(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}),\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2}),\hat{\sigma})
, under the surjection

K_{0}(C_{0}(\Gamma_{X}))\cong K_{0}(I_{X})\to K_{0}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)),

where the first map is defined from the Morita equivalence
\mathpzc{H}
.

Proof.

After order reduction, we can assume that
s=m=0
and that all bundles are trivially graded. For notational simplicity we shall even assume that the vector bundles are the trivial line bundle, up to stabilization by trivial bundles this does not alter the statement. Note that under these reductions,

\displaystyle\mathrm{End}_{C^{*}(T_{H}X)}^{*}(\mathpzc{E}(X;\mathbb{C})_{t=0})=

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)),

\displaystyle\mathbb{K}_{C^{*}(T_{H}X)}(\mathpzc{E}(X;\mathbb{C})_{t=0})=

\displaystyle C^{*}(T_{H}X),

\displaystyle\mathrm{End}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\mathpzc{H})=

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(I_{X}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{K}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}(\mathpzc{H})=

\displaystyle I_{X}.

Consider the commuting diagram with exact rows

\begin{CD}0@>{}>{}>C^{*}(T_{H}X)@>{}>{}>\mathcal{M}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))@>{}>{}>\mathcal{Q}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)@>{}>{}>0\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@ VV=V\\
0@>{}>{}>C^{*}(T_{H}X)/I_{X}@>{}>{}>\mathcal{M}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))/I_{X}@>{}>{}>\mathcal{Q}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))@>{}>{}>0\\
\end{CD}.

Since
j_{*}:K_{*}(I_{X})\to K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
is surjective the map
K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))\to K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)/I_{X})
, induced by the quotient map, is the zero map. By naturality of boundary mappings, the map
K_{*}(\mathcal{M}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))/I_{X})\to K_{*}(\mathcal{Q}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)))
is surjective. In particular, the class
[\tilde{a}]\in K_{1}(\mathcal{Q}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)))
is in the image of
K_{1}(\mathcal{M}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))/I_{X})\to K_{1}(\mathcal{Q}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
. Therefore, there is, up to stabilization, an element
\tilde{\sigma}\in\mathcal{M}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
invertible modulo
I_{X}
which is homotopic to
\tilde{a}
modulo
C^{*}(T_{H}X)
in the invertibles of
\mathcal{Q}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
. Since the set
\Gamma_{\geq\lambda_{0},X}\subseteq\widehat{T_{H}X}
is closed, and
\tilde{a}
is invertible modulo
C^{*}(T_{H}X)
,
\tilde{a}
acts invertibly over
\Gamma_{\geq\lambda_{0},X}
for some
\lambda_{0}>0
. Standard considerations ensures that we can take
\tilde{\sigma}
to act as
\tilde{a}
on
\Gamma_{\geq\lambda_{0},X}
. The proof is complete upon taking
\hat{\sigma}
to be the image of
\tilde{\sigma}
under
\mathcal{M}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))\to\mathcal{M}(I_{X})
.
∎

Remark 0.21.7
.

Since
\tilde{\sigma}
is unitary up to
I_{X}
, it is especially invertible in the trivial representation. In particular, if
F\in\Psi^{m}_{H}(X;E_{1},E_{2})
is an
H
-elliptic operator, Lemma
0.21.6
induces a (stable) isomorphism of vector bundles

\sigma_{0}:E_{1}\xrightarrow{\sim}E_{2}.

Lemma 0.21.8
.

Let
X
be a compact
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold,
E\to X
a hermitean vector bundle and
m>0
. Assume that
\tilde{a}\in\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{H}(X;E)
is invertible modulo
C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X;\mathrm{Hom}(E)\otimes|\Lambda|)
and formally self-adjoint.

Then, up to stabilizing by a trivial vector bundle, there exists a self-adjoint, regular, densely defined operator

\hat{\sigma}:\mathpzc{H}(E)\dashrightarrow\mathpzc{H}(E),

with

\mathrm{Dom}\,(\hat{\sigma})=\mathpzc{H}^{m}(E),

such that

(1)

\hat{\sigma}
, viewed as an adjointable map of
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules
\mathpzc{H}^{m}(E)\to\mathpzc{H}(E)
, has an inverse
\hat{\sigma}^{-1}:\mathpzc{H}^{m}(E)\to\mathpzc{H}(E)
modulo
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-compact operators.

(2)

\hat{\sigma}
(and similarly for
\hat{\sigma}^{-1}
) is in the image of the injection

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{*}(T_{H}X)}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{m}(X;E)_{t=0},\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}(X;E)_{t=0})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\mathpzc{H}^{m}(E),\mathpzc{H}(E)),

(cf. Proposition
0.20.17
) and there is a norm-continuous homotopy

q(\tilde{a})\sim_{h}q(\hat{\sigma}),

in the set of invertibles in

\mathcal{Q}_{C^{*}(T_{H}X)}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{m}(X;E)_{t=0},\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}(X;E)_{t=0})

(3)

For
\lambda_{0}>0
, then as operators on
\mathpzc{H}_{\geq\lambda_{0},\partial}^{m}(E):=\mathpzc{H}^{m}(E)\otimes_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}C_{0}(\Gamma_{\geq\lambda_{0},X})
,

\tilde{\sigma}=\rho_{m}(a),

and in particular
q(\tilde{\sigma})
is as an operator on
\mathpzc{H}^{m}(E)
homogeneous of degree
m
up to locally
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-compact operators.

In particular, the index class
[\tilde{a}]\in K_{1}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
, defined from the unbounded Kasparov cycle
(C^{*}(T_{H}X;E)),\tilde{a})
, is the image of the index class
[\hat{\sigma}]\in K_{1}(C_{0}(\Gamma_{X}))
, defined from the unbounded Kasparov cycle
(\mathpzc{H}(E),\hat{\sigma})
, under the surjection

K_{1}(C_{0}(\Gamma_{X}))\cong K_{1}(I_{X})\to K_{1}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)),

where the first map is defined from the Morita equivalence
\mathpzc{H}
.

Proof.

The method of proof of Lemma
0.21.6
produces a formally self-adjoint
\tilde{\sigma}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{*}(T_{H}X)}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{m}(X;E)_{t=0},\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}(X;E)_{t=0})
fulfilling item (1)-(3). Viewing the associated
\hat{\sigma}
as a densely defined operator, the self-adjointness and regularity then follows from item (1).
∎

0.21.2.
Further computations with the symbol class in the even case

The following lemma will be used for explicit geometric solutions to examples of the index problem. We note that the lemma requires assumptions that do not apply to all
H
-elliptic operators, just as is the case with the explicit geometric solution in
[
10
]
. We shall make use of Atiyah’s notion of an elliptic complex, see Definition
0.22.1
below.

Lemma 0.21.9
.

Let
X
be a compact
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold. Consider an orthogonal decomposition

\mathcal{H}=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}_{k},

of the bundle of flat representations
\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{X}
, where each
\mathcal{H}_{k}\to\Gamma_{X}
is a finite rank hermitean vector bundle with
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X},\mathcal{H}_{k})\subseteq\cap_{s>0}\mathpzc{H}^{s}
. Define
\mathcal{H}^{N}:=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{N}\mathcal{H}_{k}
and let
P_{N}
denote the associated projection.

Assume that

\hat{\sigma}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}),\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2}))

for some
N_{0}>0
satisfies

(1)

\hat{\sigma}
is a
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-linear adjointable mapping invertible modulo
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-compact operators;

(2)

[P_{N},\hat{\sigma}]\in\mathbb{K}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}),\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2}))
for
N>N_{0}
;

(3)

the class of the
(\mathbb{C},C_{0}(\Gamma_{X}))
-Kasparov cycle

((1-P_{N})\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}),(1-P_{N})\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2}),(1-P_{N})\hat{\sigma}(1-P_{N})),

vanishes in
KK_{0}(\mathbb{C},C_{0}(\Gamma_{X}))\cong K^{0}(\Gamma_{X})
for all
N>N_{0}
.

Then the vector bundle morphism

\hat{\sigma}_{N}:=(P_{N}\otimes 1_{E_{2}})\hat{\sigma}(P_{N}\otimes 1_{E_{1}}):\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\to\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{2},

is invertible outside a compact subset of
\Gamma_{X}
. The elliptic complex on
\Gamma_{X}

(\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{2},\hat{\sigma}_{N}).

defines a class
[(\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{2},\hat{\sigma}_{N})]\in K^{0}(\Gamma_{X})
that for
N>N_{0}
is independent of
N
. Moreover, for
N>N_{0}
, it holds that

[(\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{2},\hat{\sigma}_{N})]=[(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}),\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2}),\hat{\sigma})]\in K^{0}(\Gamma_{X}).

Proof.

By assumption (2), we can assume that
P_{N}
commutes with
\hat{\sigma}
. Therefore item (1) ensures that
\hat{\sigma}_{N}
is invertible modulo
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-compact operators on the sections of a vector bundle. As such,
\hat{\sigma}_{N}
is invertible outside a compact subset of
\Gamma_{X}
. Moreover, by item (3), we have that

\displaystyle[(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2}),\hat{\sigma})]=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{2},\hat{\sigma}_{N})]+

\displaystyle+[((1-P_{N})\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}),(1-P_{N})\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}_{2}),(1-P_{N})\hat{\sigma}(1-P_{N}))]=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{2},\hat{\sigma}_{N})],

for
N>N_{0}
.
∎

Remark 0.21.10
.

We note that the elliptic complex
(\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{2},\hat{\sigma}_{N})
in Lemma
0.21.9
depends on the choice of bundle of flat orbits
\mathcal{H}
; if we replace
\mathcal{H}
by
\mathcal{H}\otimes L
for a line bundle
L\to\Gamma_{X}
then
(\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{2},\hat{\sigma}_{N})
transforms to
(\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{2},\hat{\sigma}_{N})\otimes L
. On the other hand, the class
[(\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{N}\otimes\boldsymbol{E}_{2},\hat{\sigma}_{N}))]\otimes[\mathcal{H}^{*}]\in K_{0}(I_{X})
remains unaltered by changing
\mathcal{H}
to
\mathcal{H}\otimes L
because
L\otimes L^{*}\cong 1
.

Remark 0.21.11
.

Lemma
0.21.9
mimics constructions of
[
10
]
for contact manifolds. The constructions from
[
10
]
fits with decompositions of
\mathcal{H}
as follows. If
\text{\mxedr.g}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}_{\rm gr}(\mathfrak{g})
is a compact subgroup such that the graded frame bundle of
X
reduces to
\mxedr
.g
, and

[\zeta|_{\text{\mxedr.g}}]=0\in H^{1}(\text{\mxedr.g},\check{H}^{1}(\Gamma,U(1)))
and
[c_{\text{\mxedr.g}}]=0\in H^{2}(\text{\mxedr.g},C(\Gamma,U(1)))
, then

\mathcal{H}=\bigoplus_{\chi\in\hat{\text{\mxedr.g}}}\mathcal{H}_{\chi},

(49)

under the Peter-Weyl decomposition. Assuming that each irreducible
\mxedr
.g
-representation has finite multiplicity in
\mathcal{H}
, then we have a decomposition as in Lemma
0.21.9
. For the case of a
2n+1
-dimensional co-oriented contact manifold considered in
[
10
]
the relevant structure group is
\text{\mxedr.g}=U(n)
.

Lemma 0.21.12
.

Let
X
be a compact
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold with
\Gamma^{\partial}_{X}
compact. Assume that
\boldsymbol{E}\to X
is a graded, hermitean vector bundle. Consider an orthogonal decomposition

\mathcal{H}=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}_{k},

as in Lemma
0.21.9
with the additional assumption that the decomposition is dilation invariant. Assume that

\hat{\sigma}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}),\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}))

satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
0.21.9
and additionally, there is a homogenous of degree
m
and invertible

\sigma\in\mathrm{Hom}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}),\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}))

such that, for some
\lambda_{0}>0
, then as operators on
\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}_{\geq\lambda_{0},\partial}^{s}(\boldsymbol{E}):=\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E})\otimes_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}C_{0}(\Gamma_{\geq\lambda_{0},X})
,

\tilde{\sigma}=\sigma.

Then, under the composition of the natural mappings

K^{0}(\Gamma_{X})\cong K^{0}(\Gamma_{X}^{\partial}\times\mathbb{R})\to K^{0}(\Gamma_{X}^{\partial}\times S^{1}),

we have that

[(\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}),\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{H}}^{s-m}_{\rm gr}(\boldsymbol{E}),\hat{\sigma})]\mapsto[E_{\sigma,N}]-[(\mathcal{H}^{N}_{\partial}\otimes\boldsymbol{E})\times S^{1}],

where
N>N_{0}
and the vector bundle
E_{\sigma,N}\to\Gamma_{X}^{\partial}\times S^{1}
is obtained from clutching
(\mathcal{H}^{N}_{\partial}\otimes\boldsymbol{E})\times[0,1]\to\Gamma_{X}^{\partial}\times[0,1]
along
P_{N}\sigma P_{N}
at the boundary.

Proof.

Using the reduction of Lemma
0.21.9
, this is a standard computation in
K
-theory. Compare to
[
10
]
.
∎

Corollary 0.21.13
.

Let
X
be a compact regular polycontact manifold with polycontact structure
H
, equipped with a Riemannian metric
g
. Assume that
D_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{DO}_{H}^{2}(X;E)
is as in Equation (
43
) and is
H
-elliptic. For
N>>0
, the symbol class
[\sigma_{H}(D_{\gamma})]\in K_{0}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
has a pre-image
x\in K^{0}(\Gamma_{X})=K^{0}(H^{\perp}\setminus X)
that under the natural mapping

K^{0}(H^{\perp}\setminus X)\cong K^{0}(S(H^{\perp})\times\mathbb{R})\to K^{0}(S(H^{\perp})\times S^{1}),

is mapped to
[E_{\gamma,N}]-[(\oplus_{k\leq N}(p_{\Gamma}^{*}H)^{\otimes^{\rm sym}_{\mathbb{C}}k}\otimes E)\times S^{1}]
where
E_{\gamma,N}\to S(H^{\perp})\times S^{1}
is obtained from clutching
(\oplus_{k\leq N}(p_{\Gamma}^{*}H)^{\otimes^{\rm sym}_{\mathbb{C}}k}\otimes E)\times[0,1]\to S(H^{\perp})\times[0,1]
along
\oplus_{k\leq N}\gamma_{k}
at the boundary (where
\gamma_{k}
is as in Proposition
0.20.21
).

0.21.3.
Further computations with the symbol class in the odd case

Let us consider a lemma that applies to the case of a self-adjoint
H
-elliptic operator where the symbol can be decomposed along an idempotent. Recall the notation

\mathpzc{H}_{\geq\lambda_{0},\partial}^{s}(E):=\mathpzc{H}^{s}(E)\otimes_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}C_{0}(\Gamma_{\geq\lambda_{0},X}).

Lemma 0.21.14
.

Let
X
be a compact
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold with
\Gamma^{\partial}_{X}
compact. Assume that
E\to X
is a hermitean vector bundle. Consider a formally self-adjoint
\tilde{a}\in\tilde{\Sigma}^{m}_{H}(X;E)
, of order
m>0
and invertible modulo
C^{\infty}_{c}(T_{H}X;E)
. Assume that there is an idempotent
p\in\mathbb{K}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X}^{\partial})}(\mathpzc{H}_{\partial}(E))
such that for some
\lambda_{0}>0

(1)

p
extends by continuity and homogeneity to a bounded idempotent operator on
\mathpzc{H}^{m}(E)
;

(2)

[p,a]\in\mathbb{K}_{C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})}(\mathpzc{H}_{\geq\lambda_{0},\partial}^{m}(E),\mathpzc{H}_{\geq\lambda_{0},\partial}(E))
;

(3)

pa
and
-(1-p)a
are positive in the sense of densely defined quadratic forms on
\mathpzc{H}_{\geq\lambda_{0},\partial}(E)
.

Then, the index class
[\tilde{a}]\in K_{1}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
, defined from the unbounded Kasparov cycle
(C^{*}(T_{H}X;E)),\tilde{a})
, is the image of an element
x\in K^{1}(\Gamma_{X})
that under the Bott isomorphism

K^{1}(\Gamma_{X})\cong K^{0}(\Gamma_{X}^{\partial}),

is mapped to the class
[p(\mathcal{H}_{\partial}(E))]
.

Proof.

The index class
[(C^{*}(T_{H}X;E)),\tilde{a})]\in K_{1}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
is the image of the class of the projection

\left[\frac{1}{\pi}\arctan(\tilde{a})+\frac{1}{2}\right]\in\mathcal{Q}(C^{*}(T_{H}X;E)),

under the boundary mapping
K_{0}(\mathcal{Q}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)))\to K_{1}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
. The image under this boundary mapping is the class of the unitary

\mathrm{e}^{2\pi i\left(\frac{1}{\pi}\arctan(\tilde{a})+\frac{1}{2}\right)}.

(50)

On the other hand,
[p(\mathcal{H}_{\partial}(E))]
corresponds to the class
[p]\in K_{0}(I^{\partial}_{X})
under the isomorphism
K^{0}(\Gamma_{X}^{\partial})\cong K_{0}(I^{\partial}_{X})
defined from the Morita equivalence
\mathcal{H}
. The image of
[p]\in K_{0}(I^{\partial}_{X})
under

K_{0}(I^{\partial}_{X})\cong K_{1}(I_{X})\to K_{1}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)),

is given by the unitary
\mathrm{e}^{2\pi ix}
for a lift
x
of
p
under
C^{*}(T_{H}X)\to I_{X}^{\partial}
. By our assumptions it follows that
\mathrm{e}^{2\pi ix}
is homotopic inside the unitaries of
C^{*}(T_{H}X)
to the unitary (
50
).
∎

Corollary 0.21.15
.

Let
X
be a compact regular polycontact manifold with polycontact structure
H
, equipped with a Riemannian metric
g
. Assume that
P\in\Psi_{H}^{0}(X;E)
is an idempotent Hermite operator. The symbol class
[\sigma_{H}(2P-1)]\in K_{1}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
has a pre-image
x\in K^{1}(\Gamma_{X})=K^{1}(H^{\perp}\setminus X)
that under the Bott mapping

K^{1}(H^{\perp}\setminus X)\cong K^{0}(S(H^{\perp})),

is mapped to the class of the vector bundle

\sigma_{H}(P)(\oplus_{k}(p_{\Gamma}^{*}H)^{\otimes^{\rm sym}_{\mathbb{C}}k}\otimes E)\to S(H^{\perp}).

Part V
K
-homological dualities and index theory on Carnot manifolds

Introduction to part 5

To study index theory of
H
-elliptic operators on Carnot manifolds, dualities in
KK
-theory and geometric
K
-homology will play a cardinal role. Much of the technical setup of the earlier chapters in this work serves the purpose of describing these dualities in more detail. In this last part of this work we put the various pieces together. We assume that the reader is familiar with
KK
-theory
[
41
,
80
,
89
,
96
,
109
,
110
]
, geometric
K
-homology
[
7
,
8
,
11
,
52
,
132
]
and the connections between the two
[
9
,
12
,
54
]
. The goal of this part is to set the index problem for
H
-elliptic operators in the Heisenberg calculus in a
K
-homological context via the dualities discussed above in Theorem
4
and apply the
K
-theory computations from the previous Part
IV
. This part is divided into the following four sections:

•

In Section
0.22
we develop a variation of geometric
K
-homology that uses geometric cycles modelled on noncompact spin
c
-manifolds.

•

In Section
0.23
we study
K
-homological dualities.

•

In Section
0.24
we conclude index theorems for
H
-elliptic operators from Section
0.23
.

•

In Section
0.25
we discuss the problem of solving the index problem for graded Rockland sequences.

0.22.
Geometric
K
-homology with coefficients in elliptic complexes

In order to construct geometric dualities, we shall first need to construct a slight modification of Baum-Douglas geometric
K
-homology
[
7
,
8
]
. Fix a finite CW-complex
X
. A geometric cycle on
X
is a triple
(M,E,f)
, where
M
is a compact spin
c
-manifold,
E\to M
a complex vector bundle and
f:M\to X
is a continuous map. We recall that Baum-Douglas constructs
K_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
as the group of equivalence classes of geometric cycles modulo a relation generated by the direct sum/disjoint union relation, bordism and vector bundle modification
[
7
,
8
]
. By the results of
[
7
,
8
]
, proven in more detail in
[
9
,
12
]
there is a natural isomorphism

\gamma:K_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)\to K_{*}^{\rm an}(X):=KK_{*}(C(X),\mathbb{C}),

that maps the class of the cycle
(M,E,f)
to the
K
-homology class
f_{*}[D_{E}]
where
[D_{E}]\in KK_{*}(C(M),\mathbb{C})
is the
K
-homology class of a spin
c
-Dirac operator on
M
twisted by
E
.

As we saw in the preceding parts, the spin
c
-manifold
\Gamma_{X}
plays an important role when
X
is a regular Carnot manifold with flat orbits. However,
\Gamma_{X}
is not compact. For the specific example of contact manifolds, this problem was solved in
[
10
]
by using the dilation action to reduce to
\Gamma_{X}^{\partial}
which is compact as it is a trivial two-fold cover of
X
for a compact cooriented contact manifold. In general, the authors see no naturally associated compact manifold constructed from
\Gamma_{X}
. So we shall utilize a mild modification of Baum-Douglas’ geometric
K
-homology modelled on non-compact manifolds with coefficients in elliptic complexes. As discussed in Remark
0.1.1
above, this modification fits well with Baum-Douglas’ solution to the index problem for elliptic operators. This non-compact analogue of Baum-Douglas’ geometric
K
-homology relies on well known results, for instance Bunke’s work
[
25
]
, and is in its own right not overly exciting. We develop it for the sake of simplifying the description of the geometric solution to the index problem for
H
-elliptic operators. A similar model for geometric
K
-homology can be extracted from
[
63
]
.

Recall Atiyah’s notion of an elliptic complex:

Definition 0.22.1
.

Let
M
be a topological space. An elliptic complex on
M
is a triple
\xi=(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
where
E_{1},E_{2}\to M
are complex vector bundles and
\sigma:E_{1}\to E_{2}
is a vector bundle morphism which is an isomorphism outside a compact subset of
M
. An isomorphism of elliptic complexes
(E_{1}^{\prime},E_{2}^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})\cong(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
consists of isomorphisms
E_{1}\cong E_{1}^{\prime}
and
E_{2}\cong E_{2}^{\prime}
compatible with the bundle maps
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
.

We also introduce the following terminology.

•

The support of
\xi
or
\sigma
is defined as

\mathrm{supp}(\xi)\equiv\mathrm{supp}(\sigma):=\{x\in M:\sigma(x)\;\mbox{is not an isomorphism}\}.

•

If an elliptic complex
(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
has empty support
\mathrm{supp}(\sigma)=\emptyset
we say that
(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
is degenerate.

•

If
(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
is an elliptic complex on
M\times[0,1]
, we say that the elliptic complexes
(E_{1}|_{M\times\{0\}},E_{2}|_{M\times\{0\}},\sigma|_{M\times\{0\}})
and
(E_{1}|_{M\times\{1\}},E_{2}|_{M\times\{1\}},\sigma|_{M\times\{1\}})
are homotopic.

•

We say that two elliptic complexes
\xi^{\prime}=(E_{1}^{\prime},E_{2}^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
and
\xi=(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
are equivalent if there are degenerate elliptic complexes
\xi_{0},\xi_{1}
and a homotopy from
\xi\oplus\xi_{0}
to
\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi_{1}
.

The set of equivalence classes of isomorphism classes of elliptic complexes is denoted by
\tilde{K}^{0}(M)
.

It is well known that
\tilde{K}^{0}(M)\cong K^{0}(M)\cong KK_{0}(\mathbb{C},C_{0}(M))
via natural isomorphisms. Indeed, if
M
is compact the isomorphism
\tilde{K}^{0}(M)\cong K^{0}(M)
is implemented by mapping
(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)\mapsto[E_{1}]-[E_{2}]
; its inverse maps a vector bundle
E\to M
to the elliptic complex
(E,0,0)
. We note that the isomorphism
\tilde{K}^{0}(M)\cong KK_{0}(\mathbb{C},C_{0}(M))
indicates that is is possible to model
\tilde{K}^{0}(M)
on triples
(\mathpzc{E}_{1},\mathpzc{E}_{2},\sigma)
where
\mathpzc{E}_{1}
and
\mathpzc{E}_{2}
are
C_{0}(M)
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules and
\sigma
is a Fredholm map which is invertible modulo
C_{0}(M)
-compact operators. We return to this last point below in a more specific context.

Definition 0.22.2

(Cycles for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
)
.

Let
X
be a finite CW-complex. A cycle for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
(or a Baum-Douglas cycle with coefficients in elliptic complexes) is a triple
(M,\xi,f)
where

(1)

M
is a spin
c
-manifold;

(2)

\xi
is an elliptic complex on
M
; and

(3)

f:M\to X
is a continuous map.

Two cycles
(M,\xi,f)
and
(M^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},f^{\prime})
for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
are said to be isomorphic if there is a spin
c
-preserving diffeomorphism
\varphi:M\to M^{\prime}
such that
\varphi^{*}\xi^{\prime}=\xi
and
f=f^{\prime}\circ\varphi
.

We shall only concern ourselves with
X
being a finite CW-complex, but in principle one can modify the construction to non-compact
X
, but still locally compact. For instance, compactly supported cycles would be defined as above with the additional requirement that
f
has compact range and locally finite cycles would be defined as above with the additional requirement that
f
is proper on the support of
\xi
. The reader can find details on such variations in
[
63
]
.

Example 0.22.3
.

Let us give an example of a Baum-Douglas cycle with coefficients in elliptic complexes that is prototypical for how they shall be used in this work. Assume that
X
is an
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold. Take a relative cycle
(p,q,u)
for
K_{0}(I_{X})
, i.e.
p,q\in\mathcal{M}(I_{X})
are projections and
u\in\mathcal{U}(I_{X})
is a unitary muliplier with
upu^{*}-q\in I_{X}
. Assume for simplicity that
p
and
q
are
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-locally compact, i.e.
gp,gq\in I_{X}
for any
g\in C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
. Take a bundle of flat representation
\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{X}
. We then consider the triple

(p\mathcal{H},q\mathcal{H},qup).

Since
p
and
q
are
C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
-locally compact,
p\mathcal{H},q\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{X}
are complex vector bundles and since
upu^{*}-q\in I_{X}
the morphism
qup:p\mathcal{H}\to q\mathcal{H}
is an isomorphism outside a compact subset (where its inverse is
pu^{*}q
).

The prototypical example of relevance for this work is the Baum-Douglas cycle with coefficients in elliptic complexes of the form

(\Gamma_{X},(p\mathcal{H},q\mathcal{H},qup),p_{\Gamma}),

where
p_{\Gamma}:\Gamma_{X}\to X
is the projection. We equipp
\Gamma_{X}
with the spin
c
-structure from Proposition
0.17.5
. Cycles of this form are constructed from
H
-elliptic operators by means of the methods of Section
0.21
.

To construct a
K
-homology group, we need to construct an equivalence relation. We proceed as in
[
7
,
8
]
. By an abuse of notation we shall mean isomorphism class of a geometric cycle when referring to a geometric cycle and by a set of geometric cycles we tacitly mean the corresponding set of isomorphism classes of geometric cycles.

Definition 0.22.4

(The direct sum/disjoint union relation for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
)
.

Let
X
be a finite CW-complex. For two cycles for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
of the form
(M,\xi,f)
and
(M,\xi^{\prime},f)
we declare that

(M,\xi,f)\dot{\cup}(M,\xi^{\prime},f)\sim_{didi}(M,\xi+\xi^{\prime},f).

Definition 0.22.5

(The degenerate relation for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
)
.

Let
X
be a finite CW-complex. A cycle
(M_{0},\xi_{0},f_{0})
for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
is said to be degenerate if
\xi_{0}
is degenerate. We say that two cycles
(M,\xi,f)
and
(M^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},f^{\prime})
for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
are degenerately equivalent if there are degenerate cycles
(M_{0},\xi_{0},f_{0})
and
(M_{1},\xi_{1},f_{1})
for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
with

(M,\xi,f)\dot{\cup}(M_{0},\xi_{0},f_{0})=(M^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},f^{\prime})\dot{\cup}(M_{1},\xi_{1},f_{1}).

Definition 0.22.6

(The bordism relation for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
)
.

Let
X
be a finite CW-complex. A cycle with boundary for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
is a triple
(W,\xi,f)
where

(1)

W
is a spin
c
-manifold with boundary;

(2)

\xi
is an elliptic complex on
W
; and

(3)

f:W\to X
is a continuous map.

The cycle
\partial(W,\xi,f):=(\partial W,\xi|_{\partial W},f|_{\partial W})
is said to be nullbordant.

We say that two cycles
(M,\xi,f)
and
(M^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},f^{\prime})
for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
are bordant if there is a cycle with boundary
(W,\xi,f)
for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
such that

\partial(W,\xi,f)=(M,\xi,f)\dot{\cup}-(M^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},f^{\prime}).

We write this as

(M,\xi,f)\sim_{\rm bor}(M^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},f^{\prime}).

The bordism relation is an equivalence relation.

Before proceeding further we note the strength of the bordism relation in the following two lemmas.

Lemma 0.22.7
.

Let
X
be a finite CW-complex and
(M,\xi,f)
a cycle for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
. Assume that
M_{0}\subseteq M
is an open domain with smooth boundary such that
\mathrm{supp}(\xi)\subseteq M_{0}
. Then there is a bordism

(M,\xi,f)\sim_{\rm bor}(M_{0},\xi|_{M_{0}},f|_{M_{0}}).

(51)

Proof.

We note that the cycle
(M_{0},\xi|_{M_{0}},f|_{M_{0}})
is well defined because
\mathrm{supp}(\xi)\subseteq M_{0}
. To construct the bordism, we take

W:=M\times[0,1]\setminus((M\setminus M_{0})\times\{0\}).

Since
M\setminus M_{0}
is closed in
M
, it is clear that
W
is a spin
c
-manifold with boundary. We tacitly abuse the notations and write
f
for the composition of
f:M\to X
with obvious maps such as the projection
W\to M
. The cycle
(W,(\xi\times[0,1])|_{W},f)
is a cycle with boundary, and since

\partial(W,(\xi\times[0,1])|_{W},f)=(-(M_{0},\xi|_{M_{0}},f|_{M_{0}}))\dot{\cup}(M,\xi,f),

we conclude that there is a bordism as in Equation (
51
).
∎

Lemma 0.22.8
.

Let
X
be a finite CW-complex and
(M,\xi,f)
a cycle for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
. Assume that
M_{0}\subseteq M
is a pre-compact open domain with smooth boundary such that
\mathrm{supp}(\xi)\subseteq M_{0}
. Consider the spin
c
-manifold obtained from the doubling construction

Z:=2M_{0}=\overline{M_{0}}\cup_{\partial M_{0}}(-\overline{M_{0}}).

Write
\xi=(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
and define
E_{\xi}\to Z
as the vector bundle

E_{1}\cup_{\sigma|_{\partial M_{0}}}E_{2}\to Z,

obtained from clutching
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
along the isomorphism
\sigma|_{\partial M_{0}}
. Then
(M,\xi,f)
and
(Z,(E_{\xi},0,0),f)
lie in the same equivalence class of the relation generated by the disjoint union/direct sum relation, degenerate equivalence and the bordism relation.

In particular, a cycle with elliptic complexes
(M,\xi,f)
is equivalent to a cycle of the form
(Z,(E_{\xi},0,0),f)
where
(Z,E_{\xi},f)
is an ordinary Baum-Douglas geometric cycle (with a compact spin
c
-manifold and vector bundle coefficients).

Proof.

We can extend
E_{1},E_{2}\to M_{0}
to vector bundles on
Z
by gluing along the identity map on
\partial M_{0}
. By an abuse of notations we denote these also by
E_{1},E_{2}\to Z
. By construction,
E_{\xi}|_{M_{0}}=E_{1}|_{M_{0}}
and
E_{\xi}|_{Z\setminus M_{0}}=E_{2}|_{Z\setminus M_{0}}
. The morphism
\sigma
glues together with the identity on
Z\setminus M_{0}
to a vector bundle morphism

\tilde{\sigma}:E_{\xi}\to E_{2},

over
Z
. Note that the support of
\sigma
in
M_{0}
is unaltered by this extension procedure.
Next, we prove that

(Z,(E_{\xi},0,0),f)\sim_{\rm bor}(Z,(E_{\xi},0,0),f)\dot{\cup}(Z,(0,E_{2},0),f).

This follows from the fact that
(Z,(0,E_{2},0),f)=\partial(M_{0}\times[0,1],(0,E_{2}\times[0,1],0),f)
.
We have a direct sum/disjoint union relation

(Z,(E_{\xi},0,0),f)\dot{\cup}(Z,(0,E_{2},0),f)\dot{\cup}(Z,(E_{2},E_{2},0),f)\sim_{didi}(Z,(E_{\xi}\oplus E_{2},E_{2}\oplus E_{2},0),f).

Since homotopy of elliptic complexes induces a bordism, and
Z
is compact, we have a bordism

(Z,(E_{\xi}\oplus E_{2},E_{2}\oplus E_{2},0),f)\sim_{\rm bor}\left(Z,(E_{\xi}\oplus E_{2},E_{2}\oplus E_{2},\tilde{\sigma}\oplus 1),f\right).

Consider the spin
c
-manifold with boundary

W^{\prime}:=Z\times[0,1]\setminus((Z\setminus M_{0}\times\{0\}).

The cycle with boundary

\left(W^{\prime},\left((E_{\xi}\oplus E_{2},E_{2}\oplus E_{2},\tilde{\sigma}\oplus 1)\times[0,1]\right)|_{W^{\prime}},f\right)

implements a bordism

\left(Z,(E_{\xi}\oplus E_{2},E_{2}\oplus E_{2},\tilde{\sigma}\oplus 1),f\right)\sim_{\rm bor}(M_{0},\xi|_{M_{0}}+(E_{2},E_{2},1),f),

because
\tilde{\sigma}|_{M_{0}}=\sigma
. Since
(E_{2},E_{2},1)
is degenerate, we have a degenerate equivalence

(M_{0},\xi|_{M_{0}}+(E_{2},E_{2},1),f)\sim(M_{0},\xi|_{M_{0}},f).

Combining these constructions with the bordism of Lemma
0.22.7
, we see that
(M,\xi,f)
and
(Z,(E_{\xi},0,0),f)
lie in the same equivalence class of the relation generated by the disjoint union/direct sum relation, degenerate equivalence and the bordism relation.
∎

Remark 0.22.9
.

Lemma
0.22.8
is akin to the ideas appearing in Baum-Douglas’ geometric proof of the Atiyah-Singer index theorem, discussed in Remark
0.1.1
above. Indeed, if
A:C^{\infty}(X;E_{1})\to C^{\infty}(X;E_{2})
is an elliptic pseudodifferential operator on a compact manifold
X
, consider the spin
c
-manifold
M:=T^{*}X
and the elliptic complex
\xi_{A}=(p^{*}E_{1},p^{*}E_{2},\sigma(A))
. Here
p:M=T^{*}X\to X
denotes the projection. If we take
M_{0}
to be the ball bundle in
T^{*}X
, Lemma
0.22.8
provides a cycle
(Z,E_{\xi_{A}},p\cup_{\partial M_{0}}p)
bordant to
(T^{*}X,\xi_{A},p)
which coincides on the nose with Baum-Douglas’ geometric cycle representing the index problem for elliptic pseudodifferential operators
[
7
, Part 5]
. In situations where there is no natural place to clutch, one might argue that not clutching produces a more natural construction.

Definition 0.22.10

(Vector bundle modification for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
)
.

Let
X
be a finite CW-complex. Consider a cycle
(M,\xi,f)
for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
and a spin
c
-vector bundle
V\to M
of even rank. Write
M^{V}:=S(V\oplus 1_{\mathbb{R}})
for the sphere bundle in some metric on
V
, where
1_{\mathbb{R}}\to M
denotes the trivial line bundle. Let
\beta_{V}\to M^{V}
denote the Bott bundle (cf.
[
7
,
8
]
) and let
p_{V}:M^{V}\to M
denote the projection.

The vector bundle modification of
(M,\xi,f)
along
V
is the cycle for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
defined by

(M,\xi,f)^{V}:=(M^{V},p_{V}^{*}\xi\otimes\beta_{V},f\circ p_{V}).

We can now define the geometric
K
-homology group with coefficients in elliptic complexes.

Definition 0.22.11
.

Let
X
be a finite CW-complex. Write
\sim_{\rm BD}
for the equivalence relation on the set of isomorphism classes of cycles for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
generated by

•

The disjoint union/direct sum relation

•

Degenerate equivalence

•

The bordism relation

•

Vector bundle modification

Let
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
denote the set of equivalence classes under
\sim_{\rm bor}
on the isomorphism classes of cycles for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
.

Theorem 0.22.12
.

Let
X
be a finite CW-complex. The set
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
forms a
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded abelian group under disjoint union graded by parity of dimension. Moreover, the map at the level of cycles

(M,E,f)\mapsto(M,(E,0,0),f),

(52)

defines an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded abelian groups

K_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)\cong\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X).

Its inverse is the mapping

(M,\xi,f)\mapsto(Z,E_{\xi},f),

where
(Z,E_{\xi},f)
is constructed as in Lemma
0.22.8
.

Sketch of proof.

We only sketh the details in the proof of this theorem as the result follows from standard techniques. Indeed, almost by definition
-(M,\xi,f):=(-M,\xi,f)
is an inverse modulo bordism under disjoint union so
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
forms a
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded abelian group. The fact that
K_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)\cong\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
via the mapping (
52
) follows from that the map is surjective by Lemma
0.22.8
. A modification of the proof of Lemma
0.22.8
shows that the clutching construction
(M,\xi,f)\mapsto(Z,E_{\xi},f)
produces a left inverse so the map is injective.
∎

Definition 0.22.13

(Analytic assembly of cycles for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
)
.

Let
(M,\xi,f)
be a cycle for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
. Write
\xi=(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
. We choose a complete metric on
M
and consider a complete spin
c
-Dirac operator
\not{D}_{M}
on the complex spinor bundle
S_{M}\to M
. We assume that
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
are equipped with hermitean structures such that
\sigma
is unitary outside a compact. A (hermitean) connection for
\xi
is a pair of (hermitean) connections
(\nabla_{E_{1}},\nabla_{E_{2}})
for
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
, respectively, such that

\nabla_{E_{1}}=\sigma^{*}\nabla_{E_{2}}\sigma,

outside a compact subset of
M
. Choose a positive function
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(M)
such that
\chi=1
on the supports of
1-\sigma^{*}\sigma
and
\nabla_{E_{1}}-\sigma^{*}\nabla_{E_{2}}\sigma
and a
\lambda\geq 0
such that

\lambda>\sup\{\|1-\sigma(x)^{*}\sigma(x)\|,\|(\nabla_{E_{1}}-\sigma^{*}\nabla_{E_{2}}\sigma)(x)\|:\;x\in M\}.

If
(\nabla_{E_{1}},\nabla_{E_{2}})
is a hermitean connection for
\xi=(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
, we define the analytic assembly
\tilde{\gamma}(M,\xi,f)\in K_{*}^{\rm an}(X)
as the class of the
K
-cycle

\left(\begin{matrix}L^{2}(M;S_{M}\otimes E_{1})\\
\oplus\\
L^{2}(M;-S_{M}\otimes E_{2})\end{matrix},D_{\xi}(\lambda\chi+D_{\xi}^{2})^{-1/2}\right)

(53)

where
C(X)
acts on the Hilbert space by pull back along
f
and where

D_{\xi}:=\begin{pmatrix}D_{M}\otimes\nabla_{E_{1}}&\sigma^{*}\\
\sigma&-D_{M}\otimes\nabla_{E_{2}}\end{pmatrix}.

The choice of
\lambda
is justified from the proof of the following proposition.

Proposition 0.22.14
.

The
K
-cycle (
53
) defining the analytic assembly
\tilde{\gamma}(M,\xi,f)\in K_{*}^{\rm an}(X)
is well defined and the class
\tilde{\gamma}(M,\xi,f)
does not depend on the choices involved (of
\chi
,
\lambda
, connections or metrics).

Proof.

First we prove that
D_{\xi}
is self-adjoint. For that purpose, we write

D_{\xi}:=\begin{pmatrix}D_{M}\otimes\nabla_{E_{1}}&0\\
0&-D_{M}\otimes\nabla_{E_{2}}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}0&\sigma^{*}\\
\sigma&0\end{pmatrix}.

(54)

Since
D_{M}
is a Dirac operator defined from a complete metric the first term is self-adjoint, and the second term is bounded and self-adjoint by construction. The two terms in (
54
) anti-commute up to a smooth, compactly supported vector bundle endomorphism, because
\nabla_{E_{1}}=\sigma^{*}\nabla_{E_{2}}\sigma
outside a compact, so
D_{\xi}
is self-adjoint.

The decomposition (
54
) shows that

D_{\xi}^{2}=\begin{pmatrix}1+(D_{M}\otimes\nabla_{E_{1}})^{2}&0\\
0&1+(D_{M}\otimes\nabla_{E_{2}})^{2}\end{pmatrix}+V,

where

V:=\begin{pmatrix}1-\sigma^{*}\sigma&(D_{M}\otimes\nabla_{E_{1}})\sigma^{*}-\sigma^{*}(D_{M}\otimes\nabla_{E_{2}})\\
\sigma(D_{M}\otimes\nabla_{E_{2}})-(D_{M}\otimes\nabla_{E_{1}})\sigma&1-\sigma\sigma^{*}\end{pmatrix}

is a self-adjoint, smooth, compactly supported vector bundle endomorphism. In particular, with
\chi
and
\lambda
as in Definition
0.22.13
we can take
\varepsilon>0
so that

\lambda-\varepsilon\geq\sup\{\|1-\sigma(x)^{*}\sigma(x)\|,\|(\nabla_{E_{1}}-\sigma^{*}\nabla_{E_{2}}\sigma)(x)\|:\;x\in M\}.

and therefore

\langle(D_{\xi}^{2}+\lambda\chi)\varphi,\varphi\rangle_{L^{2}}\geq\varepsilon\langle\varphi,\varphi\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}.

Therefore,
D_{\xi}^{2}+\lambda\chi
is invertible and so
D_{\xi}
is “invertible at infinity” in the terminology of
[
25
]
. It follows from
[
25
, Proposition 1.13]
that the
K
-cycle (
53
) is well defined. Moreover, the choices involved in the construction are all from pathconnected spaces that therefore does not affect the class in
K_{*}^{\rm an}(X)
.
∎

By combining
[
25
]
with Kucerovsky’s theorem
[
102
]
, we in fact have the following result. We write
C_{g}(M)
for the
C^{*}
-algebra of bounded continuous function defined in
[
25
]
.

Proposition 0.22.15
.

Let
(M,\xi,f)
be a cycle for
K_{*}^{\rm an}(X)
with
\xi=(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
. Take a complete spin
c
-Dirac operator
\not{D}_{M}
on
M
and write
[\not{D}_{M}]\in KK_{*}(C_{0}(M),\mathbb{C})
for its associated
K
-homology class. We also define the
KK
-class

[M,\xi]:=\left[\left(C_{0}(M,E_{1}\oplus E_{2}),\begin{pmatrix}0&\sigma^{*}\\
\sigma&0\end{pmatrix}\right)\right]\in KK_{0}^{M}(C_{g}(M),C_{0}(M)).

Then the analytic assembly of
(M,\xi,f)
can be written as a Kasparov product

\tilde{\gamma}(M,\xi,f)=f_{*}\left([M,\xi]\otimes_{C_{0}(M)}[\not{D}_{M}]\right)\in K_{*}^{\rm an}(X).

Lemma 0.22.16
.

The analytic assembly of cycles with coefficients in the elliptic complexes respects the disjoint union/direct sum relation and degenerate equivalence.

Proof.

It is immediate that the disjoint union/direct sum relation. As for respecting degenerate equivalence, it suffices to prove that
\tilde{\gamma}(M,\xi,f)=0\in K_{*}(X)
if
\xi
is degenerate. Indeed, if
\xi=(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
is degenerate we can assume that
\sigma
is unitary and that
\nabla_{1}=\sigma^{*}\nabla_{2}\sigma
on all of
M
. We define the symmetry

F:=\begin{pmatrix}0&i\sigma^{*}\\
-i\sigma&0\end{pmatrix}.

The symmetry
F
commutes with
C(X)
and anti-commutes with
D_{\xi}
and so the argument proving
[
25
, Theorem 1.14]
shows that
\tilde{\gamma}(M,\xi,f)=0\in K_{*}(X)
.
∎

Lemma 0.22.17
.

The analytic assembly of cycles with coefficients in the elliptic complexes respects bordism and vector bundle modification.

Proof.

That analytic assembly respects vector bundle modification is proven in the same way as in
[
53
, Subsection 4.2]
. More specifically, using an argument as in
[
53
, Subsection 4.2]
we can write
\tilde{\gamma}((M,\xi,f)^{V})=\tilde{\gamma}(M,\xi,f)+x
where
x
is represented by a
K
-cycle
(\mathcal{H},F)
admitting a symmetry commuting with
C(X)
and anti-commuting with
F
up to compact operators and the argument proving
[
25
, Theorem 1.14]
shows that
x=0\in K_{*}(X)
.

To show that analytic assembly respects bordism, we need to prove that if
(W,\xi,f)
is a cycle with boundary then
\tilde{\gamma}(\partial(W,\xi,f))=0
. Write
\xi=(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
. Write
C_{g}(W)
and
C_{g}(\partial W)
for the
C^{*}
-algebras of bounded continuous functions from
[
25
]
. The Dirac operator defines a
K
-homology class
[\not{D}_{W}]\in KK_{*}(C_{0}(W^{\circ}),\mathbb{C})
, and similarly, the Dirac operator on the boundary produces a class
[\not{D}_{\partial W}]\in KK_{*}(C_{0}(\partial W),\mathbb{C})
. It follows from the Baum-Douglas-Taylor theorem that

\partial[\not{D}_{W}]=[\not{D}_{\partial W}],

under the boundary mapping induced from the short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to C_{0}(W^{\circ})\to

\displaystyle C_{0}(\overline{W})\to C_{0}(\partial W))\to 0.

Write
C_{g,0}(W^{\circ}):=\{f\in C_{g}(\overline{W}):f|_{\partial W}=0\}
. On the other hand, we have classes

\displaystyle[W^{\circ},\sigma]

\displaystyle:=\left[\left(C_{0}(W^{\circ},E_{1}\oplus E_{2}),\begin{pmatrix}0&\sigma^{*}\\
\sigma&0\end{pmatrix}\right)\right]\in KK_{0}^{W^{\circ}}(C_{g,0}(W^{\circ}),C_{0}(W^{\circ}));

\displaystyle[\partial{W},\sigma]

\displaystyle:=\left[\left(C_{0}(\partial W,E_{1}\oplus E_{2}),\begin{pmatrix}0&\sigma^{*}\\
\sigma&0\end{pmatrix}\right)\right]\in KK_{0}^{\partial{W}}(C_{g}(\partial{W}),C_{0}(\partial{W})).

Here we grade
E_{1}\oplus E_{2}
with
E_{1}
as even and
E_{2}
as odd. Using
C_{0}(\overline{W})
-linearity, we have that

[\partial{W},\sigma]\otimes_{C_{0}(\partial{W})}\partial=\partial\otimes_{C_{g,0}(W^{\circ})}[W^{\circ},\sigma],

where the second boundary mapping is that coming from the short exact sequence

0\to C_{g,0}(W^{\circ})\to C_{g}(\overline{W})\to C_{g}(\partial W)\to 0.

Let
\iota:\partial W\hookrightarrow\overline{W}
denote the boundary inclusion. Using
[
25
]
and Kucerovsky’s theorem
[
102
]
, we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}(\partial(W,\xi,f))=

\displaystyle(f\circ\iota)_{*}([\partial{W},\sigma]\otimes_{C_{0}(\partial{W})}[\not{D}_{\partial W}])=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(f\circ\iota)_{*}([\partial{W},\sigma]\otimes_{C_{0}(\partial{W})}\partial[\not{D}_{W}])=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(f\circ\iota)_{*}\circ\partial([W^{\circ},\sigma]\otimes_{C_{0}(W^{\circ})}[\not{D}_{W}])=0,

since
\iota_{*}\circ\partial=0
.

∎

The following observation shows that assembly of cycles with coefficients in elliptic complexes can by gluing together a compact manifold from the support of the elliptic complex, as in Lemma
0.22.8
, can be reduced to assembly of an ordinary Baum-Douglas cycle.

Proposition 0.22.18
.

Let
X
be a finite CW-complex and
(M,\xi,f)
a cycle for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
. Construct
(Z,E_{\xi},f)
as in Lemma
0.22.8
. Then it holds that

[\tilde{\gamma}(M,\xi,f)]=\gamma[(Z,E_{\xi},f)]\in K_{*}^{\rm an}(X).

Proposition
0.22.18
follows from combining Lemma
0.22.8
with the Lemmas
0.22.16
and
0.22.17
.

Theorem 0.22.19
.

Let
X
be a finite CW-complex. The analytic assembly map induces an isomorphism

\tilde{\gamma}:\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)\to K_{*}^{\rm an}(X).

of
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded abelian groups. The analytic assembly map
\tilde{\gamma}
fits into the commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}^{\rm an}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\gamma}}

(55)

with the isomorphism of Theorem
0.22.12
and the analytic assembly map
\gamma:K_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)\to K_{*}^{\rm an}(X)
of Baum-Douglas.

Proof.

Firstly, it follows from Lemma
0.22.16
and
0.22.17
that
\tilde{\gamma}
is well defined. Secondly, by definition, the diagram (
55
) commutes at the level of cycles up to an operator homotopy from
\lambda\chi
to
1
in the definition of analytic assembly. Therefore the diagram commutes. We deduce that
\tilde{\gamma}
is an isomorphism from Theorem
0.22.12
and the fact that
\gamma:K_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)\to K_{*}^{\rm an}(X)
is an isomorphism (see
[
9
]
or
[
12
]
).
∎

Definition 0.22.20

(Chern characters on
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
)
.

Consider a cycle
(M,\xi,f)
for
\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
. Let
[M]\in H_{*}^{\rm lf}(M)
denote the fundamental class in the localy finite homology,
\mathrm{Td}(M)\in H^{\rm ev}(M;\mathbb{Q})
the Todd class and
\mathrm{ch}(\xi)\in H^{\rm ev}_{c}(M;\mathbb{Q})
the Chern character in compactly supported cohomology of
[\xi]\in K^{0}(X)
. We define

\mathrm{ch}(M,\xi,f):=f_{*}(\mathrm{ch}(\xi)\cap\mathrm{Td}(M)\cap[M])\in H_{*}(X;\mathbb{Q}).

We note that if
(\nabla_{E_{1}},\nabla_{E_{2}})
is a connection for
\xi=(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
as in Definition
0.22.13
, then
\mathrm{ch}(\xi)
is represented in the compactly supported de Rham cohomology group
H^{\rm ev}_{\rm c,dR}(M)
by Chern-Weil theory as

\mathrm{ch}(\xi)=\left[\mathrm{Tr}_{E_{1}}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\nabla_{E_{1}}^{2}}{2\pi i}}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}_{E_{2}}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\nabla_{E_{2}}^{2}}{2\pi i}}\right)\right].

Theorem 0.22.21
.

Let
X
be a finite CW-complex. The Chern character induces a well defined mapping

\mathrm{ch}:\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)\to H_{*}(X;\mathbb{Q}).

of
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded abelian groups. The Chern character is a rational isomorphism.

Sketch of proof.

It is clear from the definition that
\mathrm{ch}
respects the disjoint union/direct sum relation and degenerate equivalence (since
\mathrm{ch}(\xi)=0\in H^{\rm ev}_{c}(M;\mathbb{Q})
if
M
is degenerate). A standard computation with characteristic classes shows that if
V\to M
is an even rank spin
c
-vector bundle then

(p_{V})_{*}(\mathrm{ch}[\beta_{V}]\cap\mathrm{Td}(M^{V})\cap[M^{V}])=\mathrm{Td}(M)\cap[M],

so
\mathrm{ch}
is unaltered by vector bundle modification. Finally, if
(W,\xi,f)
is a cycle with boundary then

\displaystyle\mathrm{ch}(\partial(W,\xi,f))=

\displaystyle(f\circ\iota)_{*}(\mathrm{ch}(\xi|_{\partial W})\cap\mathrm{Td}(\partial W)\cap[\partial W])=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(f\circ\partial)_{*}(\mathrm{ch}(\xi)\cap\mathrm{Td}(W)\cap[W])=0,

where
\iota:\partial W\hookrightarrow W
denotes the boundary inclusion. Therefore
\mathrm{ch}
respects bordism. As such
\mathrm{ch}
is well defined. It follows that
\mathrm{ch}
is a rational isomorphism from Theorem
0.22.12
and the fact that
\mathrm{ch}:K_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)\to H_{*}(X;\mathbb{Q})
is a rational isomorphism.
∎

Remark 0.22.22
.

In
[
7
, Part 5]
, Baum-Douglas introduced what is now called the Baum-Douglas index problem. In its most general form, it is stated as follows. Let
X
be a finite CW-complex and
x\in K_{*}^{\rm an}(X)
a
K
-homology class. How to construct a geometric cycle
(M,E,f)
such that
\gamma[(M,E,f)]=x
? This problem was solved when
x
was the
K
-homology class defined from an elliptic pseudodifferential operator by Baum-Douglas
[
7
]
, thus giving a geometric proof of Atiyah-Singer’s index problem. The Baum-Douglas index problem played a pivotal role in Baum-van Erp’s solution
[
10
]
to the index problem for
H
-elliptic operators on contact manifolds. This line of thought has also been put to use in the study of secondary invariants and Higson-Roe’s analytic structure group
[
55
]
.

In light of the theory developed in this section, we can formulate a non-compact version of Baum-Douglas index problem. If
X
is a finite CW-complex and
x\in K_{*}^{\rm an}(X)
is a
K
-homology class, how to construct a geometric cycle with coefficients in elliptic complexes
(M,\xi,f)
such that
\tilde{\gamma}[(M,\xi,f)]=x
? The noncompact version of the Baum-Douglas index problem is weaker than the original Baum-Douglas index problem, but Lemma
0.22.8
describes how to go from a non-compact solution to a compact one.

0.23.
Dualities on Carnot manifolds

0.23.1.
Poincaré duality – geometric and analytic

We first study Poincaré duality on a compact manifold
X
. Analytic Poincare duality is an isomorphism

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}:K^{*}(T^{*}X)\rightarrow K_{*}(X),

that in rough terms reconstructs an elliptic pseudodifferential operator from the index class of its principal symbol in
K^{*}(T^{*}X)
. The construction could be framed in terms of Kasparov’s Poincaré duality or in terms of the tangent groupoid. We take the latter approach as a definition and state Kasparov’s index theorem relating the two.

Let
\mathbb{T}X\rightrightarrows X\times[0,\infty)
denote Connes’ tangent groupoid, see for instance
[
39
]
or Example
0.10.9
above. The kernel of the restriction mapping
C^{*}(\mathbb{T}X|_{X\times[0,1]}))\to C_{0}(T^{*}X)
is isomorphic to the contractible
C^{*}
-algebra
C_{0}((0,1],\mathbb{K}(L^{2}(X)))
. Therefore the induced restriction mapping

e_{0}:KK_{0}(C(X),C^{*}(\mathbb{T}X|_{X\times[0,1]}))\rightarrow KK_{0}(C(X),C_{0}(T^{*}X))

is an isomorphism.

For a
C(X)
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
, we note that, viewing
X
as a trivial groupoid, there is a natural isomorphism
A\rtimes C(X)\simeq A
. Define the natural transformation:

\alpha_{A}:KK(\mathbb{C},A)\rightarrow KK^{X}(\mathbb{C},A)\xrightarrow{\rtimes C(X)}KK^{X}(C(X),A)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{Forget}}KK(C(X),A).

Definition 0.23.1

(Analytic Poincare duality)
.

We define analytic Poincare duality

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}:K^{*}(T^{*}X)\rightarrow K_{*}^{\rm an}(X),

as the mapping

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}:=e_{1}\circ e_{0}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{C_{0}(T^{*}X)}.

Proposition 0.23.2
.

Assume that
D
is an elliptic differential pseudodifferential operator
C^{\infty}(X;E_{1})\to C^{\infty}(X;E_{2})
with principal symbol
\sigma(D)
. We let
\xi_{D}:=(p^{*}E_{1},p^{*}E_{2},\sigma(D))
denote the associated elliptic complex on
T^{*}X
, defining its index class
[\sigma(D)]:=[\xi_{D}]\in K^{0}(T^{*}X)
. Then it holds that

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}[\xi_{D}]=[D]\in K_{0}(X).

This proposition is well known. For a proof, one can apply Proposition
0.21.4
to the trivial filtration of
X
. We remark that any elliptic complex
\xi
on
T^{*}X
is equivalent to one of the form
\xi_{D}
for an elliptic pseudodifferential operator
D
. Therefore the property
\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}[\xi_{D}]=[D]
in Proposition
0.23.2
characterizes Poincaré duality in degree
0
.

Theorem 0.23.3

(Kasparov’s index theorem)
.

Let
X
be a compact manifold and let
[\bar{\partial}_{T^{*}X}]\in KK_{0}(C_{0}(T^{*}X),\mathbb{C})
denote the class of the Dolbeault-Dirac operator defined from the almost complex structure on
T^{*}X
. Then

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}(x)=\alpha_{C_{0}(T^{*}X)}(x)\otimes_{C_{0}(T^{*}X)}[\bar{\partial}_{T^{*}X}],\quad x\in K^{*}(T^{*}X).

The reader can find Kasparov’s index theorem as
[
95
, Theorem
5
]
or
[
41
, Section 12.3]
. Let us proceed by describing the geometric analogue of Poincaré duality.

Definition 0.23.4

(Geometric Poincaré duality)
.

We define geometric Poincare duality

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}:K^{*}(T^{*}X)\rightarrow\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X),

by setting

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}(\xi):=(T^{*}X,\xi,p),

for any elliptic complex
\xi
on
T^{*}X
. Here
p:T^{*}X\to X
denotes the projection map.

Remark 0.23.5
.

As Definition
0.23.4
stands,
\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}
is only defined
K^{0}(T^{*}X)\rightarrow\tilde{K}_{0}^{\rm geo}(X)
. For the odd case, we use that
K^{1}(T^{*}X)=K^{0}(T^{*}X\times\mathbb{R})
as the definition of odd
K
-theory in topological
K
-theory, and

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}:K^{1}(T^{*}X)\rightarrow\tilde{K}_{1}^{\rm geo}(X),

is defined by

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}(\xi):=(T^{*}X\times\mathbb{R},\xi,p),

for any elliptic complex
\xi
on
T^{*}X\times\mathbb{R}
.

It is clear that geometric Poincaré duality is well defined. Indeed, geometric Poincaré duality maps degenerate elliptic complexes to degenerate geometric cycles and homotopies of elliptic complexes to bordisms of geometric cycles.

Remark 0.23.6
.

Geometric Poincaré duality was in
[
10
]
defined using the ordinary Baum-Douglas model of geometric
K
-homology. Let us describe their construction and use Theorem
0.22.12
to reconcile the two definitions.

For a compact manifold
X
, consider
\Sigma X=S(T^{*}X\times 1_{\mathbb{R}})
, i.e. the unit sphere bundle of
T^{*}X\times 1_{\mathbb{R}}
in some metric. Since
\Sigma X
is a hypersurface in the stably almost complex manifold
T^{*}X\oplus 1_{\mathbb{R}}
,
\Sigma X
is a compact spin
c
-manifold. We have that
\dim(\Sigma X)=2\dim(X)
is even. Let
B^{*}X
be the unit ball bundle of
T^{*}X
and
S^{*}X
the unit sphere bundle of
T^{*}X
. Then we can write

\Sigma X=B^{*}X\cup_{S^{*}X}B^{*}X,

where we identify the first copy of
B^{*}X
with the upper hemisphere and the second copy of
B^{*}X
with the lower hemisphere. The role of
M_{0}
in Lemma
0.22.8
will be played by
B^{*}X
so
\Sigma X=Z
.

Let
\xi=(E_{1},E_{2},\sigma)
be a cycle of
K^{0}(T^{*}X)
. We note that since
T^{*}X
is homotopy equivalent to
X
, the bundles
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
can be assumed to be pulled back from
X
. Therefore the dilation action on the fibres of
T^{*}X\to X
lifts to
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
. By rescaling, we can assume that the support of
\sigma
is contained in the interior of
B^{*}X
. For
M_{0}=B^{*}X
in Lemma
0.22.8
, the bundle
E_{\sigma}
over
\Sigma X=Z
is exactly

E_{\xi}=E_{1}\cap_{\sigma}E_{2}.

The geometric Poincare duality map of
[
10
]
is

K^{0}(T^{*}X)\rightarrow K_{0}^{\rm geo}(X),\ \xi\mapsto(\Sigma X,E_{\xi},p).

Therefore, the geometric Poincare duality map of
[
10
]
is compatible with the geometric Poincaré duality map of Definition
0.23.4
under the isomorphism of Theorem
0.22.12
.

Theorem 0.23.7
.

Let
X
