be a compact manifold. Then all the morphisms in the following diagram are isomorphisms and the diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{*}(T^{*}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}^{\rm an}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\gamma}}

The reader can find a proof of this result in
[
12
]
. The result in
[
12
]
was stated in terms of
K_{0}^{\rm geo}(X)
and ordinary geometric cycles, but the results in the two formulations are equivalent by Theorem
0.22.19
.

0.23.2.
Poincaré duality and Carnot manifolds

We now turn to studying Poincaré dualities on Carnot manifolds. We proceed as in Subsection
0.23.1
.

Lemma 0.23.8
.

The restriction mapping

e_{0}:KK_{0}(C(X),C^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{H}X|_{X\times[0,1]}))\rightarrow KK_{0}(C(X),C^{*}(T_{H}X))

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The kernel of
e_{0}
is
C^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{H}X|_{X\times(0,1]})\cong C_{0}((0,\infty),\mathbb{K}(L^{2}(X)))
, which is contractible.
∎

Definition 0.23.9

(Analytic Poincare
H
duality)
.

We define analytic Poincare
H
duality

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}_{H}:K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))\rightarrow K_{*}^{\rm an}(X),

as the mapping

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}_{H}:=e_{1}\circ e_{0}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{C^{*}(T_{H}X)}.

We note the following consequence of Proposition
0.21.4
. Recall the definition of the symbol class
[\sigma_{H}(D)]\in K_{0}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
of an
H
-elliptic operator from Definition
0.21.1
.

Proposition 0.23.10
.

Let
X
be a compact Carnot manifold. Assume that
D
is an
H
-elliptic differential pseudodifferential operator
C^{\infty}(X;E_{1})\to C^{\infty}(X;E_{2})
. Then it holds that

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}_{H}[\sigma_{H}(D)]=[D]\in K_{0}(X).

To describe the geometric analogue of Poincaré duality on a Carnot manifold we need to assume
\boldsymbol{F}
-regularity. The reader is encouraged to revisit Example
0.22.3
and the construction of the metaplectic correction
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})
of a a bundle of flat representations
\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{X}
in Section
0.14
.

Definition 0.23.11

(Geometric Poincaré
H
duality)
.

Let
X
be an
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold and choose a bundle of flat representations
\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{X}
. We define geometric Poincaré
H
duality

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}:K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))\rightarrow\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X),

as follows. For
*=0
, we set

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}(x):=(\Gamma_{X},(p\mathcal{H},q\mathcal{H},qup)\otimes\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H}),p_{\Gamma}),

where
x\in K_{0}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
has been lifted to a pre-image in
K_{0}(I_{X})
represented by a relative
K
-theory cycle
(p,q,u)
as in Example
0.22.3
. Here
p_{\Gamma}:\Gamma_{X}\to X
denotes the projection map.

For
*=1
, we set

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}(x):=(\Gamma_{X}^{\partial},(p\mathcal{H},q\mathcal{H},qup)\otimes\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H}),p_{\Gamma}),

where
x\in K_{1}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
has been lifted to a pre-image in
K_{0}(I_{X}^{\partial})
represented by a relative
K
-theory cycle
(p,q,u)
as in Example
0.22.3
but over
\Gamma_{X}^{\partial}
.

Theorem 0.23.12
.

Let
X
be a compact
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold. Then the geometric Poincaré
H
duality
\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}:K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))\rightarrow\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
is a well defined map making the following diagram commutative

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}(C^{*}(TX))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}}

Proof.

Consider the map
\beta:K^{*}(\Gamma_{X})\to\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)
,
\xi\mapsto(\Gamma_{X},\xi,p_{\Gamma})
and the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}(I_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{*}(\Gamma_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{*}(\Xi_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{*}(T^{*}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{*}(\Gamma_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes\mathcal{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\Xi}^{X}!}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\Xi}^{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

(56)

By definition, we have that

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}\circ j_{*}=\beta([\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})]\otimes\cdot).

Therefore, the theorem follows from the commutativity of the diagram (
56
). The bottom right square commutes due to the same general argument as in
[
10
, Lemma 2.7.4]
. The upper (irregular) hexagon commutes by Theorem
0.14.11
.
∎

We can now conclude some computations of geometric Poincaré
H
dual classes to symbol classes from the computations of Section
0.21
. From Corollary
0.21.13
the following proposition follows.

Proposition 0.23.13
.

Let
X
be a compact regular polycontact manifold with polycontact structure
H
, equipped with a Riemannian metric
g
. Assume that
D_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{DO}_{H}^{2}(X;E)
is as in Equation (
43
) and is
H
-elliptic. For
N>>0
, we have that

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}[\sigma_{H}(D_{\gamma})]=\left[\left(S(H^{\perp})\times S^{1},E_{\gamma,N},p\right)\right],

where
p:S(H^{\perp})\times S^{1}\to X
denotes the projection map and
E_{\gamma,N}\to S(H^{\perp})\times S^{1}
is obtained from clutching
(\oplus_{k\leq N}(p^{*}H)^{\otimes^{\rm sym}_{\mathbb{C}}k}\otimes E)\times[0,1]\to S(H^{\perp})\times[0,1]
along
\oplus_{k\leq N}\gamma_{k}
at the boundary (where
\gamma_{k}
is as in Proposition
0.20.21
).

From Corollary
0.21.15
the following proposition follows.

Proposition 0.23.14
.

Let
X
be a compact regular polycontact manifold with polycontact structure
H
, equipped with a Riemannian metric
g
. Assume that
P\in\Psi_{H}^{0}(X;E)
is an idempotent Hermite operator. Then

\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}[\sigma_{H}(2P-1)]=\left[\left(S(H^{\perp}),\sigma_{H}(P)(\oplus_{k}(p^{*}H)^{\otimes^{\rm sym}_{\mathbb{C}}k}\otimes E),p\right)\right],

where
p:S(H^{\perp})\to X
denotes the projection and
\sigma_{H}(P)(\oplus_{k}(p^{*}H)^{\otimes^{\rm sym}_{\mathbb{C}}k}\otimes E)\to S(H^{\perp})
is the vector bundle obtained from the range of the Heisenberg symbol of
P
.

0.23.3.
The commuting tetraeder

We now come to the main
K
-theoretical result of this part of the work. It relates the different dualities from the last section to the map
\psi:K^{*}(T^{*}X)\to K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
constructed in Theorem
0.13.9

Theorem 0.23.15
.

Let
X
be a compact
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold. Then all morphism in the following diagram are isomorphisms and the diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{*}(T^{*}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{PD}_{H}^{\rm geo}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{PD}_{H}^{\rm an}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\gamma}}

If
X
is a Carnot manifold which is not necessarily
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular, the diagram above with the upper left arrow removed only consists of isomorphisms and commutes.

The bottom triangle commutes by Theorem
0.23.7
with all maps being isomorphisms, with no assumption or reference to Carnot structures. The left upper triangle commutes by Theorem
0.23.12
– commutativity of this part of the diagram requires
\boldsymbol{F}
-regularity. If the diagram in Theorem
0.23.15
commutes, the fact that the bottom triangle commutes with all maps being isomorphisms and that
\psi
is an isomorphism (see Theorem
0.13.9
) implies that all of the morphisms are isomorphisms. We shall below in Theorem
0.23.16
prove that the right upper triangle commutes with no assumption of regularity on the Carnot structure.

Theorem 0.23.16
.

Let
X
be a compact Carnot manifold. Then the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{*}(T^{*}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}^{\rm an}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{PD}_{H}^{\rm an}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

Theorem
0.23.16
was proven for contact manifolds in
[
10
, Theorem 5.4.1]
. Our proof follows a similar line of thought.

Proof.

Recall the construction of the adiabatic parabolic tangent groupoid
\mathbb{A}_{H}X
of
X
from Section
0.17
.
The following diagram is commutative

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathbb{A}_{H}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathbb{T}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{H}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(X\times X),}

where the maps are the appropriate evaluations at
1
.
Therefore, we have the following commutative diagram in
KK

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KK_{*}(C(X),T^{*}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KK_{*}(C(X),C^{*}(\mathbb{T}X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KK_{*}(C(X),C^{*}(TX))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KK_{*}(C(X),C^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{H}X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KK_{*}(C(X),C^{*}(T_{H}X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KK_{*}(C(X),C^{*}((T_{H}X)_{\rm adb}))}

Thus the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KK_{*}(C(X),C^{*}(TX))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KK_{*}(C(X),C^{*}(T_{H}X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{*}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

(57)

Since
\alpha
is a natural transformation, the diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KK_{*}(\mathbb{C},C^{*}(T_{H}X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KK_{*}(\mathbb{C},C^{*}(TX))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KK_{*}(C(X),C^{*}(T_{H}X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KK_{*}(C(X),C^{*}(TX))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{C^{*}(T_{H}X)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{C^{*}(TX)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

(58)

Combining (
57
) with (
58
) we arrive at the conclusion of the theorem.
∎

0.24.
Index theorems for
H
-elliptic operators

In this section we conclude abstract index formulas on a compact Carnot manifold from the commuting tetraeder in Theorem
0.23.15
. We note that using the formalism of Section
0.21
, the index theorems of this section extends ad verbatim to graded
H
-elliptic operators.

Theorem 0.24.1
.

Let
X
be a compact Carnot manifold. Assume that
D:C^{\infty}(X;E_{1})\to C^{\infty}(X;E_{2})
is an
H
-elliptic pseudodifferential operator. Then the Baum-Douglas index problem for
[D]
is solved by
\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}(\psi^{-1}[\sigma_{H}(D)])
.

Proof.

By Proposition
0.23.10
,
[D]=\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}_{H}[\sigma_{H}(D)]
and by Theorem
0.23.15
,

\tilde{\gamma}(\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}(\psi^{-1}[\sigma_{H}(D)]))=\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}_{H}[\sigma_{H}(D)].

∎

The next index formula which Theorem
0.24.1
implies has already been studied in the literature, see
[
66
,
67
,
112
]
.

Corollary 0.24.2
.

Let
X
be a compact Carnot manifold. Assume that
D:C^{\infty}(X;E_{1})\to C^{\infty}(X;E_{2})
is an
H
-elliptic pseudodifferential operator. Then it holds that

\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(D)=\int_{T^{*}X}\mathrm{ch}(\psi^{-1}[\sigma_{H}(D)])\wedge\mathrm{Td}(X).

Remark 0.24.3
.

The reader can revisit Subsection
0.19.2
for more details on the analytic interpretation of
\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(D)
. Indeed, by Theorem
0.19.24
, the index
\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(D)
is the same when interpreting as the index of
D
acting on Sobolev spaces, or as a closed operator on
L^{2}
or as when acting on
C^{\infty}
.

Theorem 0.24.4
.

Let
X
be a compact
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold. Assume that
D:C^{\infty}(X;E_{1})\to C^{\infty}(X;E_{2})
is an
H
-elliptic pseudodifferential operator. Then the Baum-Douglas index problem for
[D]
is solved by
\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}([\sigma_{H}(D)])
.

Proof.

By Proposition
0.23.10
,
[D]=\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}_{H}[\sigma_{H}(D)]
and by Theorem
0.23.15
,

\tilde{\gamma}(\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}([\sigma_{H}(D)]))=\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}_{H}[\sigma_{H}(D)].

∎

From Theorem
0.24.4
, and the definition of
\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}
(see Definition
0.23.11
), we conclude the following.

Corollary 0.24.5
.

Let
X
be a compact
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold and choose a bundle of flat representations
\mathcal{H}\to\Gamma_{X}
. Assume that
D:C^{\infty}(X;E_{1})\to C^{\infty}(X;E_{2})
is an
H
-elliptic pseudodifferential operator. Suppose that
[\sigma_{H}(D)]\in K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
is the image under
j:I_{X}\to C^{*}(T_{H}X)
of a relative
K
-theory cycle
(p,q,u)
for
I_{X}
with
gp,gq\in I_{X}
for all
g\in C_{0}(\Gamma_{X})
. Then the Baum-Douglas index problem for
[D]
is solved by the geometric cycle with coefficients in the ellliptic complexes

(\Gamma_{X},(p\mathcal{H},q\mathcal{H},qup)\otimes\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H}),p_{\Gamma}).

In this case,

\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(D)=\int_{\Gamma_{X}}\mathrm{ch}[(p\mathcal{H},q\mathcal{H},qup)]\wedge\mathrm{e}^{c_{1}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})}\wedge\mathrm{Td}(\Gamma_{X}).

The reader can return to Section
0.14
for further details on the construction of the bundle of flat representations
\mathcal{H}
and its metaplectic correction line bundle
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H})
. Further details on the symbol class
[\sigma_{H}(D)]\in K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
and the construction of pre-images under
j:I_{X}\to C^{*}(T_{H}X)
as a relative
K
-theory cycle
(p,q,u)
can be found in Section
0.21
.

Theorem 0.24.6
.

Let
X
be a compact
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold. Then there is a Chern character

\mathrm{ch}_{H}:K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))\to H^{*}_{\rm dR}(X),

defined from

\mathrm{ch}_{H}(j_{*}x):=(p_{\Gamma})_{*}\left(\mathrm{ch}(x\otimes\mathcal{H})\cup\mathrm{e}^{c_{1}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H}))+\frac{1}{2}c_{1}(\Gamma_{X})}\right),

for
x\in K_{*}(I_{X})
. For
y\in K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
, we have that

\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,_{T_{H}X}(y)=\int_{X}\mathrm{ch}_{H}(y)\wedge\hat{A}(X).

Proof.

The map
\mathrm{ch}_{H}
is well defined since
j_{*}:K_{*}(I_{X})\to K_{*}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
is surjective and by the Riemann-Roch theorem (cf.
[
32
, Section 5]
) it satisfies
(\mathrm{ch}_{H}(y)\cup\hat{A}(X))\cap[X]=\mathrm{ch}_{*}^{\rm geo}(\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}(y))
where
[X]
denotes the fundamental class in homology and
\mathsf{PD}^{\rm geo}_{H}:K_{*}^{\rm geo}(X)\to H_{*}(X)
denotes the geometric Chern character in homology.
∎

The index theorems above hold in the generality of
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifolds. Let us utilize the
K
-theory computations of Section
0.21
in two concrete examples.

0.24.1.
Example: A Boutet de Monvel type index theorem

The following index theorem follows from Proposition
0.23.14
and Theorem
0.24.4
. It extends an index formula of
[
21
]
from boundaries of strictly convex domains in complex manifolds, and the results of
[
65
]
from co-oriented contact manifolds, to polycontact manifolds.

Theorem 0.24.7
.

Let
X
be a compact, regular, polycontact manifold with polycontact structure
H
. Assume that
P\in\Psi_{H}^{0}(X;E)
is an idempotent Hermite operator. We write
[P]\in K_{1}^{\rm an}(X)
for the
K
-homology class of
(L^{2}(X;E),2P-1)
. Let
p:S(H^{\perp})\to X
denote the projection. Set
\mathpzc{F}_{X}:=\bigoplus_{k}(p^{*}H)^{\otimes^{\rm sym}_{\mathbb{C}}k}
.

Then
(S(H^{\perp}),\sigma_{H}(P)(\mathpzc{F}_{X}\otimes E),p)
solves the Baum-Douglas index problem for
[P]
. Moreover, for any
u\in C(X,GL_{N}(\mathbb{C}))
the Toeplitz operator

T_{u}:=(P\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}^{N}})u(P\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}^{N}}):PL^{2}(X;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{N})\to PL^{2}(X;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{N}),

is Fredholm and its index is

\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(T_{u})=\int_{S(H^{\perp})}\mathrm{ch}[p^{*}u]\wedge\mathrm{ch}(\sigma_{H}(P)(\mathpzc{F}_{X}\otimes E))\wedge\mathrm{Td}(S(H^{\perp})).

The reader should note that by Theorem
0.19.19
, recalled from
[
148
]
, there is a plethora of idempotent Hermite operators on polycontact manifolds.

Example 0.24.8
.

Suppose that
X
is a compact, co-oriented contact manifold and
P\in\Psi^{0}_{H}(X)
is a Szegö projection, i.e. that
\sigma^{0}_{(x,\pi)}(P)
is the ground state projection if
\pi
is a non-abelian representation with positive central character and
\sigma^{0}_{(x,\pi)}(P)=0
otherwise. In this case,
\sigma_{H}(P)\mathpzc{F}_{X}
is the trivial line bundle over the component
X\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}\subseteq\Gamma_{X}
. Theorem
0.24.7
ensures that

(\Gamma_{X}^{\partial},\sigma_{H}(P)(\mathpzc{F}_{X}),p)\sim_{\rm BD}(X,X\times\mathbb{C},\mathrm{id}),

solves the Baum-Douglas index problem for
[P]\in K_{1}^{\rm an}(X)
. This recovers the classical Boutet de Monvel index theorem
[
21
]
(see
[
65
]
for the general co-oriented contact case):

\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(T_{u})=\int_{X}\mathrm{ch}[u]\wedge\mathrm{Td}(X).

For a more direct proof, see
[
13
]
. We also have the following generalization.

Recall the notion of a generalized Szegö projection at level
N
from
[
65
, Chapter 6]
. See more in Example
0.19.18
above or further examples in
[
22
, Chapter 15.3]
or
[
64
]
.

Corollary 0.24.9
.

Let
X
be a compact, co-oriented contact manifold and
P_{N}\in\Psi_{H}^{0}(X,\mathbb{C})
a generalized Szegö projection at level
N
. The index problem for
[P_{N}]
is solved by the geometric cycle

(\Gamma_{X}^{\partial},\sigma_{H}(P_{N})(\mathpzc{F}_{X}),p)=(X,\oplus_{k=0}^{N}H^{\otimes^{\rm sym}_{\mathbb{C}}k},\mathrm{id}).

In particular, for any
u\in C(X,GL_{q}(\mathbb{C}))
the Toeplitz operator

T_{u}:=(P_{N}\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}^{q}})u(P_{N}\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}^{q}}):P_{N}L^{2}(X;\mathbb{C}^{q})\to P_{N}L^{2}(X;\mathbb{C}^{q}),

is Fredholm and its index is

\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(T_{u})=\sum_{k=0}^{N}\int_{X}\mathrm{ch}[u]\wedge\mathrm{ch}(H^{\otimes^{\rm sym}_{\mathbb{C}}k})\wedge\mathrm{Td}(X).

Let us solve the index problem for when
X
is an arbitrary contact manifold, not necessarily co-oriented. Take a projection
P\in\Psi^{0}_{H}(X)
with
\sigma^{0}_{(x,\pi)}(P)
being the ground state projection for all non-abelian representations
\pi
. See more in Example
0.19.18
above. Also here we use the Fock bundle
\mathpzc{F}_{X}:=\oplus_{k=0}^{\infty}(p^{*}H)^{\otimes^{\rm sym}_{\mathbb{C}}k},\to\Gamma_{X}
, defined from the complex structure on
p^{*}H\to\Gamma_{X}
induced from the Kirillov form. In this case,
\sigma_{H}(P)\mathpzc{F}_{X}
is the trivial line bundle on
\Gamma_{X}
.

Corollary 0.24.10
.

For a compact contact manifold
X
and
P\in\Psi^{0}_{H}(X)
a projection as in the preceding paragraph. The index problem for
[P]\in K_{1}^{\rm an}(X)
is solved by the geometric cycle

(\Gamma_{X}^{\partial},\sigma_{H}(P)(\mathpzc{F}_{X}),p)=(\Gamma_{X}^{\partial},\Gamma_{X}^{\partial}\times\mathbb{C},p).

In particular, for any
u\in C(X,GL_{q}(\mathbb{C}))
the Toeplitz operator

T_{u}:=(P\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}^{q}})u(P\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}^{q}}):PL^{2}(X;\mathbb{C}^{q})\to PL^{2}(X;\mathbb{C}^{q}),

is Fredholm and its index is

\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(T_{u})=\int_{\Gamma_{X}^{\partial}}\mathrm{ch}[p^{*}u]\wedge\mathrm{Td}(\Gamma_{X}^{\partial}).

Moreover,
[P]=0
if
X
is co-oriented.

Proof.

Theorem
0.24.7
provides the solution for the index problem and the index formula for Toeplitz operators is immediate. Concerning the vanishing in the co-oriented case, if
X
is co-oriented, the co-orientation ensures that
\Gamma_{X}=\Gamma_{X}^{+}\dot{\cup}\Gamma_{X}^{-}
, where we can identify
\Gamma_{X}^{\pm}=X\times\mathbb{R}_{\pm}
. The complex structure on
p^{*}H|_{\Gamma_{X}^{+}}\to\Gamma_{X}^{+}
descends to
H\to X
, and
X
is equipped with the spin
c
-structure induced from the stably almost complex structure (using
TX\cong H\oplus\mathbb{R}
). We note that
\Xi_{X}\to\Gamma_{X}
can be identitfed with
p^{*}H\to\Gamma_{X}
, and so the spin
c
-structure on
\Gamma_{X}
is the one making the identification defined from the co-orientation

\Gamma_{X}=(X\times\mathbb{R}_{+})\dot{\cup}(\overline{X}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}),

spin
c
-preserving. Therefore, the class
[P]\in K_{1}^{\rm an}(X)
admits the geometric solution
(X,X\times\mathbb{C},\mathrm{id})\dot{\cup}(\overline{X},X\times\mathbb{C},\mathrm{id})
which is nullbordant. In particular,
[P]=0\in K_{1}^{\rm an}(X)
if
X
is co-oriented.
∎

0.24.2.
Example: Baum-van Erp type operators for polycontact manifolds

The following index theorem follows from Proposition
0.23.13
and Theorem
0.24.4
. It extends an index formula of
[
10
]
from co-oriented contact manifolds to polycontact manifolds.

Theorem 0.24.11
.

Let
X
be a compact regular polycontact manifold with polycontact structure
H
, equipped with a Riemannian metric
g
. Assume that
D_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{DO}_{H}^{2}(X;E)
is as in Equation (
43
) and is
H
-elliptic. For a sufficiently large
N>>0
, the index problem for
[D_{\gamma}]
is solved by the geometric cycle

\left(S(H^{\perp})\times S^{1},E_{\gamma,N},p\right),

where
p:S(H^{\perp})\times S^{1}\to X
denotes the projection map and
E_{\gamma,N}\to S(H^{\perp})\times S^{1}
is obtained from clutching
(\oplus_{k\leq N}(p^{*}H)^{\otimes^{\rm sym}_{\mathbb{C}}k}\otimes E)\times[0,1]\to S(H^{\perp})\times[0,1]
along
\oplus_{k\leq N}\gamma_{k}
at the boundary (where
\gamma_{k}
is as in Proposition
0.20.21
).

Recall that
H
-ellipticity of
D_{\gamma}
was characterized in Theorem
0.19.11
, see also
[
85
, Theorem 1.1]
.

Example 0.24.12
.

Let us compute an example with co-oriented contact manifolds in order to explain the two spin
c
-structures in Baum-van Erp’s index formula
[
10
]
for operators of the form
D_{\gamma}
on a co-oriented contact manifold. In this case, we have for any relative
K
-theory cycle
(p,q,u)
as in Example
0.22.3
that

\displaystyle(\Gamma_{X},(p\mathcal{H},q\mathcal{H},qup)

\displaystyle\otimes\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H}),p)\sim_{\rm BD}

\displaystyle\sim_{\rm BD}

\displaystyle((0,\infty)\times X,(p\mathcal{H}_{+},q\mathcal{H}_{+},qup),p)+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+((-\infty,0)\times X,(p\mathcal{H}_{-},q\mathcal{H}_{-},qup),p).

The elliptic complexes
(p\mathcal{H}_{\pm},q\mathcal{H}_{\pm},qup)
correspond to classes
[u_{\pm}]\in K^{1}(X)
under the Bott isomorphism or to
K
-theory classes
[E_{\pm}]-N[1]
in
K^{0}(X\times S^{1})
. We can conclude that

\displaystyle(\Gamma_{X},(p\mathcal{H},q\mathcal{H},qup)

\displaystyle\otimes\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{H}),p)\sim_{\rm BD}

\displaystyle\sim_{\rm BD}

\displaystyle(X\times S^{1},E_{+},p_{1})+(-(X\times S^{1}),E_{-},p_{1}),

in
\tilde{K}_{1}^{\rm geo}(X)
, where
p_{1}:X\times S^{1}\to X
denotes projection onto the first factor. We note here the additional sign that is coming from the difference in signs of orientation and spin
c
-structure on
(0,\infty)
versus that of
(-\infty,0)
.

We can give an example of an index result for a differential operator
D_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{DO}_{H}^{2}(X;E)
, analogous to that in Theorem
0.24.11
, for a four-dimensional manifold
X
with an Engel structure, see Example
0.9.22
and
0.16.9
. If
X
is a four-manifold, we say that a pair of vector fields
(Y_{1},Y_{2})
generate an Engel structure if
Y_{1},Y_{2},[Y_{1},Y_{2}],[Y_{1},[Y_{1},Y_{2}]]
span
TX
in all points. By results of Vogel
[
152
]
, existence of a pair generating an Engel structure is equivalent to
X
being parallelizable. We equipp
X
with the depth three Carnot structure where
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
are sections of
T^{-1}X
, and
[Y_{1},Y_{2}]
is a section of
T^{-2}X
. We conclude the following index result from work of Helffer
[
79
]
. We use the notation

S_{0}:=\{\lambda\in\mathbb{C}:\mathrm{Re}(\lambda)\neq 0\mbox{ or }|\mathrm{Im}(\lambda)|<1/2\}.

Theorem 0.24.13
.

Let
X
be a compact, four-dimensional Carnot manifold with the depth three Carnot structure defined from a pair
(Y_{1},Y_{2})
generating an Engel structure. Assume that
E\to X
is a hermitean vector bundle with connection
\nabla
and
\gamma\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{End}(E))
. Consider a differential operator
D_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{DO}_{H}^{2}(X;E)
such that

D_{\gamma}=\nabla_{Y_{1}}^{*}\nabla_{Y_{1}}+\nabla_{Y_{2}}^{*}\nabla_{Y_{2}}+\gamma\nabla_{[Y_{1},Y_{2}]}+\mathcal{DO}_{H}^{1}(X;E).

Then
D_{\gamma}
is
H
-elliptic if and only if
\gamma-\lambda:E\to E
is a vector bundle isomorphism for any
\lambda\in S_{0}
. In this case, it holds that

\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(D_{\gamma})=0.

Proof.

The
H
-ellipticity of
D_{\gamma}
was characterized by Helffer
[
79
]
. From the characterization of
H
-ellipticity and the fact that
S_{0}
is invariant under rescaling by
t\in[0,1]
, we can construct a path
(D_{t\gamma})_{t\in[0,1]}
with
D_{1\gamma}=D_{\gamma}
and
D_{0\gamma}=\nabla_{Y_{1}}^{*}\nabla_{Y_{1}}+\nabla_{Y_{2}}^{*}\nabla_{Y_{2}}
. From self-adjointness of
D_{0\gamma}
, it is clear that

\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(D_{\gamma})=\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(D_{1,\gamma})=\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(D_{0,\gamma})=0.

∎

Remark 0.24.14
.

The results of
[
79
]
holds in larger generality, and applies to operators of the form
D_{\gamma}=\sum_{j}\nabla_{Y_{j}}^{2}+\sum_{j,k}\gamma_{j,k}\nabla_{[Y_{j},Y_{k}]}+\mathcal{DO}_{H}^{1}(X;E)
for suitable bracket generating vector fields
(Y_{j})_{j}
. In particular, it is of interest to ask if
\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,(D_{\gamma})=0
for large classes of Carnot manifolds whose oscullating Lie algebra has step length
>2
in all points.

0.25.
An outlook on graded Rockland sequences and their index theory

By virtue of the BGG-complexes, there are several graded Rockland sequences of interest in geometry. See Subsubsection
0.18.1
and Example
0.19.16
above or
[
28
,
45
,
46
]
. While the (curved) BGG-complexes, at least for a flat connection, is constructed from a quasi-isomorphism of complexes its index is often computable. However, in light of usage of BGG-like complexes in the Baum-Connes conjecture
[
91
,
92
,
93
,
153
]
, it is of interest to understand the
K
-homological aspects of the associated classes. Here we present some relevant features deducible from abstract principles. Explicit results, both in the abstract and in examples, will be left to future work.

0.25.1.
The symbol class of a Rockland sequence

To set up the symbol class, we shall use a model of
K
-theory akin to Atiyah’s notion of an elliptic complex. The model is not new, but we have not been able to locate a reference. The reader can compare the model to Higson-Roe’s notion of a Hilbert-Poincaré complex (see
[
81
]
).

Definition 0.25.1
.

Let
B
be a
C^{*}
-algebra and assume that

\displaystyle 0\to\mathpzc{E}_{1}\xrightarrow{d_{1}}\mathpzc{E}_{2}\xrightarrow{d_{2}}\cdots\to\mathpzc{E}_{N}\xrightarrow{d_{N}}\mathpzc{E}_{N+1}\to 0,

\displaystyle 0\leftarrow\mathpzc{E}_{1}\xleftarrow{b_{1}}\mathpzc{E}_{2}\xleftarrow{b_{2}}\cdots\leftarrow\mathpzc{E}_{N}\xleftarrow{b_{N}}\mathpzc{E}_{N+1}\leftarrow 0,

are two sequences of
B
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules with adjointable maps between. We say that this data
(\mathpzc{E}_{\bullet},d_{\bullet},b_{\bullet})
is a
B
-Fredholm sequence of length
N+1
if it holds that

b_{j}d_{j}+d_{j-1}b_{j-1}-1\in\mathbb{K}_{B}(\mathpzc{E}_{j}),

for all
j
(for
b_{j}=0
and
d_{j}=0
if
j\notin\{1,\ldots,N+1\}
). If
(\mathpzc{E}_{\bullet},d_{\bullet},b_{\bullet})
is a
B
-Fredholm sequence with

b_{j}d_{j}+d_{j-1}b_{j-1}=1,

for all
j
, we say that
(\mathpzc{E}_{\bullet},d_{\bullet},b_{\bullet})
is acyclic.

Two
B
-Fredholm sequences of length
N+1
are said to be isomorphic if there are unitary isomorphisms of the
B
-modules intertwining the operators
d_{\bullet}
and
b_{\bullet}
.

The reader should note that prescribing a length
2

B
-Fredholm sequence is the same prescribing a
B
-Fredholm map
\mathpzc{E}_{1}\to\mathpzc{E}_{2}
and an inverse modulo compact operators. There is a notion of direct sum of
B
-Fredholm sequences

(\mathpzc{E}_{\bullet},d_{\bullet},b_{\bullet})+(\mathpzc{E}_{\bullet}^{\prime},d_{\bullet}^{\prime},b_{\bullet}^{\prime})=(\mathpzc{E}_{\bullet}\oplus\mathpzc{E}^{\prime}_{\bullet},d_{\bullet}\oplus d_{\bullet}^{\prime},b_{\bullet}\oplus b_{\bullet}^{\prime}).

We say that two
B
-Fredholm sequences
(\mathpzc{E}_{\bullet},d_{\bullet},b_{\bullet})
and
(\mathpzc{E}_{\bullet}^{\prime},d_{\bullet}^{\prime},b_{\bullet}^{\prime})
are homotopic if there is a
C([0,1],B)
-Fredholm sequence
(\tilde{\mathpzc{E}}_{\bullet},\tilde{d}_{\bullet},\tilde{b}_{\bullet})
such that

(\tilde{\mathpzc{E}}_{\bullet},\tilde{d}_{\bullet},\tilde{b}_{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathrm{ev}_{0}}B\cong(\mathpzc{E}_{\bullet},d_{\bullet},b_{\bullet})\quad\mbox{and}\quad(\tilde{\mathpzc{E}}_{\bullet},\tilde{d}_{\bullet},\tilde{b}_{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathrm{ev}_{1}}B\cong(\mathpzc{E}^{\prime}_{\bullet},d^{\prime}_{\bullet},b^{\prime}_{\bullet})

For notational convenience, we also write

0\to\mathpzc{E}_{1}{}_{d_{1}}\leftrightarrows^{b_{1}}\mathpzc{E}_{2}{}_{d_{2}}\leftrightarrows^{b_{2}}\cdots\leftrightarrows\mathpzc{E}_{N}{}_{d_{N}}\leftrightarrows^{b_{N}}\mathpzc{E}_{N+1}\to 0,

for a
B
-Fredholm sequence
(\mathpzc{E}_{\bullet},d_{\bullet},b_{\bullet})
.

Definition 0.25.2
.

For a
C^{*}
-algebra
B
, let
\tilde{K}_{0}(B)
denote the set of equivalence classes of
B
-Fredholm sequence under the equivalence relation generated by homotopy and declaring acyclic
B
-Fredholm sequences equivalent to the zero complex.

Theorem 0.25.3
.

For a
C^{*}
-algebra
B
, the mapping

\displaystyle KK_{0}(\mathbb{C},B)

\displaystyle\to\tilde{K}_{0}(B),

\displaystyle\left[\left(\begin{matrix}\mathpzc{E}_{+}\\
\oplus\\
\mathpzc{E}_{-}\end{matrix},\begin{pmatrix}0&F_{+}\\
F_{-}&0\end{pmatrix}\right)\right]\mapsto\left[\left(0\to\mathpzc{E}_{-}{}_{F_{+}}\leftrightarrows^{F_{-}}\mathpzc{E}_{+}\to 0\right)\right],

is a natural isomorphism of abelian groups.

Sketch of proof.

The proof of this theorem is by standard techniques, so we only sketch it. We note that the mapping is well defined since it maps homotopies to homotopies. To show that the mapping is bijective, an inverse is constructed as follows. If
(\mathpzc{E}_{\bullet},d_{\bullet},b_{\bullet})
is a
B
-Fredholm sequence, one sets
\mathpzc{E}_{+}:=\bigoplus_{k}\mathpzc{E}_{2k}
,
\mathpzc{E}_{-}:=\bigoplus_{k}\mathpzc{E}_{2k+1}
and
F_{0}:=\sum d_{2k+1}+b_{2k}:\mathpzc{E}_{-}\to\mathpzc{E}_{+}
. The operator
F_{0}
is
B
-Fredholm, with inverse modulo compacts of the form
\sum d_{2k}+b_{2k+1}
. Functional calculus techniques applied to
F_{0}
produces an almost unitary
F_{+}:\mathpzc{E}_{-}\to\mathpzc{E}_{+}
homotopic to
F_{0}
in the space of
B
-Fredholm operators. Modulo homotopy and acyclic
B
-Fredholm sequences, this procedure produces an inverse to the mapping in the theorem.
∎

Corollary 0.25.4
.

The mapping

\tilde{K}_{0}(B)\to K_{0}(B),\quad(\mathpzc{E}_{\bullet},d_{\bullet},b_{\bullet})\mapsto\mathrm{i}\mathrm{n}\mathrm{d}\,\left(\sum d_{2k+1}+b_{2k}:\bigoplus_{k}\mathpzc{E}_{2k+1}\to\bigoplus_{k}\mathpzc{E}_{2k}\right),

is an isomorphism of abelian groups.

We are now in a position to define the symbol class in
K
-theory of a graded Rockland sequence, recall its definition from Section
0.19.16
.

Definition 0.25.5
.

Consider a graded Rockland sequence

0\to C^{\infty}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1})\xrightarrow{D_{1}}C^{\infty}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{2})\xrightarrow{D_{2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{D_{N-1}}C^{\infty}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{N})\xrightarrow{D_{N}}C^{\infty}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{N+1})\to 0,

of order
\boldsymbol{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{N})
. Let
d_{j}\in\tilde{\Sigma}^{m_{j}}_{H,{\rm gr}}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{j},\boldsymbol{E}_{j+1})
be lifts of
\sigma_{H,{\rm gr}}^{m_{j}}(D_{j})
and
b_{j}\in\tilde{\Sigma}^{-m_{j}}_{H,{\rm gr}}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{j+1},\boldsymbol{E}_{j})
be lifts of
\sigma_{H,{\rm gr}}^{m_{j}}(B_{j})
, where
B_{j}\in\Psi_{H,{\rm gr}}^{-m_{j}}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{j+1},\boldsymbol{E}_{j})
is as in Theorem
0.20.6
.

Letting
D_{\bullet}
denote the Rockland sequence, we define the symbol class

[\sigma_{H}(D_{\bullet})]\in K_{0}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)),

as the image under the isomorphism in Corollary
0.25.4
of the class of the
C^{*}(T_{H}X)
-Fredholm sequence:

\displaystyle 0\to

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s_{1}}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1})_{t=0}{}_{d_{1}}\leftrightarrows^{b_{1}}\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s_{2}}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{2})_{t=0}{}_{d_{2}}\leftrightarrows^{b_{2}}\cdots

(59)

\displaystyle\cdots{}_{d_{N-1}}\leftrightarrows^{b_{N-1}}\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s_{N}}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{N})_{t=0}{}_{d_{N}}\leftrightarrows^{b_{N}}\boldsymbol{\mathpzc{E}}^{s_{N+1}}_{\rm gr}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{N+1})_{t=0}\to 0,

where
s_{1},\ldots,s_{N},s_{N+1}
are such that
s_{j}-s_{j+1}=m_{j}
for any
j
.

We note that the symbol complex (
59
) is a well defined
C^{*}(T_{H}X)
-Fredholm sequence by Lemma
0.20.2
and the properties of
B_{1},\ldots,B_{N}
given in Theorem
0.20.6
.

0.25.2.
The
K
-homology class of a Rockland sequence

We now turn to the convoluted business of associating a
K
-homology class with a graded Rockland sequence. For a graded vector bundle
\boldsymbol{E}
, we write
\boldsymbol{E}_{{\rm tr}}
for the associated trivially graded vector bundle. We fix families of order reducing operators
\mathfrak{D}_{\boldsymbol{E},s}\in\Psi^{s}_{H,{\rm gr}}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{{\rm tr}},\boldsymbol{E})
- invertible operators constructed as direct sums of suitable powers of operators as in Proposition
0.19.15
.

Proposition 0.25.6
.

Consider a graded Rockland sequence

\displaystyle 0\to C^{\infty}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1})\xrightarrow{D_{1}}

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{2})\xrightarrow{D_{2}}\cdots

(60)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{D_{N-1}}C^{\infty}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{N})\xrightarrow{D_{N}}C^{\infty}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{N+1})\to 0,

of order
\boldsymbol{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{N})
. Take
s_{1},\ldots,s_{N},s_{N+1}
such that
s_{j}-s_{j+1}=m_{j}
for any
j
.

Let
\boldsymbol{E}_{j,{\rm tr}}
denote
\boldsymbol{E}_{j}
equipped with the trivial grading. Define the operators

D_{j,{\rm tr}}:=\mathfrak{D}_{\boldsymbol{E}_{j+1},s_{j+1}}D_{j}\mathfrak{D}_{\boldsymbol{E}_{j},-s_{j}}\in\Psi^{0}_{H}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{j,{\rm tr}},\boldsymbol{E}_{j+1,{\rm tr}}).

Then the sequence

\displaystyle 0\to C^{\infty}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{1,{\rm tr}})\xrightarrow{D_{1,{\rm tr}}}

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{2,{\rm tr}})\xrightarrow{D_{2,{\rm tr}}}\cdots

(61)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{D_{N-1,{\rm tr}}}C^{\infty}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{N,{\rm tr}})\xrightarrow{D_{N,{\rm tr}}}C^{\infty}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{N+1,{\rm tr}})\to 0,

is a Rockland sequence.

Denoting the graded Rockland sequence (
60
) by
D_{\bullet}
, we call the Rockland sequence (
61
) the order reduction of
D_{\bullet}
and we denote this by
D^{\rm tr}_{\bullet}
. We note the following proposition which is immediate from the definition of
D^{\rm tr}_{\bullet}
.

Proposition 0.25.7
.

Consider a graded Rockland sequence
D_{\bullet}
as in (
60
) and its order reduction
D^{\rm tr}_{\bullet}
as in (
61
). Then it holds that

[\sigma_{H}(D_{\bullet})]=[\sigma_{H}(D_{\bullet}^{\rm tr})]\in K_{0}(C^{*}(T_{H}X)).

Definition 0.25.8
.

Consider a graded Rockland sequence
D_{\bullet}
as in (
60
) on a compact Carnot manifold
X
. Take operators
B_{j,{\rm tr}}\in\Psi_{H}^{0}(X;\boldsymbol{E}_{j+1,{\rm tr}},\boldsymbol{E}_{\rm tr})
as in Theorem
0.20.6
defined from the order reduction
D^{\rm tr}_{\bullet}
.

We define the
K
-homology class
[D_{\bullet}]\in K_{0}^{\rm an}(X)
as the class of the even
K
-cycle

\left(L^{2}(X;\oplus_{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{k,{\rm tr}}),F\right),

where
L^{2}(X;\oplus_{k}\boldsymbol{E}_{k,{\rm tr}})
is graded by the parity of
k
and
F
is obtained from polar decomposition of

F_{0}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&F_{0,+}\\
F_{0,+}^{*}&0\end{pmatrix},\quad\mbox{for}\quad F_{0,+}:=\sum_{k}D_{2k+1,{\rm tr}}+B_{2k,{\rm tr}}.

In the case of the BGG-sequence for the complex semi-simple Lie group
SL(3,\mathbb{C})
, an associated
SL(3,\mathbb{C})
-equivariant
K
-homology class was constructed by Yuncken
[
153
]
. If the class constructed
[
153
]
can be reconciled with the construction above, it would provide a general method to construct
\gamma
-elements for complex semi-simple Lie groups.

Theorem 0.25.9
.

Consider a graded Rockland sequence
D_{\bullet}
on a compact Carnot manifold
X
. Then it holds that

[D_{\bullet}]=\mathsf{PD}^{\rm an}_{H}([\sigma_{H}(D_{\bullet})])\in K_{0}^{\rm an}(X).

In particular, the
K
-homology class
[D_{\bullet}]
only depends on the Rockland sequence and not on auxiliary choices.

Proof.

By Proposition
0.25.7
and the definition of
D_{\bullet}
, we can assume that
D_{\bullet}=D_{\bullet}^{\rm tr}
. The theorem now follows from Proposition
0.23.10
.
∎

Remark 0.25.10
.

The abstract methods of Section
0.24
applies to graded Rockland sequences due to Theorem
0.25.9
. We nevertheless believe it to be of interest to further clarify the analytic and geometric structure of the
K
-homology class
[D_{\bullet}]\in K_{0}^{\rm an}(X)
and the symbol class
[\sigma_{H}(D_{\bullet})]\in K_{0}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
.

In principle, the localization and approximation methods of Section
0.21
apply to the symbol class
[\sigma_{H}(D_{\bullet})]\in K_{0}(C^{*}(T_{H}X))
for an
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular Carnot manifold. However, we believe it would be interesting if there was a more direct method of localization for the symbol class
[\sigma_{H}(D_{\bullet})]
to an
I_{X}
–Fredholm complex in case of an
\boldsymbol{F}
-regular manifold, similar to Lemma
0.21.6
but for symbols of Rockland sequences. The concrete BGG-complexes arising on
X=G/B
, where
B
is the Borel subgroup, as in the original work of Bernstein-Gelfand-Gelfand
[
19
]
, or the examples computed in
[
45
,
46
]
can prove to be interesting testing grounds.
